## visr-v0.6.1 (2026-10-27)

### Summary

- The loading bar now moves at an even pace through the whole wait, so the loading screen's chant builds up from its opening instead of skipping ahead.
- The loading screen's tone keeps sounding until the level appears, instead of dropping out just before it.
- Benchmark recordings now get several minutes to download instead of 30 seconds, and the Benchmark menu shows "Downloading benchmark recordings…" with a percentage while they download. Slower connections should no longer show "Benchmark recordings are unavailable".

iCloud Saves starts off. To try it, sign in to iCloud on each device, then turn on iCloud Saves under Network Setup in the game's profile settings (on iPhone, iPad and Apple Vision Pro also on VISR's page in the Settings app). It takes effect at the next launch.

VISR automatically sends missing shader descriptions and device model information to improve loading. Reports exclude gameplay and personal file names. Apple's upload service attaches an account-specific identifier. Shader diagnostics are required in builds with shader uploads enabled.

VISR automatically sends game performance data to improve the game. Reports exclude names, chat, and personal file names. Apple's upload service attaches an account-specific identifier. Performance diagnostics are required in builds with performance reports enabled. Sharing this data is part of testing; leaving the beta stops it.

### Full changelog

### feat

- **benchmarks**: link each build in the page's lists and captions to its release — the results sheet gains a `release_url` column (GitHub release pages by tag, the hosted page for the OpenCE web build, never a download), `sheet_export.release_url` fills it from `RELEASE_PAGES` for VISR, OpenCE-macOS, OpenCE web, CoreCE, Chupathingy, Halo OG, master-chef, Beeman, ChupathingyCE iOS, HaloPad and SnailBBQ, `charts.py` and `benchmarks.js` carry it as each entry's `url`, and the Builds checkbox labels, the captions and the not-measured lists link the build name
- **profiles**: 120 frames a second in campaign on the iPhone 17 and iPhone Air too — `iphone-15-pro.toml` (now "iPhone 15 Pro, 16 Pro, 17 and Air") lists `iPhone18,3` and `iPhone18,4`, which had fallen past it to the 16e's 60 because their 120 Hz panels leave the A17 Pro source's envelope; Steve chose 120 for them on 2026-10-27, assumed until benchmarked, and variable games keep 60. `profile_probe.c` checks the iPhone 17. Profile set revision 11.
- **profiles**: Pro iPhones target 120 frames a second in campaign — `iphone-17-pro.toml` (and the 17 Pro Max through `and_newer`) and `iphone-15-pro.toml` (which the iPhone 16 Pro and Pro Max inherit through the hardware catalog) set `display.frame_rate = "120"` for the predictable workload and keep 60 in `[variable.display]` for multiplayer, co-op and enemy multipliers; the 120 is assumed, Steve's decision on 2026-10-27, to be validated with benchmarks, and the earlier sweep measurements move to the variable value's evidence. Profile set revision 9.
- **benchmark**: trim a catalog's recordings to the ticks its windows replay — `tools/trim_recordings.py` drops the `.actions`, `.seeds` and `.input` tail past each case's anchor plus `lead` plus `window` (with a 20 s and 10% margin) and leaves the catalog and routes byte-identical, and `tools/replay_trim_check.py` replays full and trimmed cases through the benchmark suite on the Mac runner and compares their routes
- **profiles**: 32-player capacity on the iPhone 15 Pro and 16e, and 120 frames a second on the M1 iPad Pro — `iphone-15-pro.toml` and `iphone-16e.toml` set `network.player_capacity = 32` to match the iPhone 17 Pro (assumed: the same GPU class, not measured separately); `ipad-pro-m1.toml`, which newer M-series iPads inherit, sets `display.frame_rate = "120"` from the MetalFX runs that held 99.90% (a10) and 99.75% (d40) of frames on time at 120, and is marked provisional until 120 is checked at 1.5x enemies. Profile set revision 8.

### fix

- **profiles**: match the iPhone 16 Pro and Pro Max by the Pro profile so they get its 120 in campaign — they inherited `iphone-15-pro.toml` through the hardware catalog, whose A17 Pro envelope stops at 60 Hz, so with the profile at 120 their 120 Hz panels put them outside it and they fell to the 16e's 60; `iphone-15-pro.toml` (now "iPhone 15 Pro and 16 Pro") lists `iPhone17,1` and `iPhone17,2`, and `profile_probe.c` checks the 16 Pro. The iPhone 17 and Air stay on the 16e's profile at 60. Profile set revision 10.
- **data-packs**: give the benchmark recordings download minutes and show its progress — `host_data_pack.m` cut every feed off at `timeoutIntervalForResource = 30`, so the 16 MB recordings catalog failed below about 4.4 Mbit/s and each retry restarted from zero; `recordings` feeds now get 900 s, `HostDataPackStore` reports `downloadReceived`/`downloadExpected`, and the Benchmark menu shows "Downloading benchmark recordings…" with a percentage during every attempt instead of "unavailable" while the retry runs
- **loading**: keep the loading screen's tone sounding until the level's first frame stops it — it faded out for most of a second just before the level appeared
- **loading**: trust the textures' measured rate gradually — the first textures made are small and fast, which dropped the remaining-time estimate and raced the bar to a third in the first 1.3 s; the estimate blends from the 8 s guess to the measured rate over 5 s
- **loading**: the loading bar spends its time evenly — it is COMPILE_END times elapsed over elapsed plus what is left, estimated from the textures' units at the rate so far, the level's read and the compiles, so a wait raced to half the bar at its start and sat between 60 and 90 percent while the chant's layers (which come in at 0.4, 0.5 and 0.55) were skipped; the shares given to phases are gone

### build

- **benchmark**: bind the 20-case tester catalog to the 0.6.1 guest at its own feed — `docs/site/packs/tester/recordings-c7fe63a3433bb5ef215fbd498679caf5688f56663b65879d496d39981f482b91.json` is the 0.6.0 catalog with its trimmed recordings, signed as sequence 1793123149 with compatibility `benchmark-catalog-1:c7fe63a3433bb5ef215fbd498679caf5688f56663b65879d496d39981f482b91`, which 0.6.1 builds read; the older feeds stay byte-identical, and `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` and `tools/test_pages_main_assets.py` name the new envelope's SHA-256
- **profiles**: re-sign the generation 2 tester shards at profile revision 11 and add the iPhone 17 and iPhone Air — every `docs/site/packs/tester/device-profiles-2/` shard is sequence 1793123003 from the revision 11 `profiles/` tree, which matches the iPhone 16 Pro, 17 and Air by `iphone-15-pro.toml` (120 frames a second in campaign, 60 in variable games); `ios/iPhone18,3/_` and `ios/iPhone18,4/_` are new. `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` names the 53 SHA-256s and `tools/test_pages_main_assets.py` counts them.
- **profiles**: re-sign the generation 2 tester shards at profile revision 9 and add the iPhone 16 Pro and Pro Max — every `docs/site/packs/tester/device-profiles-2/` shard is sequence 1793118609 from the revision 9 `profiles/` tree, so 0.6.0 builds on the iPhone 15 Pro, 16 Pro and 17 Pro families take 120 frames a second in campaign; `ios/iPhone17,1/_` and `ios/iPhone17,2/_` are new (they inherit `iphone-15-pro.toml`). `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` names the 51 SHA-256s and `tools/test_pages_main_assets.py` counts them.
- **benchmark**: republish the 0.6.0 tester catalog with trimmed recordings — `docs/site/packs/tester/recordings-28b14010a886a159afff1bec13ec7f516b5235f270934906f15ff59ff10b0fd1.json` is the same 20 cases with each recording cut after its window, signed as sequence 1793115186 with the same compatibility string, 62.76 MB down to 49.31 MB (15.9 MB down to 12.4 MB gzipped); `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` and `tools/test_pages_main_assets.py` name the new envelope's SHA-256
- **profiles**: publish revision 8 shards for the M-series iPads and the iPhone 17 Pro Max — the hardware catalog's `ipad-air-m1`, `ipad-m2`, `ipad-m3`, `ipad-m4` and `ipad-m5` models (`iPad13,16`, `iPad13,17`, `iPad14,3` to `iPad14,6`, `iPad14,8` to `iPad14,11`, `iPad15,3` to `iPad15,6`, `iPad16,3` to `iPad16,6`, `iPad16,8` to `iPad16,11`, `iPad17,1` to `iPad17,4`) inherit `ipad-pro-m1.toml`, and `iphone-17-pro-max` (`iPhone18,2`) inherits `iphone-17-pro.toml`, but had no shard and so kept their bundled revision 7 profiles; each shard is signed from the revision 8 `profiles/` tree, and every `docs/site/packs/tester/device-profiles-2/` shard is re-signed as sequence 1793116245. `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` names the 49 SHA-256s and `tools/test_pages_main_assets.py` counts them.
- **profiles**: re-sign the generation 2 tester shards at profile revision 8 and add the iPhone 15 Pro and Pro Max — every `docs/site/packs/tester/device-profiles-2/` shard is sequence 1793114296 from the revision 8 `profiles/` tree, so 0.6.0 builds take the 32-player capacity on the iPhone 15 Pro and 16e and 120 frames a second on the M1 iPad Pro over their bundled revision 7; `ios/iPhone16,1/_` and `ios/iPhone16,2/_` are new, since a model without a shard keeps its bundled profiles. `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` names the 22 SHA-256s and `tools/test_pages_main_assets.py` counts them; generation 1 `profiles/` is unchanged.
- **release**: name 0.6.0 for visionOS in the latest-release record — `docs/site/packs/tester/latest.json` is re-signed as sequence 1793108456 with visionOS added beside iOS, tvOS and macOS, so a Vision Pro build whose benchmark catalog is retired names the version to install; `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` names its SHA-256
- **release**: publish the signed latest-release record for 0.6.0 — `docs/site/packs/tester/latest.json` is sequence 1793108184 under `latest-release-1` and names 0.6.0 for iOS, tvOS and macOS, so a build whose benchmark catalog is no longer published can name the version to install; `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` names its SHA-256

### docs

- **benchmarks**: add the VISR 0.6.0 Mac, Apple TV HD and Vision Pro rows, chart the Apple TV HD at its profile's frame cap, and list Vision Pro frame time and on-time share — the snapshot gains 14 VISR 0.6.0 rows from the results sheet, `REFERENCE_BOXES` names the Apple TV HD, the Apple TV caption says only VISR has Apple TV builds, the Vision Pro caption says VISR replays a recorded route while master-chef runs with no input, `query.details` puts 95th-percentile frame time and on-time share under a chart, and a build without a 1% low (master-chef) draws a zero-length bar so VISR keeps its 1% low series in `charts.py` and `benchmarks.js`; `method_group` reads Metal System Trace and benchmark replay methods as such
- **changelog**: fold the Unreleased entries into 0.6.0 — commitizen replaced the `## Unreleased` section with the generated `visr-v0.6.0` section and dropped its 92 entries, all of which are in the tag; the 0.6.0 section now lists both, and its Summary is the What to Test text the builds carry

### test

- **profile**: expect 120 frames a second from the Pro iPhone and M1 iPad Pro profiles — `profile_probe.c` still asserted 60 for `iPhone18,1`, `iPad13,9` and `iPhone16,2`, which revisions 8 and 9 raised to 120 in a predictable workload, and the iPhone 17 now falls past the A17 Pro source, whose 60 Hz envelope its 120 Hz target leaves, to the 16e's profile at 60
- **loading**: the progress probe expects the bar to be paced by time, within the compiles' range
- **loading**: the progress probe's stub for the copy's progress is unused now that the bar is paced by time

## visr-v0.6.0 (2026-10-27)

### Summary

- Textures can now be upscaled on your device. After importing the game, the import screen shows one "Upscaling textures" bar; the main menu's art, including the HALO logo, is upscaled first, and then "Start playing" appears so you can skip the rest. Games imported before this version show that bar once at the next launch. Upscaling continues in the background in menus and loading screens and pauses during gameplay. On Apple TV this is on for Apple TV 4K (3rd generation) and newer.
- When a level's textures are not ready yet, its loading screen shows the upscaling progress: the game's blue loading picture with a moving bar, a spinner and "Press A to Skip" in the HUD's blue. Games imported before VISR took that picture from the disc show a stand-in; importing the game again restores the original. Check that each level plays either fully upscaled or fully original.
- On-screen touch controls no longer lag while textures upscale in the background.
- Dynamic resolution is on for more devices: iPhone 12 Pro, iPhone 15 Pro, iPhone 16e, iPhone 17 Pro, iPad Air (4th generation), iPad Pro 12.9-inch (2nd generation), iPad Pro (M1) and newer, Apple TV 4K (1st generation) and Apple TV HD, and it now also works on external displays. Watch for smoother frame pacing in heavy fights and busy multiplayer games.
- iPad Pro (M1) and newer M-series iPads now upscale the scene with MetalFX. Look for sharper edges than before.
- External displays: when a display is connected at the main menu, the game moves to it and fills it.
- Smaller CPU costs per frame, and shadows are cheaper on devices that struggled with them. Report any change in frame rate or in how shadows look.
- On Apple Vision Pro, the render scale setting now takes effect.
- VISR no longer needs the extended virtual addressing entitlement, so devices that could not reserve the game's memory, such as the Apple TV HD and the iPad Pro 12.9-inch (2nd generation), should now start. Report any launch that fails.
- Fixed: the first multiplayer map load could show a white screen, and joining a network game or changing maps could show a frame of only sky.
- Fixed: the loading screen's chant now plays continuously.
- Fixed: pause menus no longer move your player, and on touch controls buttons no longer act on the game while a menu is open. In split screen, one player's pause menu no longer freezes the others.
- The server browser marks a game BIG only when it is at least half as full as your device holds, and no longer shows "(0 dedicated)".
- When a later release retires this version's benchmark, the launch menu says so and names the version to update to.

iCloud Saves starts off. To try it, sign in to iCloud on each device, then turn on iCloud Saves under Network Setup in the game's profile settings (on iPhone, iPad and Apple Vision Pro also on VISR's page in the Settings app). It takes effect at the next launch.

VISR automatically sends missing shader descriptions and device model information to improve loading. Reports exclude gameplay and personal file names. Apple's upload service attaches an account-specific identifier. Shader diagnostics are required in builds with shader uploads enabled.

VISR automatically sends game performance data to improve the game. Reports exclude names, chat, and personal file names. Apple's upload service attaches an account-specific identifier. Performance diagnostics are required in builds with performance reports enabled. Sharing this data is part of testing; leaving the beta stops it.

### Full changelog

### feat

- **upscale**: an already-imported game shows the import screen's upscaling bar once — `host_texture_upscale_first_run_phase` answers once per install where upscaling is on (profile and switch), any import phase counts as shown, and tvOS gets the same progress-only screen; `texture_upscale_first_run_phase` is probed
- **profiles**: turn dynamic resolution on in the `iphone-15-pro` and `iphone-16e` profiles — both render at the display's native height, where a native-ceiling pool costs nothing while the GPU keeps up. Presented runs on an iPhone 17 Pro at native 2622x1206 stayed at the ceiling for every frame in `a10-heroic` and `b30`, and in Blood Gulch with 32 players cut late frames from 10 to 0 using 1117 to 1206 lines. The campaign window is 1080 to native; `[variable.display]` opens the floor to 540.
- **ios**: tell a build whose benchmark catalog is no longer published which version to install — the launch menu reads the signed `latest.json` beside the recording feed through a new `latest` kind in `HostDataPackStore`, and when the recording feed answers 404, no catalog is cached and the record names a newer version for this platform, it shows "Benchmarking is no longer supported on this version. Update to VISR X.Y.Z." ahead of the profile-server text; `host_data_updates_latest_newer` is the hook for a future update note, and `data_updates_probe.m` covers version comparison, record shape, the 404 latch and a signed record staged and read back
- **release**: a signed latest-release record kind — `tools/data_pack.py --kind latest` signs `latest-release.json` (platform to version, two levels deep) under `latest-release-1`, `tools/latest_release.py` merges a published release into the previous record without lowering a version, `tools/pages_asset_overlay.py` accepts `docs/site/packs/tester/latest.json` as an unpinned signed feed, and the publish step of `tools/release.py` prints the command to prepare the record
- **upscale**: log when the import offers Start playing — `host_texture_upscale_prepare_import` says when the main menu's textures are ready
- **upscale**: upscale the main menu's art first and hold the import's Skip until it is done — `texture_policy.c` treated every `ui\\` bitmap (the big HALO logo `ui\\shell\\main_menu\\halo_logo` among them) as HUD art and left it original; the `ui\\shell\\` bitmaps are now in scope as graphics, `ui\\hud\\` stays original, `texture_upscale_map_rank` and `texture_upscale_import_skippable` in `texture_upscale_schedule.h` put the menu map first and withhold "Start playing" until its textures are ready, and the iOS and tvOS import screens show the menu phase's progress without the button
- **benchmarks**: VISR blue and gold bars and even chart margins — the average and 1% low bars use `--chart-avg` and `--chart-low` (the icon's `#1fa9d4` and `#ffc24d`, deepened to keep 3:1 contrast on light backgrounds), the legend uses matching swatches, and `finishChart` in `benchmarks.js` fits each chart's view box to its content plus an even margin so the last tick label is no longer clipped; the pre-rendered fallback SVG goes through the same step
- **shaders**: add 233 list entries from six stock-map `ShaderObservation` uploads — `a10` 3, `b30` 126 (two uploads, merged without duplicates), `damnation` 59 and `ratrace` 45, with their dependency records, from ordinary 0.5.0, 0.5.1 and 0.5.2 play; each upload was validated with `tools/shader_observations.py` (stock fingerprint, released guest, descriptor admission and dependency checks) and merged with `tools/shader_lists.py merge`, which also rewrites the `damnation` and `a10` pipeline lines that predate the separate alpha factors into their current form
- **release**: attach the ad hoc signed Mac app to every GitHub release — `tools/release_unsigned.py` builds `VISR-macOS-unsigned.zip` with its `.sha256` from the release tag (`--native-mac --sandbox on`, no team, no provisioning profile, only the App Sandbox entitlements, so no iCloud features), checks that every bundle and Mach-O file reports `Signature=adhoc` with no team, no entitlement that needs a profile, no team ID string and the release guest, and `tools/release.py publish` runs the same check with the team ID before the first signed build. `--only macos` builds and checks just the Mac app to add it to an earlier release.
- **network**: join version 39 hosts — retain the 38 codec and mark dedicated games in the shared browser
- **ios**: let the guest arena start at any 16 KiB boundary — `tools/ios_asm_convert.py` now forms each guest address as `add x15, x27, wN, uxtw` instead of `mov` plus `orr`, turns `adrp` results and values taken from sp or the frame pointer x29 into guest pointers by subtracting x27, leaves x29-based accesses native, re-adds x27 when a guest pointer becomes sp, and rejects any other use of sp or x29; `host_pointer` adds, `guest_pointer` subtracts, and the memory-watch fault handler converts the fault address the same way. `arena_reserve` in `host_memory.c` still takes 4 GiB at a multiple of 4 GiB first, then 4 GiB anywhere, then the shorter range anywhere, so a device that refuses only the aligned range (an Apple TV HD, an A10X iPad Pro) still gets an arena. `HALO_ARENA_UNALIGNED=1` places it 0x7FFFC000 past a multiple of 4 GiB for testing; `memory_probe unaligned` and `unaligned-reduced`, the ILP32 probe at both placements and `tools/test_ios_asm_convert.py` cover it. The guest ABI version goes to 3. The lifted guest is 4.9% fewer instructions.
- **ios**: reserve the guest arena before `main` — a constructor in `host_loader.c` calls the new `host_memory_reserve_early`, which takes the 4 GiB range at a multiple of 4 GiB, or the shorter one, before UIKit, Metal, SDL or any thread of the game maps memory, so a device near its address-space limit is not refused because of where something else landed first; `host_memory_initialize` adopts that reservation, retries once if it was refused, and logs which it used (`reserved at launch` or `now`). `memory_probe early` checks the adopted path.
- **profile**: time held immediate-mode draws on their own — `halo_immediate_flush` is summed a frame as `d3d8_immediate_flush`, since the held draw goes out inside whichever call needs it next (a constant, a clear, a target) and counted in that call's row; `docs/profiling.md` lists it and the object update sections.
- **profile**: time the parts of each object's update — the profiling build's recording sums `object_update.type`, `.damage`, `.export_functions`, `.node_matrices`, `.functions`, `.change_colors`, `.lights` and `.postprocess` a tick, and `objects_update` gains scopes for its activation scan, its created and deleted objects and its garbage collection, so the part of `objects_update` that the per-type updates do not cover is attributed. The sections leave no tokens in a normal build.
- **tools**: print the reason a crash report's registers carry — `guest_symbolicate.py` decodes `host_crash.c`'s packed x0-x28 from an `.ips` thread state or a `.crash` report's text
- **mac_run**: copy the crash reports of a native run that did not exit cleanly — into the result folder's `crash-reports`, so a run that crashed keeps its report with its logs
- **tools**: add `guest_symbolicate.py` to name a crash report's guest frames — it reads an `.ips` report or `ios-runtime.log` and the build's `guest-symbols.txt` (or the app's own `__halosyms`), since the arena's alias of the guest code is outside every image the report lists
- **ios**: end fatal errors in a native crash instead of the blue halt screen — `halt_and_catch_fire` hands the game's last error to `host_guest_halt`, and host bugs call `host_crash`, which log the guest's named frames to `ios-runtime.log` and raise an uncaught `NSException` whose reason Apple's crash reports keep; faults outside the memory watch's pages are logged with their registers and summarized in `__crash_info`; `debug.halt_screen` keeps the halt screen and `debug.crash_test` fails on purpose to check a report
- **profiles**: dynamic resolution between 540 and 480 lines on the Apple TV HD — `display.dynamic_resolution` and `display.dynamic_resolution_floor = 480` in `apple-tv-hd.toml`; presented at 30 frames a second it took d40 from 89.3% to 92.5% of frames on time and a10 from 93.0% to 95.0%, and a 720 ceiling did no better
- **pacing**: halve the frame rate target in Low Power Mode — `debug.low_power_frame_rate`
- **profiles**: give variable workloads their own device-profile values — `[variable.display]` in a profile lies over its own values while a map is a variable workload (multiplayer maps, online co-op, LAN co-op beyond four players or with extra enemies, split screen, any enemy multiplier above 1x) and the scene classes lie over either; single-player campaign and LAN co-op of four or fewer at standard settings stay predictable
- **benchmark**: pipelined offscreen runs and a virtual display for paced ones, with each frame's waits in the report
- **benchmark**: `debug.frame_cpu_probe` also lists the app's busiest threads in the runtime log every 600 frames
- **benchmark**: `debug.frame_cpu_probe` adds per-frame thread CPU, process CPU and a clock probe to reports
- **metal**: time dynamic resolution's frames by command buffer where stage sampling is missing — on a GPU without stage-boundary counter sampling (an A9X iPad, likely the Apple TV HD) the planner now takes S as the union of the scene's command buffers' GPU spans, which matched stage-sampled S in every 600-frame window on an M1 iPad Pro, and U+H as the median of the rest's spans over the last 32 frames, since a present buffer sometimes holds a drawable wait; such frames are marked coarse (`available` bit 16), and dynamic resolution is off only where the renderer has no Metal timing at all. `perf_metal_probe.m` covers the union, the median against a drawable wait and a refused scene
- **shadows**: blur shadow maps above 128 texels over the game's footprint — the game blurs a shadow with four taps half a 128-texel map's texel apart diagonally, in texture coordinates, so at `display.shadow_map_size` 512 the taps fall 4 texels apart on a sharper silhouette and each shows as its own shifted copy. Shadow maps above 128 texels now first average a box of about the scale's texels a side (a pre-pass into pre-pass pages laid out as the atlas's, one render pass a page) and then take the game's taps from that, so the blur covers the area it did at 128: an 8-texel box at 512. At 256 and 512 texels the box is exact; other sizes get four taps spread evenly over it. The pre-pass pages are bounded (64 MB) and reused for runs of atlas pages, and shadows past the atlas get the same two blurs into the game's own pair of targets. 128-texel shadows keep the game's blur byte for byte; `debug.shadow_blur` "xbox" brings back the game's taps at larger sizes for comparison.
- **gpu**: copy rectangles of a texture into several textures at once — `gpu_texture_copy_rects` copies each rectangle of level 0 of a source into the top left of its own destination; Metal makes the whole list one blit encoder, GL uses `glCopyImageSubData` (or a framebuffer blit on ES without copy-image). Render validation checks bounds and formats, and render captures record it as `GPU_CAPTURE_TEXTURE_COPY_RECTS`.
- **metal**: let dynamic resolution run at a native ceiling without the HUD layer — with `render_height = 0` the pool is the display's own size, so while it renders the ceiling the scene and HUD share its target and it is presented itself, as without dynamic resolution; the display-sized HUD layer comes on only while the scene renders below. `gpu_metal_present` measures that presented pool as the frame's one scene (`MetalTexture.pooled`), which had left the planner idle at native ceilings, and split screen is refused in the guest, since the planner measures one view. On the Mac runner the pool held at a native ceiling matches dynamic resolution off exactly with bilinear and MetalFX, with the same passes, traffic and live targets; pinned at 1080 it matches a plain 1080-line scene (MetalFX exactly; bilinear within 1 level on 0.0003% of pixels), and MetalFX's scaler from the native allocation to the same output size builds and runs
- **profiles**: turn dynamic resolution on for the A10X, A14, M1 and A19 Pro profiles — `display.dynamic_resolution = true` in the Apple TV 4K (1st generation), iPad Pro 12.9-inch (2nd generation), iPad Pro M1, iPad Air 4, iPhone 12 Pro and iPhone 17 Pro profiles, each with its evidence: on the A10X iPad the Maw fight at 1080 lines and 60 frames a second went from 76.1% and 92.5% of frames on time to 99.8% and 99.9%, the M1 and the iPhone 12 Pro held the same with it on as off at their ceilings, and the A14 reported the stage timing the planner needs. Ceilings stay each profile's `render_height`; the floor stays 540 lines or half the ceiling. Profile set revision 2
- **release**: attach the unsigned iPhone and iPad, Apple TV and Apple Vision Pro apps to every GitHub release — a new `unsigned` job builds `VISR-iOS-unsigned.ipa`, `VISR-tvOS-unsigned.ipa` and `VISR-visionOS-unsigned.ipa` with their `.sha256` files from the release tag with `tools/release_unsigned.py`, using the manifest's version, build number, epochs, feeds and optimization settings without the iCloud features, and `tools/release.py publish` checks them again with the team ID before the first signed build and attaches them to the draft just before publication
- **tools**: sign and verify `device-profiles-2` shards — `data_pack.py` adds the revision to each shard and refuses one any released app of its generation would refuse, built from that tag's own validator; Pages admits `device-profiles-2/` beside the frozen `profiles/` and refuses a lower revision; release validation takes the profile domain from the feed directory and requires this generation's for new releases; `ios_build.py` and `profile_pr.py` default to it
- **ios**: read device profiles from the `device-profiles-2` feed and never let older ones mask the bundled set — a cached `device-profiles-1` shard lives under another root and is ignored, and a shard whose revision is below the bundled one (or equal with other sources) is refused when served and when cached; `HostDataPackStore.supersededCheck` recognizes it before the sequence check, and the launch menu then benchmarks with the bundled profiles as it does after a 404. `profile_feed_probe.m` shows the iPhone 17 Pro keeping `network.player_capacity` and the Apple TV 4K (3rd generation) keeping `display.upscaled_textures` with their live v1 shards stored
- **profiles**: stamp the profile set with a revision — `profiles/hardware/revision.toml` holds a revision and the SHA-256 of every profile and the hardware catalog, `tools/profile_revision.py` checks it and `--bump` advances it, the app bundles it, and `host_profile_validate_feed_folder` refuses it in a `device-profiles-1` shard (as 0.2.2 to 0.5.1 would) and requires it in a `device-profiles-2` one
- **textures**: make upscaled textures on the Apple TV 4K (3rd generation) and newer — `apple-tv-4k-3rd.toml` sets `display.upscaled_textures = true` (Steve's decision), its `and_newer` rule carries that to later Apple TVs, and the HD, the 4K first and second generation keep the Apple TV class's false; tvOS builds now bundle the model. The A9X and A10X iPad profiles drop the false they had: the A12 iPad Air 3 inherits the A10X profile, and the Neural Engine check already keeps the A9X and A10X off. `profile_probe.c` checks each case.
- **ios**: report dynamic resolution's lines as a distribution — a benchmark run's `render_size` is its ceiling again, not the last frame's lines, and a run with dynamic resolution on adds `dynamic_resolution`: the ceiling, the fewest, median and most lines and each level's frames and seconds over the window (`host_perf_hooks_dynamic_lines`); per-game reports list `display.dynamic_resolution` and its floor with the other graphics settings
- **metal**: dynamic resolution changes the scene's render resolution behind `display.dynamic_resolution`
- **textures**: keep "auto" upscaling out of deterministic runs too — a fixed timestep or `debug.screenshot_every` run (check-metal and the other image comparisons) does not make upscaled textures unless the generator is forced.
- **textures**: make upscaled textures on devices whose profile allows them — `debug.texture_upscale_generate` defaults to "auto", so a device upscales when its profile's `display.upscaled_textures` and the player's switch are on and Core ML has a Neural Engine; every Apple TV stays off (its class profile), the iPad Pro 12.9-inch first and second generation (A9X, A10X) are set off with their evidence, and A9 to A11 devices without a profile are off through the Neural Engine check. "auto" also stays out of measured runs (a benchmark suite, a benchmark replay, any input replay), where the menus between takes would heat the device; "force" still runs there.
- **textures**: say what a stop for gameplay interrupts — the "paused for gameplay" line names the texture in hand and its stage (its predictions, or refinement, measures and compression), so a log shows whether the stop landed mid-texture and how long the handoff took.
- **textures**: carry the import's upscaling phase on in the background with iOS 26's continued processing — when the "Upscaling textures" phase is on screen, a `BGContinuedProcessingTaskRequest` (identifier under `BGTaskSchedulerPermittedIdentifiers`' `.texture-upscale.*` wildcard, registered just before it is submitted, submission strategy fail) lets the work continue if the player switches away, with the system's progress showing the whole game's work units; expiration stops the generator at the texture in hand and completes the task unsuccessfully, the app's return resumes it, and the phase's end completes it. The generator uses no GPU, so the request asks for no extra resource; a queue that stops while the app is away (an error, or the Neural Engine refused in the background) ends the task. iPhone and iPad on iOS 26 and later only, on devices whose profile upscales; nothing on tvOS, visionOS or Mac, where the API is unavailable. The rules are a state machine in `texture_upscale_continued.h` with a probe, and `HALO_TEXTURE_UPSCALE_IMPORT_PHASE` (development only) shows the import's upscaling phase over game data already imported, so a device test can reach it.
- **debug**: `debug.init_after` holds `init.txt`'s commands for that many seconds after start-up — a test can let the game sit at the main menu and then start a level (`map_name a10`) in the same launch, which the on-device upscaler's proof needs to have a texture in hand when gameplay begins; `console_startup_update` runs them from `main_loop`, and 0 (the default) runs them at once as before.
- **textures**: schedule on-device upscaling — the device profile's `display.upscaled_textures` gates everything before any queue or directory exists, and the player's switch can only turn it off; the import screen gets an "Upscaling textures" phase on the progress bar with "Start playing" as its skip; the work then continues in menus, while a level loads and after a pause has lasted two seconds, and stops before the first gameplay frame (Core ML between predictions, the BC7 and ASTC encoders between block rows and tiles); a campaign level's loading screen waits for that level's missing textures ("Upscaling assets", A skips), and a level plays upscaled only when every texture its policy upscales has a current entry, otherwise entirely original. `debug.texture_upscale_generate` becomes "off" (default), "auto" or "force". The generator is released whenever the queue stops, so its model and working memory are not held across gameplay. The Settings pane shows how many textures are ready.

### fix

- **guest**: keep string data intact when converting the guest's assembly — the loading screen's tone looped half a chant and then a DC step
- **upscale**: the loading screen draws without the player's picture and its bar moves through the whole wait — game data imported before the importers took `loading.tga` from the disc left the picture black, so `progress_bar_load_loading_texture` now makes dark grain with LOADING across it when the file is missing, and the upscale wait spans 0.12 to 0.8 of the bar instead of a quarter of it
- **upscale**: the loading note's spinner is a drawn ring of dots, its text uses the HUD message color, and the bar stays lit while the level's textures are made — the picture and light band follow `sin(pi t)` and were black near 0, so the bar stands from 0.25 to 0.5 for the wait (also while the worker lists the textures) and the read and compiles fill the rest
- **upscale**: the loading screen's upscaling note uses a drawn spinner and the HUD's color, and the screen stays lit — the bar stands from 0.25 to 0.5 while the level's textures are made (the picture and light band follow `sin(pi t)` and were black near 0), then the read and compiles fill the rest
- **network**: keep the loading screen's last picture until a network map's local player has a view — a client's clock takes the host's time from its first update, so joining a game already going (a host that starts without waiting, a rejoin, the server's next map) starts it far past the third tick that `main_loop`'s guard waits for, while the host spawns the player some ticks later; until then the player had no unit and the first person camera stood at the world's origin, outside the level, and every map opened on the sky with the HUD's text and no weapon or meters. `network_game_create_game_objects` now arms `main_network_view_waiting`, which keeps `render_frame` off while a local player has neither a unit nor a body to watch, or its camera is outside the structure, for at most 150 ticks, and logs each map's first drawn frame with what it waited for
- **metal**: hold every frame of the level's first 180 that skips a draw for a pipeline still compiling, not only those before the first whole frame — the start hold ended at the first frame with nothing skipped, but the frames after it draw what the first did not (the HUD, effects, Blood Gulch's ground through a vertex shader the map's list pairs only with other pixel shaders) and make more pipelines, so on an iPhone 17 Pro three frames with the ground skipped showed the sky alone after one held frame. `metal_start_hold_frame` now holds any skipping frame in a window of `START_HOLD_WINDOW` frames, at most `START_HOLD_LIMIT` in a row, and logs the window's held and shown-with-skips counts at its end; `metal_compile_probe` covers a skipping frame after a whole one, the limit in a row and the window's end
- **loading**: hold the level's first frames until no draw is skipped for a pipeline still compiling — a shader the map's list does not name (`shader list …: made while drawing`) compiled on a worker after the loading screen ended, so the first frames showed the level without the draws that needed it (the sky alone, then the structure, the weapon and the HUD a frame later); the loading screen's end now sends `GPU_COMPILE_PROGRESS_FIRST_FRAME`, and with `display.async_shaders` the Metal backend keeps the window's last picture for frames that skip such a draw, for at most 120 frames. `metal_compile_probe` and `test_loading_progress.py` cover the hold, its limit and the call
- **external**: log what is on screen, not what was requested, and stop the ten-second mode refusal loop — `external_log_verified` reads back the window's visibility, key state, scene, activation state, whether the Metal view is in that window and whether the system still mirrors the main screen (at the move and 2 s and 6 s after), and the move line now says the renderer was moved; after two refused modes with the display still in its connect mode `host_external_poll` keeps that mode with the bounded drawable instead of trying every candidate
- **external**: fill the display, offer the scene again to a display plugged in while running, and let dynamic resolution step with the companion — the drawable now sits in a `VISRExternalView` fitted by `host_external_fit` to the scene's bounds (re-fitted on `UIScreenModeDidChangeNotification`) so a 1728x1080 picture is scaled up to a 2560x1600 display with bars only for a different shape; `host_external_refresh_due` registers the scene accessory again (twice at most) when a second screen or an HDMI/AirPlay route appears and the registration never becomes available; `dynamic_resolution_refused` no longer treats the companion picture as a refusal, since the companion records only the native HUD layer, so `debug.dynamic_resolution_sweep` and the planner run on an external display
- **texture-upscale**: background upscaling no longer keeps user-initiated priority and every core after the import wait — `boost` queued a user-initiated block behind the pump, which raised the pump (one block for the whole backlog, so its encoder threads too) until the backlog was done; the priority is now set per texture from `texture_upscale_priority_qos`, and outside a wait the encoder uses half the cores at utility so the touch controls and the render server keep a share of the processor
- **network**: mark a browsed game BIG only when it is half as full as this device holds, and drop the ticker note — `player_capacity_marked` adds the player count to `player_capacity_exceeded`'s limit check, so an empty lobby with a large limit is not flagged; the ", more than this device holds" text leaves both browsers' tickers
- **upscale**: read an old `debug.texture_upscale_generate = false` as auto — 0.5.x wrote the boolean into every `config.toml`, and `texture_upscale_mode_parse` read anything but `auto`, `true`, `1` and `force` as off, so an upgraded player's device never upscaled, with no log line. Only `off` and `force` keep their meaning now, and `off` logs why upscaling is off. `texture_upscale_schedule_probe.c` checks `false`, `0`, `""`, `off`, `force`.
- **upscale**: the pre-level wait is the loading screen, not an `Upscaling assets` screen — the line appears only while the worker is making the level's textures (never for an instant at 0%, never when they are ready), as a spinner with `Upscaling textures ...` at the lower left and `Press A to Skip` at the lower right in the HUD message font, and the loading bar tracks the upscaling; `texture_upscale_level_note_visible` is probed
- **upscale**: one whole-set `Upscaling textures` bar on the import screen — it read "Choose XISO" text while upscaling and labeled a separate `main menu` phase with that phase's own units; `host_texture_upscale_prepare_import` now always reports whole-set units, the bar is gray until the menu's textures are ready then VISR blue with `Start playing`, and the intro text, QR code and code are hidden on iOS and tvOS while textures are prepared
- **input**: hold only the player a menu belongs to — `ui_widgets_hold_player_input` freezes a local player's input for a menu opened for them or for no one, so another player's split-screen pause menu no longer stops the rest
- **touch**: keep the player still behind an open menu while the sticks and D-pad navigate it — `input_abstraction_update` now zeroes the player's movement, look and buttons while any widget is up, so a network game's pause menu no longer walks the player with the D-pad or stick; the touch pad passes the sticks again (the menu reads them) and `tools/test_touch_pause.py` checks the sticks pass and the triggers stay off
- **visionos**: fit `host_foveation_scaled_quality` per axis to the sweep and the configured maximum — review found the single 0.49 constant only held at a maximum of 0.6; it now inverts the fitted width and height lines at the configured `display.render_quality`, honors the game's 0.25 scale floor, and `foveation_scale_probe` checks both axes at maxima 0.6 and 1.0
- **visionos**: apply `display.render_scale` to foveated stereo views — the Compositor fixes each view's logical size, so the scale was ignored and `b30` rendered 5009x4018 at 0.8; `host_foveation_scaled_quality` now lowers the layer's runtime render quality by (1 - scale) / 0.49 below `display.render_quality`, which shrinks the views' physical size, with `foveation_scale_probe` checking the mapping
- **network**: leave the dedicated count out of the browser's game count when no game is dedicated — `browser_game_count_text` always appended "(0 dedicated)"; `tools/test_network_browser_dedicated.py` covers zero and nonzero counts
- **loading**: hold the loading screen over a network game's map load — `network_game_create_game_objects` loaded the level and started `halo_shader_list_warm` but drew the first frames at once, so with `display.async_shaders` the sky and structure draws were skipped and the fog color (cream on `bloodgulch`, lavender on `beavercreek`) showed with the level filling in over the next frames. The new `halo_loading_screen_begin_network`, `halo_loading_screen_finish` and `halo_loading_screen_cancel` hold the screen over the lobby's last frame until the compiles are done and `game.loading_screen_seconds` have passed, with no tail and no upscale wait, and give up after 5 s because the game's other machines wait for this one. `tools/test_loading_progress.py` covers the network hold, its limit and the cancel.
- **upscale**: a level with textures the decisions never listed is not ready — `texture_cache_progress` ignored such keys, so a map added since passed on its shared textures and mixed upscaled with original; the import polls the menu map's keys once
- **upscale**: a skipped pre-level wait still plays original when the catalog is not ready — `host_texture_upscale_level_ready` checks `level_skipped` before choosing the disk path, and `texture_upscale_driver_probe.m` covers a level loading before the catalog
- **upscale**: a level that loads before the launch's texture catalog is built reads its own list — `host_texture_upscale_level_ready` found no list in `published_paths` and played original ("its textures are not listed yet"); it now uses the saved decisions and manifest through `texture_upscale_level_source`, and `keys_for_map` scans the data root for a map the catalog does not list
- **touch**: block gameplay touch input while an in-game menu is up — the network game keeps running behind its pause menu, and only the sticks were zeroed, so fire and grenade (the triggers) and reload, weapon switch, crouch, zoom, flashlight and grenade switch still reached the game; with `VISR_PAUSE_MENU` set the touch pad now passes only accept, back, the D-pad and Start, and `tools/test_touch_pause.py` covers every axis and button
- **hud**: restore the Xbox's player names by default — OpenCE's `hud_draw_player_names` (added in `f2ba71d9a`, with the red enemy color and motion sensor range in `634b4197f`, `b438cea47` and `39350fb81`) drew every player's name over their head without the reticle on them, so `display.player_names` now defaults to `none` and only the game's own `internal_rasterize_target_name` shows the name of the player under the reticle; `all`, `allies` and `enemies` stay selectable
- **network**: keep the 38-to-39 join gate self-contained — preserve extracted probes and test refusal against version 40
- **ios**: take a crash's guest addresses relative to the arena's start — `host_crash.c` and `tools/guest_symbolicate.py` took a native address's low 32 bits as its guest address, which holds only for an arena at a multiple of 4 GB; with the arena free to start at any 16 KiB boundary, `host_crash.c` now subtracts `host_arena`, and `guest_symbolicate.py` subtracts the start from `ios-runtime.log`'s `CRASH: arena` line or from `--arena`, keeping the low-32-bit reading when neither is given.
- **ios**: match sp and x29 only as whole operands and keep x29 a frame address in every guest unit — `tools/ios_asm_convert.py` took a symbol such as `f.sp` for a register, refusing or rewriting it; it now compares comma-separated operands, and it refuses a load into x29 other than the epilogue's `ldp x29, x30`, so x29 cannot carry a spilled guest value. `GUEST_ABI_FLAGS` gains `-fno-omit-frame-pointer`, so musl, its math units and the probes reserve x29 as the game does. `tools/ios_test.py` now runs `tools/test_ios_asm_convert.py`, which runs with or without pytest; the README says how to turn a crash report's native address into a guest address, and the branch-range comment counts the converter's new instruction sequences.
- **ios**: read saved return addresses and frame pointers as guest addresses at an unaligned arena — `walk_up` in `stack_walk_windows.c` took the low halves of a frame record and `d3d8_device.c`'s render-validation caller took `__builtin_return_address(0)` as guest addresses, which holds only when the arena starts at a multiple of 4 GiB; both now go through `halo_guest_address` (`port/runtime/include/halo_guest_native.h`), which subtracts the arena's start read from x27, and `tools/ios_asm_convert.py` allows that one read of x27. The ILP32 probe checks a converted return address against its own code at both arena placements, and `probe_runner` no longer exits 0 when only failure bits above 255 are set.
- **ios**: name a crash log's return addresses by the calling function — `crash_now`'s frames are return addresses, and after a `noreturn` call that ends a function the return address is the next function's first byte, so a halt's log named frame 0 `host_map_load_failed+0x0`; `guest_symbol` and the `dladdr` lookup now look one byte back for a return address, and a fault's `pc` is still looked up as it is
- **ios**: pin the crash trap's reason pointer to `x16` — with every other register clobbered, the compiler passed it in `x30`, so a report's `lr` read as the pointer instead of the return address; `TRAP_WITH_REASON` now binds it to `x16` with a register variable and leaves the link register alone
- **ios**: name the map file in the level-load message — `levels\ui\ui` read as an internal path, so the player sees `ui` and `ui.map`
- **ios**: tell the player when a level fails to load instead of crashing — `main_new_map`'s "game_load() failed." is almost always a damaged or missing map file, which a crash report cannot help with, so `host_map_load_failed` logs the game's messages and shows a `host_fatal` message
- **mac_run**: launch native runs with `-ApplePersistenceIgnoreState` — after a run that crashed, AppKit asked before the app started whether to reopen its windows, and every later run on that host waited on it until killed
- **mac_run**: pick a crashed run's report by the pid its log names — the crash reporter writes a report seconds after the crash, so a later run's launch-time window caught the previous run's
- **ios**: carry a fatal error's reason in the crashed thread's registers — on macOS 27 a crash report keeps neither an uncaught `NSException`'s reason, a Swift `fatalError` message nor an app's `__crash_info`, so `host_crash.c` traps in `visr_guest_halt`, `visr_guest_abort` or `visr_host_error` with "VISRCRSH" and the guest image's short hash and the reason packed into x0-x15 and x19-x28
- **objects**: deactivate an active object outside the map instead of halting at `objects.c` #369
- **metal**: count a frame late in the frame log only past its cap's refreshes — under `display.frame_rate = "30"` on a 60 Hz display, or Low Power Mode's 30, every frame was counted late, since the log asked only for the pacing's or a scene class's refreshes
- **pacing**: halve a ProMotion screen's own 120 in Low Power Mode even if it reports 60, and catch a brief Low Power Mode in a benchmark run
- **metal**: give the system each frame's deadline, so a frame rate cap no longer drops the CPU's clock — `display.frame_deadline`
- **metal**: take the timing fallback's U+H as the least recent span, not the median — on a CPU-bound A9X iPad the present buffer's drawable waits put even the median of the HUD and present spans at 3-16 ms; the HUD's own work is about the same every frame and a wait only adds, so the least of the last 32 frames is its cost, and an underestimate errs toward holding the resolution
- **shadows**: count the blur pages where they do not replace the second targets, and stop retrying them at a scale that cannot use them — the atlas's memory budget counted a blur page's tile as its shadow's second target, which holds only under Metal's views; under GL (or with `HALO_METAL_NO_TILE_VIEWS`) both exist, so the budget now counts both there (`halo_shadow_blur_views`). At a shadow map scale whose tiles are not the second targets' size every frame blurred its shadows into the pages, failed the copy and blurred them all again; the failure now holds until the layout changes, and those frames blur into the slots directly (with the footprint pre-pass above 128 texels). The layout is also redone when the scale changes at the same page size, since the blur pages' columns follow the scale.
- **metal**: never make a view of a texture that is itself the linear source of views — `gpu_metal_texture_copy_rects` replaced such a destination's texture with a view but kept its `linear_buffer`, so a later copy from it would have made views of the stale buffer. It is copied instead.
- **ios**: compile the continued-processing submit against SDKs older than iOS 27 — `host_texture_upscale_continued.m` called `submitTaskRequest:completionHandler:`, which only the iOS 27 SDK declares, so the Xcode 26.6 CI build failed; the call is now guarded by `__IPHONE_OS_VERSION_MAX_ALLOWED` and older SDKs use `submitTaskRequest:error:`
- **tools**: keep signing whole-set `profiles-1`/`profiles-2` packs from `profiles/` — `snapshot` refused the folder once `hardware/revision.toml` was there, so `test_data_pack.py` and `test_profile_newer.py` failed on the wrong refusal; those packs predate revisions and their apps refuse the file, so it is now left out of them. Device shards keep their rules.
- **ios**: leave an older profile shard's sequence unused — `HostDataPackStore` used up the sequence of an envelope `supersededCheck` refused, so a build sharing the cache that bundles older profiles (a downgrade, a branch build with a higher revision, or a fixed build after one without a revision) then refused the same shard as already seen; the check runs before the sequence check at every download, so nothing depended on it. `profile_feed_probe.m` refuses a shard with newer bundled profiles and then stages and uses it after a downgrade.
- **textures**: keep the upscaled textures in Library/Caches and stop when storage is short — on iPhone, iPad and Vision Pro the cache was under Documents, shown in the Files app and only kept out of backups by its flag; it is now `Library/Caches/texture-cache`, which the system may purge under storage pressure (never while the app runs) and never backs up, and the old folder is removed at launch. tvOS's data root was already in `Library/Caches`, and Macs keep the cache under their data folder. The game's reader asks the host for the folder (`host_texture_cache_directory`). Before a pump and before each texture the generator checks the space the system offers a purgeable cache (`NSURLVolumeAvailableCapacityForOpportunisticUsageKey`; the volume's free space on tvOS, which does not offer it), and below the reserve it logs the numbers, stops as "low storage" and tries again later. A purged cache is made again, and readiness reads the files, so a partly purged one plays original.
- **ios**: leave the lines distribution out of reports where dynamic resolution is off for lack of stage timing — on an A9X iPad the benchmark report carried `dynamic_resolution` with every frame at the ceiling while the log said it was off; `host_perf_hooks_dynamic_lines` now also requires stage-boundary timing
- **metal**: repeat a pooled scene's edge texels before SMAA reads its copy — past the content `post.color` still held an earlier, larger frame, which SMAA's edge and blend passes read as edges along the last column and row; a 4-texel band of the edge is copied there for pooled targets only, so a split-screen view's neighbor is read as before
- **ios**: return dynamic resolution to the ceiling on unmeasured frames, and turn it off without stage timing — split screen's frames are ineligible for the planner, so `host_dynamic_resolution_lines` kept handing out the level it had when split screen began; a frame the planner could not measure now returns the ceiling and resets the plan. On a GPU without stage-boundary counter sampling the planner never decides, so it is off and the log says so once instead of holding the ceiling while claiming to be on. The settings are no longer settled before the device profile is installed (`host_config_profile_installed`), so a profile alone turns it on. `dynamic_resolution_actuation_probe.c` adds split screen, no-timing and profile-only cases
- **metal**: keep dynamic resolution's pool through passing refusals — a cutscene, the menus, a benchmark's own lines, the companion picture or a hand-set render scale had turned the pool off, so the first refused frame made a plain ceiling-sized color and depth pair beside the pooled one and both stayed for the session; now only the renderer, stereo, the native HUD and `debug.rate_map_test` turn it off, every other refusal renders the ceiling inside the pool, and when the pool does turn on or off `screen_targets_release` frees the other kind (the plain pair made before it was known to be on included). In-engine cutscenes are now eligible; only the menus are refused. On the Mac runner a10's opening cutscene into gameplay with the lines pinned keeps one pool, with the same live target count as a run without dynamic resolution
- **metal**: repeat the content's edge texels past it before MetalFX — the spatial scaler reads a few texels beyond `inputContentWidth`, so a pooled scene showed stale texels in the last 4 output columns and row; with a 4-texel band of the edge copied out, MetalFX at 720 lines inside a 1080-line pool matches a 720-line scene exactly on the Mac runner
- **textures**: never play a mixed level when the generator is off — with generation off (a player who turned it off after a partial run, or a measured run), `host_texture_upscale_level_ready` returned 1 without checking, so a partly filled cache drew some of a level's textures upscaled and the rest original. The generator now saves the whole-game decisions beside the manifest (`decisions.tsv`, `texture_cache_decisions_save`), and with the generator off a level plays upscaled only if those decisions, the manifest on disk and the level's own textures show every texture it upscales made and its file present; a cache without its decisions plays original, and no cache leaves the reader as before.
- **textures**: give the generator's large buffers their own mappings — freed blocks of a megabyte or more stayed in libmalloc's large-block cache, dirty and counted in the app's footprint, and `malloc_zone_pressure_relief` did not return them: on a Mac runner 395 MB of "Malloc Large (empty)" remained after the generator was released, and the footprint while generating reached 1,321 MB. `texture_upscale_big.h` maps blocks of 1 MB or more with `mmap` and unmaps them on free; the generator's images and outputs, the pixel and refinement modules and the host's source copies use it, and `texture_upscale_generate_raw4x` hands back a `malloc` copy. Measured on the same runner: 646 MB while generating, 44 MB of freed large blocks left after release.
- **import**: let the upscaling phase outlive the import's UIKit background time — the extraction's `beginBackgroundTaskWithName:` expiration set `cancelled`, which skipped the upscaling phase and completed the continued-processing task with it; it now cancels only the extraction, and always ends the UIKit task. The continued task ends for a stopped queue only after three idle readings a second apart, since a pump's end and the next leave a moment with neither, and "Start playing" during the phase says "Starting Halo…".
- **textures**: keep the upscaler's queue from failing quietly — a map that cannot be read or classified is left out of the catalog (its level plays original) instead of disabling the whole feature, and the import phase ends when the queue is idle rather than waiting at 0% for a catalog; a pump that ends on an error is tried again after two minutes, and the import phase logs "stopped" with the reason instead of "finished"; the Settings app's delete runs on the worker, between textures, emptying the manifest with the files, and a level counts as ready only when every record it needs is on disk at its size, so it can never play mixed; the player's `display.upscaled_textures` is read again at each pump; and a pump already running is raised to user-initiated priority when the import phase or a level's wait begins.
- **textures**: stop the generator's last stage for gameplay too — `texture_upscale_generate_end` (refinement, the damage measure, the high level and mips) had no cancel point, so `stop_for_gameplay` could wait its full 3 s and the texture's CPU work ran on into play; the bounded refinements now end between rows and `texture_upscale_generate_end` between its stages once the scheduler's stop flag is set (`texture_refine_set_cancel`), and the texture is made again later.
- **ios**: read the arena's start from a fatal error's log lines — `guest_symbolicate.py` matched only the signal handler's `CRASH: arena 0x…` line, but a halt, abort or `host_crash` writes `CRASH: guest image sha256 …, arena 0x…` instead, so those logs fell back to the low 32 bits and named the wrong guest frames for an arena off a multiple of 4 GB; it now also takes that line and the launch's `guest arena at …` line, and ends its output with a note when the arena's start is not known.

### perf

- **shaders**: add the shaders and pipelines an iPhone 17 Pro made while drawing on Blood Gulch and Sidewinder to their lists — 32 and 51 lines from its `shader list …: made while drawing` log lines, among them Blood Gulch's ground pipelines (vertex shader `b203a797c8a77c42` with its lightmapped pixel shaders), so they compile while the map loads; Sidewinder also gets the 1 vertex and 4 pixel shaders its new pipelines use from the other maps' lists, since a list's pipelines must name its own shaders (`host_shader_list_merge`), and the menus' one pipeline whose pixel shader no list has is left out
- **shadows**: let a device profile raise the smallest size that casts an object shadow
- **metal**: take the retains and releases out of a draw's lookups — `table_get`, `buffer_record` and `texture_record` returned their record as an `id` from an inlined function, which ARC still retained for the return and released after, and `front_cache_get`, `draw_pipeline`, `depth_state` and `sampler_state` did the same with their cached objects, about a sixth of `gpu_metal_draw`'s own time on the Mac runner. The lookups are now macros reading the table's unretained array, and the cached getters return the object unretained (`void *`, bridged by the caller): every one is kept for good by its table or dictionary before it is returned. `foveation_sizes` tests whether the target is foveated before taking its map. `gpu_metal_draw`'s retain and release calls fall from 59 to 19; nothing else changes. `test_metal_border.py`, which copies `sampler_state` into its probe by signature, finds the new one.
- **config**: find a setting by a hash of its name — `config_setting_index` compared the name with each of the settings in turn, and every `config_` read goes through it, many of them every frame. A table of the settings' indices by an FNV-1a hash of the name (open addressing, built on the first lookup under its own lock, since the guest's C library has no `pthread_once`) finds one with a single comparison; a name listed twice keeps its first index, as the scan did. `config_reload_probe.c` checks that every setting's name finds the first setting of that name and that other names find none.
- **textures**: read `display.upscaled_textures_memory` only when the replacement cache is made — `texture_replacements_start` read the setting before its early return, and it runs at every bind of a texture that has an upscaled replacement (`texture_entry_replacement`), so a level drawn upscaled looked the setting up by name several thousand times a frame; that lookup was about 15% of the main thread on Blood Gulch with 64 players on the Mac runner. The setting still sizes the cache when it is made.
- **shadows**: skip a new atlas tile's own clear — a deferred shadow's tile is already black: `shadow_atlas_page` clears its whole page, gutters included, before the frame's first tile in it, and a tile holds one shadow a frame, so `_rasterizer_environment_shadow_begin` no longer clears the tile again (the debug color still clears it). Blood Gulch with 64 players on the Mac runner (offscreen, profiling build): clears fall from 143 to 2.8 a frame, `render_object_shadows` from 1.05 to 0.99 ms a tick, the benchmark's translation time from 2.20 to 2.13 ms. On devices (profiling build, presented, alternating runs against the same build with this reverted) the removed tile clears turn out cheap: Providence (M1, 64 players) clears 142 to 64 a frame and `d3d8_clear` 0.57 to 0.53 ms, Bedroom (A10X, 64 players) 137 to 56 and about 1.06 to 0.89 ms, Photo Frame (A9X, 32 players) 90 to 22 and about 1.08 to 0.83 ms; frame rates unchanged, all three GPU-bound there. `check-metal`'s Metal frames are byte-identical to main's; the GL reference's `a10` frames 900 and 1050 change and now match Metal, where main's frame 900 had a 32,913-pixel Metal and GL difference within its accepted bounds, so GL's scissored clear of a tile was reaching pixels already drawn.
- **profiles**: scale the M1 iPad Pro profile up with MetalFX spatial — `display.upscaler = "metalfx"` in `ipad-pro-m1.toml` (inherited by newer M-series iPads through `and_newer`) costs about 1.0 ms of GPU time a frame and kept every measured tier on time on Providence (60 Hz: 99.93 to 99.98%; 120 Hz: 99.75 to 99.90%), with smoother edges than the bilinear stretch; profile revision bumped to 4
- **profiles**: 128-pixel shadows on the devices that miss or barely hold their tier — the iPad Pro 12.9-inch (1st and 2nd generation), the iPhone 12 Pro, the Apple TV 4K (1st generation) and the Apple TV HD now take the game's own shadow map size, as do the iPhone and iPad and Apple TV class floors. Shadow detail is the first setting lowered before a tier: at 64 players on Blood Gulch an A10X's GPU took 28 to 30 ms a frame at 128 against 53 to 54 at 512, and an A14's 24.6 against 29.8 to 30.2, with the batched shadow blur. Each profile's evidence gives its device's numbers. Profile set revision 3.
- **metal**: make copied tiles views of their source — the first `gpu_texture_copy_rects` from a BGRA8 render target moves it into a buffer as a linear texture with its contents, and from then on each destination becomes a view of its rectangle in that buffer instead of a copy, so the shadow blur pages' tiles are sampled in place, each through its own texture with native border addressing. A draw that samples a view declares the source texture to its encoder (`useResource:`), so Metal orders it after the source's pass and the source's next pass after it. Destinations of another size or at an offset the buffer's alignment does not allow are still copied; `HALO_METAL_NO_TILE_VIEWS` turns the views off.
- **shadows**: blur deferred shadows into a shared page — each deferred shadow's blur had a render pass of its own into its slot's 128x128 second target, about 145 passes a frame at 64 players. The blurs now go into tiles of one blur page laid out like the atlas pages, a page's worth at a time, and each batch is copied into the slots' second targets in one copy (`halo_shadow_blur_copy`, `gpu_texture_copy_rects`), so a batch is one render pass and one blit. The shadows are still applied from their own targets with border addressing, so sampling is unchanged; each blur covers the same pixels of its tile as of its target, and the texels it leaves (its quad stops half a texel short of the far edges) are black in the cleared page as in a new target. A batch that cannot be copied (a shadow map scale that does not divide evenly) is blurred into its slots as before. The blur page counts against the atlas's memory budget. `check-metal`: menu and `a10` frames byte-identical to main's under GL and Metal.
- **metal**: limit a pooled pass to the content — `pass_begin` sets `renderTargetWidth`/`renderTargetHeight` to the content when every attachment has the same `gpu_texture_set_content` size, so loads, stores and clears cover the level's tiles rather than the ceiling's, and `target_size` keeps scissors and whole-target clears inside it. With Metal API validation on, the Mac runner reports no errors and the pinned pool still matches a plain scene
- **textures**: reuse the generator's large mappings while it runs — a fresh mapping faults in every page again, which made refinement and the end stage 20 to 25% slower per texture than with malloc's cache on an M1 iPad; freed mappings are now kept for the next block of the same size, up to 192 MB, and `texture_upscale_big_trim` unmaps them when the scheduler releases the generator, so nothing stays after the release.

### refactor

- **shadows**: blur pages without gutters, one copy a page — the blurs go into tiles of as many blur pages as the frame's deferred shadows need, each about 2048 pixels a side with a power of two of tiles a row (16 at 128-pixel shadow maps, 4 at 512) and no gutters, since nothing samples a blur page past a tile. A page is cleared before its first blur of the frame, and after a window's blurs each page's tiles become their shadows' second targets with one `gpu_texture_copy_rects`; a window whose tiles cannot is blurred into its slots as before. At 512-pixel shadow maps the single gutter page held 9 tiles, so a frame's 145 shadows took 16 passes and as many copies.

### build

- **benchmark**: bind the 20-case tester catalog to the 0.6.0 guest at its own feed — `docs/site/packs/tester/recordings-28b14010a886a159afff1bec13ec7f516b5235f270934906f15ff59ff10b0fd1.json` is the unchanged ship-042 catalog signed as sequence 1793099944 with compatibility `benchmark-catalog-1:28b14010a886a159afff1bec13ec7f516b5235f270934906f15ff59ff10b0fd1`, which 0.6.0 builds read; the 0.4.2, 0.5.0, 0.5.1 and 0.5.2 feeds stay byte-identical, and `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` and `tools/test_pages_main_assets.py` name the new envelope's SHA-256
- **profiles**: publish the 20 tester device-profile shards for generation 2 — 0.6.0 builds read `docs/site/packs/tester/device-profiles-2/`, which had no shards, so the release policy could not pin any and testers would only ever get bundled profiles; each shard is signed as sequence 1793073358 from the revision 7 `profiles/` tree with compatibility `device-profiles-2:<target>`, one per target the 0.5.2 policy pins, `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` names their SHA-256s and `tools/test_pages_main_assets.py` counts them. `profiles/` (generation 1) stays byte-identical.
- **ios**: embed the guest's symbol table beside its code — `tools/ios_embed_guest.py` writes `guest-symbols.bin` into `__TEXT,__halosyms` and the same table as `guest-symbols.txt`, which an App Store package and the `release-provenance` artifact keep, so a crash report's guest frames can be named
- **benchmark**: bind the 20-case tester catalog to the 0.5.2 guest at its own feed — `docs/site/packs/tester/recordings-a949cfcc94abf93df5bd091bd9d27a6933dbc688b81f52c3ea863fa720ff99eb.json` is the unchanged ship-042 catalog signed as sequence 1793039045 with compatibility `benchmark-catalog-1:a949cfcc94abf93df5bd091bd9d27a6933dbc688b81f52c3ea863fa720ff99eb`, which 0.5.2 builds read; `docs/site/packs/tester/recordings.json`, the 0.5.0 and the 0.5.1 feeds stay byte-identical, and `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` and `tools/test_pages_main_assets.py` name the new envelope's SHA-256
- **profiles**: re-sign the Apple TV 4K (1st generation) tester shard with the 1080 output cap — `docs/site/packs/tester/profiles/tvos/AppleTV6,2/_.json` is sequence 3, the only shard whose files changed, and `docs/site/packs/tester/publication.json` names its new SHA-256; against sequence 2 only `apple-tv-4k-1st.toml` differs (`display.output_height` 1080 instead of 720, the header and the evidence), with `apple-tv.toml` and `hardware/catalog.toml` byte-identical. The 0.4.2, 0.5.0 and 0.5.1 profile resolvers and delivery validators accept it and resolve `AppleTV6,2` to an output height of 1080 at render 720.
- **textures**: compile `HALO_TEXTURE_UPSCALE_CLEAN` and `HALO_TEXTURE_UPSCALE_MODEL` out of App Store packages too, under `HALO_DEVELOPMENT_HOOKS`, and make the guard test track nested conditionals so a switch under another `#if` no longer passes as guarded.
- **textures**: compile the upscaler's development switches out of App Store packages — `HALO_TEXTURE_UPSCALE_STOP_TEST` and `HALO_TEXTURE_UPSCALE_IMPORT_PHASE` are read only under `HALO_DEVELOPMENT_HOOKS`, a CMake option that is off unless asked for; `tools/ios_build.py` and `tools/macos_build.py` turn it on for development builds and off for `--app-store-package`, and `tools/test_ios_build.py` checks both the flag and that the switches are read only inside the guard.

### docs

- **benchmarks**: drop "with each app's default settings" from the iPhone, iPad, Apple TV and Vision Pro captions and the device methodology line, and "lifting its cap crashes it" from the master-chef line — `page.py` and the three `*.chart.toml` paragraphs say the runs are presented at the display rate, and the master-chef line keeps its fixed 30 fps below the display rate
- **benchmarks**: add the master-chef Vision Pro rows from the results sheet — the snapshot gains the a10 and b30 rows, a build whose `fps_1pct_low` is blank charts its average alone (`charts.py` and `benchmarks.js` skip the 1% low bar and legend entry instead of plotting zero), the Vision Pro caption notes that master-chef is fixed at 30 fps as shipped, and `static-vision-pro.svg` is the no-JavaScript chart
- **benchmarks**: describe each platform's timing method and the device runs from the data — `page.py` builds a "Timing by platform" section from the rows' `method`, `frame_mode` and ports (Mac `metalperftrace`, iPhone and iPad Metal System Trace `displayed-surfaces-interval`, Vision Pro app reports), the iPhone, iPad, Apple TV and Vision Pro captions and chart filters describe presented runs at the display rate instead of "frame cap and vsync off", Halo OG's full-size rendering and missing settings and master-chef's fixed 30 fps are noted from the rows, the Multiplayer line says how the match and the bot player are set and that cross-port runs are equivalent workloads rather than identical replays, and each suite reports its measured ports or "No measurements yet" from the data
- **site**: link "unmatched performance" to the benchmarks page — the home page uses the relative `benchmarks.html` and the README block uses the page's site address, both generated from `docs/features.toml`
- **benchmarks**: publish the cross-port benchmark page — one chart per platform in its device frame (Mac charted; iPhone and iPad, Apple TV and Vision Pro coming with VISR 0.6.0), average fps with the 1% low as the geometric mean over each suite's windows, a suite switch and a build list per platform, data from the public results sheet snapshotted at publish (`tools/perf_compare/sheet_fetch.py`) with only released builds, and the methodology at the bottom
- **changelog**: list only the tagged work under 0.5.2 and the held work as unreleased — the generated `visr-v0.5.2` section named the 66 main commits held for the cut and dropped the Unreleased section; it now lists the network version 39 join, and Unreleased holds its own entries plus the held commits
- **linux**: list version 39 among the versions the Server Browser joins — `port/linux/README.md` still said 29 to 32, 34 to 36 and 38
- **ios**: name `host_crash.c`, not `host_crash.m`, in the crash comments — three comments pointed at a file that does not exist
- **ios**: describe crash reports and `guest_symbolicate.py` — where a fatal error's report goes, how its reason and guest frames are read, and the release artifact that keeps a build's guest symbols
- **packs**: describe profile feed generations and revisions — which directory each app reads, when to bump the generation, how revisions order shards against the bundle, and how to roll a profile back
- **changelog**: list only the tagged work under 0.5.1 and the held work as unreleased — the generated `visr-v0.5.1` section also named the 26 commits from `0cd67af41` to `ac7fb5fa5`, which sit in history before the tag but were held out of its tree; they move to an Unreleased section with a summary line for no longer needing the extended virtual addressing entitlement, and the 0.5.1 summary now says what the tagged build changes.

### test

- **network**: stub `main_network_view_wait_arm` in `test_network_shader_warmup.py` — `network_game_create_game_objects` now arms the first-frame wait, which the probe's stubs did not define
- **release**: skip the Mac zip checks where `ditto` is missing — `tools/test_release_unsigned.py` zips and unzips the Mac app with macOS's `ditto`, so the Linux Tooling job failed three tests on `main` since `a7cde7491`; they now skip there and still run on macOS
- **loading**: cover the network loading screen's tail guard and the loader's begin, finish and cancel — `test_network_shader_warmup.py` stubs the three `halo_loading_screen_*` network entries and counts them on success, a failed `game_load` and a failed spawn, and `test_loading_progress.py` loads a solo scenario through the network entry so `!loading_screen.network` decides the tail; the cap's log line now names the elapsed hold
- **config**: `display.player_names` migrates from a written `"all"` to `"none"` — `check_player_names_default` in `config_origins_probe.c` shows a 0.5.x install whose `config.origins` marks `"all"` as the default follows the new default, a player-chosen `"all"` stays, and a new install records `"none"` as its written default
- **objects**: define the profile scope macros in the #369 probe — `objects_update` now marks its sections with `profile_scope_enter` and `profile_scope_exit`, which the probe's prologue did not define, so it no longer compiled; the probe defines them as `cseries/profile_sections.h` does outside the profiling build.
- **ios**: add `debug.crash_test` "map" to fail the first level load — so the message `host_map_load_failed` shows for a damaged map file can be checked without one
- **objects**: make the #369 negative control fail on the assertion — the probe's `match_assert` only counted, so without the fix the out-of-range bit read that follows it tripped AddressSanitizer first, and the control caught any `AssertionError`, a compile failure included; `match_assert` now stops the probe as a checked build does, and the control requires that stop.
- **metal**: give the border probe's texture records and encoder what views use — `bind_stages` now reads `view_parent` and may call `useResource:usage:stages:`, so `metal_border_probe.m` declares both (the probe makes no views, so a call fails it).
- **release**: give the release fixtures this source's profile feed directory — `require_asset_policy` now refuses a new release whose `profile_feed_url` is the frozen `profiles/` directory, so five `test_release.py` cases stopped before the check each one tests.
- **profile**: preprocess the game's files on Apple Silicon — `test_new_files_are_empty_in_a_normal_build` passed `-march=native` to a `--target=i686-linux-gnu` clang, which resolves it to the host CPU (`apple-m2`) and rejects it; the flags now drop it, use the macOS SDK with an i686 Darwin target where there is no Linux C library, and build the generated forced-include headers that are missing
- **textures**: `HALO_TEXTURE_UPSCALE_STOP_TEST` holds a level's start until the upscaler has a texture in hand (up to 10 s, development only), so a device run can show the stop for gameplay interrupting a texture and how long the handoff takes; on an M1 iPad the worker otherwise finishes a level's textures before its load ends, and the stop lands between textures.

### chore
