## visr-v0.1.2 (2026-10-16)

### feat

- **profiles**: add bounded `and_newer` rules with exact-match priority
- **tools**: generate settings schemas and reference tables from C definitions
- **packs**: publish the tester profile pack as `profiles-2`, sequence 1 — `docs/site/packs/tester/profiles-2.json` is the signed snapshot of `profiles/` with its external-display sections, at the path apps now fetch; `profiles.json` (`profiles-1`) is removed, so apps built before `profiles-2` keep their bundled profiles, and `docs/data-packs.md` says so
- **packs**: publish the tester profile pack, sequence 3 — `docs/site/packs/tester/profiles.json`
- **packs**: publish the tester profile pack, sequence 2 — `docs/site/packs/tester/profiles.json`
- **packs**: publish the tester profile pack, sequence 1 — `docs/site/packs/tester/profiles.json` is the signed snapshot of `profiles/` that tester builds download from the project site
- **loading-screen**: the loading screen stands in for the level's first ticks, and its progress moves smoothly — `halo_loading_screen_progress` eases a displayed value toward a target by elapsed time (the lesser of the minimum time's share and the work's share, scaled to end at `COMPILE_END`, with a slow creep while the compiles stall) and never moves it back; once the work is done the screen goes on as a tail, `main_loop` drawing it with `main_pregame_render` in place of the game while the level's opening script gets its ticks, then stands game time still until the picture has faded to black, so the cutscene's first frame follows with no black gap and no player's-start frame. `progress_bar_render` skips its own wobble and per-frame lag while the loading screen drives it. The black hold stays as the fallback where the loading screen is off.
- **benchmark**: the beach fight on b30 is a gameplay scene — `b30-beach` is a hand-played heroic take from an Apple TV (controller input, per-tick actions and seeds, all numbers; its `.route` names the scene `b30-fight` and measures the first 85 s, while the player is alive and fighting, and its `.checks` hold where player 1 stood every 300 ticks), with `tools/route_checks/b30-beach.checks` for the Mac runner
- **pacing**: re-read the look stick just before the camera is built, behind `display.late_look` — `late_look` in `render_interpolation.c` turns the first-person camera, not the facing, by what the stick asks for over the time since the game read it this frame (`halo_look_stick_latest` in `xinput_sdl.c`), at the turn rate the game itself just applied, so sensitivity, zoom, acceleration and aim assist are not repeated; off by default, F9 switches it while playing, and it does nothing under `debug.fixed_timestep`, a test's input or a replay; every 600 frames the log says how old the game's read was at the camera, on or off, which is all there is to gain
- **pacing**: start a frame's work as late as it can be and still make the next refresh — `display.late_start`: the frame pacer waits before a frame reads its input (`frame_pacer_frame_start`), so the wait for the display comes before the picture is decided instead of after, and what the player does reaches the screen sooner; on in the Mac app, off on iPhone, iPad and Apple TV until measured there, never in a Vision Pro build, and the Mac app's F8 switches it while playing
- **ios**: add wired external output and a HUD companion
- **ios**: say what internet play reaches before turning it on, and offer it at the first internet screen behind `network.online_ask` — Apple builds now turn `network.online` on only from a question that tells the player the matchmaking servers are other people's and that those servers and the other players see their IP address (`HALO_INTERNET_DISCLOSURE`): a join link's "Join this game over the internet?" carries it, and Settings' INTERNET PLAY switch asks "Turn on Internet Play?" before it writes anything (`platform_internet_play_request`, asked at the next pump as a link's question is; Turn On writes the setting and starts internet play at once, Not Now leaves it off); with the new `network.online_ask` true, the first of Create Game > Internet, Join Game > Server Browser or Direct Link opened with internet play off asks the same question once (`menu_internet_screen_opened`, `halo_internet_should_ask`: never in a network game or its lobby), and either answer writes it false; it defaults to false (`DEFAULT_ONLINE_ASK`), so nothing changes until that is decided, and "true" is what internet play on by default means on an Apple build, since nothing reaches a server before the player agrees; `debug.link_consent_answer` answers both questions, `debug.network_test`'s host asks as Create Game > Internet does, `tools/mac_run.py` resets the setting between runs, and `tools/ios_test.py` checks that each write of `network.online` follows its question
- **p2p**: say when no matchmaking server can be reached, and when the server browser's games are all on other network versions — `p2p_servers_status` tells whether the signalling brokers are untried, being tried, reached or unreachable (none reached in 12 seconds, or every one failed; `halo_internet_servers` in the new `halo_internet.h`, pure and probed in `internet_probe.c`), and the log says so each time that changes for the worse or back; the Server Browser's empty list then reads "Can't reach the matchmaking servers: check your internet" instead of "No public games found", a host's Server Setup shows it in place of the invite and its lobby in place of "Invite link copied"; `p2p_lobby_other_versions` counts the public games heard whose network version is not this build's (unverified listings, forgotten as games are), so an empty list says how many there are and whether they are newer ("update this game"); `tools/ios_test.py` also checks that `p2p.c` begins signalling only for a join, a hosted internet game and the server browser, and STUN only for the first two
- **ios**: keep the game's log in `stderr.log` once a join link opens, so that internet play's steps are on the device afterward
- **tools**: add `tools/p2p_broker_probe.py`, which checks from a computer's network that the brokers in brokers.txt answer MQTT, counts the public games they list by network version, checks the STUN servers, and finds an invite's host's public listing
- **p2p**: say on Direct Link which step of joining an invite failed, as soon as it is known, and log each step with its time
- **ios**: share the host's invite with the system's share sheet — `posix_share_link` presents it over the game from a window of `host_present_window`'s (a popover on iPad, theater mode too), Server Setup's copy invite opens it beside the clipboard copy, and the lobby's Y opens it, on Apple builds alone
- **menus**: open Direct Link when a join link is taken in the menus — `p2p_invite_received` marks an invite `poll_invite_file` took and the main thread hands it to `menu_invite_taken` (as `platform_link_consent` does after its join), Direct Link says it connects until the host's game comes, a link that launched the app opens it once the main menu is up, and in a game or a lobby a notice says where to find the invite afterwards
- **net**: ask to turn on internet play for a join link opened with it off on Apple builds — the host now writes `join_link.txt` either way, the guest's `platform_link_consent` takes it while `network.online` is off and p2p does not run (so it never races `poll_invite_file`), and Join turns internet play on through `xnet_start_internet_play`, which starts p2p late and tells it the game's sockets already open; INTERNET PLAY in Settings now starts at once, and new internet games are private by default
- **ios**: tell the player when a `halo:` link is not a join link — `host_join_link_open` leaves the message waiting and `host_join_link_poll`, called from `host_sdl_poll_event` between the guest's frames, shows "That link isn't a Halo invite, or it's from an older version of the game." (spec section 3); join-links only logged it
- **menus**: say what the LAN list can find on Apple devices — `halo_lan_empty_text` picks the empty-list text, so with local network access refused it points to the setting, and without broadcasts it says other Apple devices are found and other systems need an invite link
- **ios**: find system link games on Apple devices with Bonjour — `host_bonjour.m` registers and browses `_halo-ce._udp` on a private queue while the game hosts or searches, resolves peers to IPv4 on the interface they were found on, retries while local network access is refused, and turns broadcasts on from the build's multicast entitlement
- **ios**: take the system link's address from Wi-Fi, then the hotspot network — `halo_interface_rank` orders `en0`, `bridge100` and other broadcast interfaces and never takes cellular or VPNs, and `posix_local_ipv4_address` now prefers it to the default route on Apple devices through `host_ranked_interface`
- **ios**: the peer table Bonjour fills — `host_lan.c` keeps found machines per interface with up to four addresses each, evicts the oldest when full, never lists this machine, builds and matches the `v=` and `f=` TXT record, and holds discovery while hosting or for 10 seconds after a search
- **net**: broadcasts reach the machines discovery found — `halo_lan_targets` adds the peers after `network.broadcast`'s list and the real broadcast without ever removing either, the listening sockets hold discovery up while a machine hosts, and a search refreshes it
- **net**: LAN discovery in `posix.h` — `posix_lan_discovery`, `posix_lan_peers`, `posix_lan_status` and `posix_lan_broadcast_address`, stubbed on Linux and Windows, with the Apple host's broadcast permission from `network.lan_broadcast` and the build's entitlement
- **runner**: clone the movies into the native runner's data folder when `--maps` has a `bink` folder beside it
- **bink**: play the intro and credits movies in the Apple apps, behind the `HALO_BINK` build switch
- **import**: copy the intro and credits movies out of the disc image with the maps
- **bink**: decode a Bink movie with the vendored FFmpeg in `bink_decoder.c`, with a probe and `tools/bink_test.py`
- **render**: draw a seated rider's legs under the view — in a vehicle's first-person seat the camera is the vehicle's own marker, ahead of where the game seats the body (a Warthog passenger's hips are about 0.22 world units behind it), so looking down showed the footwell and the waist somewhere behind the eye. `first_person_legs_node_matrices` now draws the seated body moved ahead along the way it faces by `display.first_person_legs_seat_shift` (0 to 1, default 1) of the way that brings the hips under the eye, at most `FIRST_PERSON_LEGS_SEAT_SHIFT_MAXIMUM` (0.2 world units) so the knees stay short of the dash; the body's shadow moves with it. The pose is the game's own, and on foot and in third-person seats nothing changes
- **menus**: a Frame Rate row in the Mac app's Video Setup — `display.frame_rate` could only be set in `config.toml`, since the settings screens tag a row for the desktop builds or for every build and had no tag for the Mac app alone
- **profiles**: let a device profile set `display.anti_aliasing` — `off`, `fxaa` or `smaa` in `port/linux/src/port_profile_keys.h`; the key already resolves through the settings layers in `port_config.c`, so a profile's value takes effect at startup through `anti_aliasing_read`, while a value in `config.toml`, `HALO_ANTI_ALIASING` and the live chord still win; `profile_probe.c` checks that a value outside the choices is refused
- **render**: log each minute that the FXAA pass runs, and pass `HALO_ANTI_ALIASING` from a launch's environment to the game on iOS — FXAA already ran in every scene on the iPhone 17 Pro (menu, cutscene and gameplay, one pass per frame over the whole 2622x1206 scene), but the guest's `platform_log` lines reach only a `stderr.log` that the app never creates, so `anti-aliasing:` never showed; `anti_aliasing_pass` now logs its first pass and one every 7200, `gpu_metal_anti_alias` logs once if it skips, and `host_main.m` forwards the variable, which the guest's fixed environment list had dropped
- **build**: allow `--extended-virtual-addressing` for iPhone and iPad builds — a first-generation iPad Pro (A9X, 4 GB) cannot reserve the game's 4 GB arena, and only tvOS and visionOS could sign with the entitlement; `tools/ios_build.py` now refuses the flag only for `--mac`, the iOS branch of `port/ios/CMakeLists.txt` signs with the new `VISR-iOS-extended.entitlements`, and `--cloud-saves` adds its iCloud keys to that file as it does on tvOS
- **benchmark**: return to the main menu after the report screen's Done instead of quitting — `host_benchmark_next` answers 2 when a report screen was shown, and `benchmark_suite.c` then clears the run overrides and goes to the menu
- **menus**: the main menu has no Quit on an iPhone, iPad, Apple TV or Vision Pro — these systems don't let an app quit itself, and Quit's OK did nothing (the Siri Remote's Back on the root menu brought up "are you sure" and pressing OK was dead); the main menu's `main_menu_item_quit_game` child and the `back` and `b` handlers that open the quit question now have `platform="quit"`, a new value of the menus' existing `platform` attribute (`menu_platform.h`), read where the app can end itself: the desktop ports, and the Mac app, whose host sets `HALO_APP_CAN_QUIT` for the game (`host_main.m`; the Apple devices' guest is built `HALO_ILP32` for all of them, so the Mac cannot be told apart at build time); the item is not read at all, so Up and Down wrap over the four items left and the row has no gap, since Quit was the last item; the Mac app's `platform_request_quit` was a no-op like the others' and now calls `exit` when the host says the app can end itself; the pause menu's quit to main menu is another screen (`pause_game_quit_to_main_menu`) and is not touched; `tools/ce_menus.py` and `tools/port_settings.py` produce the same `main_menu.xml` (`WIDGET_PATCHES`, `child_platform`); `tools/ios_test.py` builds `menu_platform_probe.c` for the desktop, the Apple guest, the Mac app and Android and checks `main_menu.xml` for four items a row apart and no way left to the question
- **build**: device and simulator builds download signed updates from the project's own feeds — `tools/ios_build.py` pins the project's Ed25519 public key (`DATA_PACK_PUBLIC_KEY`) and the feeds on its site, `https://code.steverice.org/visr/packs/CHANNEL/profiles.json` and `recordings.json`, with the `tester` channel for `--tester` and `stable` otherwise; `--mac` (the runner) downloads nothing unless a key or feed is named, `--data-packs off` turns downloads off for any build, and named keys and feeds still replace the defaults
- **benchmark**: a benchmark session keeps the recordings it started with — `host_benchmark_snapshot.m` copies the selected recording snapshot (bundled or downloaded, then `local-routes`) into the benchmark folder with a hash of every file and the session's identity (guest image, resolved profile, suite, mode and device settings), and an unfinished session resumes only when that snapshot still verifies byte for byte and its hash matches the one bound to the journal; anything else starts a fresh session, so a recording update that arrives mid-suite cannot change the routes; the report adds `data_updates` (each snapshot's sequence, hash and compatibility identifier, so `data_updates.recordings.compatibility` is the identifier a recording pack for this build must carry), `recording_snapshot` and `profile_sha256`
- **packs**: profiles and benchmark recordings update from signed snapshots without a new build — `host_data_updates.m` opens one store for profiles (`profiles-1`) and one for recordings (`benchmark-replay-1:` plus the guest image's SHA-256), selects their snapshots once before any profile or replay is read, checks each feed at most once a day over HTTPS without redirects, and marks the launch healthy from `host_gpu_dispatch.c` once a main-menu frame is presented; `tools/ios_build.py` takes `--data-pack-public-key`, `--profile-feed-url` and `--recording-feed-url`, refuses a malformed key and any feed URL that is not plain HTTPS, and always passes all three to CMake for device and Mac builds, which put them in every app's `Info.plist` with the `tester` or `stable` channel; with none set, as by default, the app never downloads anything; `docs/data-packs.md` describes building, publishing and rolling back
- **profiles**: admit a downloaded profile snapshot only as a complete valid set — `host_profile_validate_folder` refuses unknown settings, out-of-range values, names with control characters, symlinks, incomplete class coverage, overlapping model identities and catalog entries that point at the wrong profile; `data_pack.py` refuses the same profile names before signing, and `host_profile_encode` turns control characters in a value's source into spaces, so a profile name can never start another field or setting in the encoded values
- **packs**: a signed data-pack store that stages, activates and rolls back profile and recording snapshots — `HostDataPackStore` verifies an Ed25519-signed envelope against a pinned key, checks the payload hash, the kind, channel and compatibility identifier, file names and sizes (manifest up to 64 KiB), stages a complete generation by rename, selects it only at the next launch, keeps it once a main-menu frame is presented and returns to the last healthy snapshot or the bundled data after a failed launch; sequences only go up, duplicate JSON keys are refused, and a FIFO or other non-regular file in the cache fails closed without blocking (`O_NONBLOCK`), with child-process regressions in `data_pack_probe.m` for the state file and a cached envelope
- **packs**: publish validated offline data snapshots — `data_pack.py` signs profiles and deterministic replay sets with Ed25519
- **profiles**: inherit the strongest compatible hardware profile at or below the device
- **replay**: a replay logs where player 1 stands and warns when the level runs at another difficulty than the take's — `debug.input_replay_route` (every this many ticks) logs "route segment S tick T at X,Y,Z" from `route_log`, with whether the tick took a recorded action and how many ticks the game's random seeds were not the recording's before they were set; `replay_check_difficulty` compares the `.route`'s `difficulty` with the level's at the first replayed tick and says so when they differ, since a replay at another difficulty meets other enemies and other scripts (a10's look tutorial runs only on easy and normal), and each segment's beginning is logged with why it began
- **network**: a player whose app goes to the background keeps their place for a grace period (`network.background_grace`, 60 seconds) — an Apple device suspends an app in the background within seconds, and the other players of an internet game dropped it at 15 to 20 seconds (`PEER_TIMEOUT`, `NETWORK_GAME_SERVER_CLIENT_TIMEOUT`, the reliable stream's write timeout); now the app asks the system for its background time (`beginBackgroundTaskWithName`, `host_lifecycle.m`) and tells its peers it is away and for how long (`_packet_away`, `update_away` in `p2p.c`, every 2 seconds while it can run, and 0 when it is back); a peer that is away is waited for until the grace ends, not timed out (`update_peers`, `network_game_server_client_machine_timed_out`, `network_connection_flush_reliable`, whose reliable queue grows to hold what is sent meanwhile); `network_away.c` shows each local player "NAME's game is in the background: waiting up to N seconds", "NAME is back" or "NAME did not come back in time"; a joiner whose game was lost while it or its host was away joins the host's invite again from the main menu (`p2p_rejoin_invite`, `network_away_game_lost`); the packet and the wait are in `p2p_away.h`; not yet seen on devices
- **loading**: hold the game's loading screen over a campaign level's load and its shader compiles — the screen in `progress_bar.c` ran only while a map was first copied to the cache, and drew nothing here: its draws name Direct3D's fixed function, which the device lacks, and it sampled the back buffer it was drawing into. `progress_bar.c` now draws with a pass-through vertex program and keeps each frame's picture in a target of its own for the blur, across the screen's width with the picture's 640 columns centered. `loading_screen.c` starts the screen in `main_change_map_name` and keeps drawing it at the end of `main_new_map`, sixty frames a second, until `gpu_compiles_outstanding` is 0 and `game.loading_screen_seconds` (1.5) have passed, with no game tick and no `sound_render` behind it, then restarts the frame clock so the level's opening starts at its first frame. `game.loading_screen` is "auto" (off in deterministic runs and in stereo), "on" or "off"
- **gpu**: add `gpu_compiles_outstanding` — the count of shaders and pipelines still compiling off the game thread under `display.async_shaders` (the Metal backend's `compiles_outstanding`; 0 from GL), so the front end can wait for a level's listed pipelines without blocking on them
- **audio**: mix DirectSound buffers, for the loading screen's tone — `DirectSoundCreateBuffer` made silent stand-ins, so the four loops of `compressed_monk_tone` that `progress_bar_make_stuff_ready` starts were never heard. A buffer with mono Xbox ADPCM data is now decoded when its data is set and mixed while it plays, looped, at the pitch `IDirectSoundBuffer_SetPitch` gives (4096 units an octave from 48 kHz) and the volume `IDirectSoundBuffer_SetVolume` gives, ramped across each mix; the game's silent keep-alive buffer still mixes nothing
- **import**: take the loading screen's picture from the player's disc — the loading screen lights a 320 by 240 gray picture that the PAL disc carries as `maps\loading.tga`, while the NTSC disc holds it in `default.xbe` as runs of gray levels, so an NTSC import had no picture and the screen lit nothing. `xiso_extract_maps` now writes `maps/loading.tga` from the disc's own file or from its executable (`loading_picture.h`), never failing an import over it, and `tools/loading_picture.py` writes the same file into maps imported earlier. The repository holds only the run format, no picture
- **render**: capture a window of frames at the `gpu.h` boundary and replay it without the game — `gpu_capture.c` records the front end's calls for the frames `debug.render_capture_frames` names into `debug.render_capture`'s folder, one gzip file a window, holding only what those frames use (each texture, shader and page of mirrored vertex memory is written the first time a call uses it); `gpu_replay.c` plays a file into a backend, mapping the file's handles and streamed ranges to the run's own and refusing a record that does not hold together; `host_render_replay.c` does it in the app (`HALO_RENDER_REPLAY`), through the validator, saving each frame; `tools/render_replay.py` replays, checks a set against its records, fuzzes and compares across backends
- **render**: name every fallback and skip for render validation — `GPU_VALIDATION_REPORT` in `gpu_metal.m` and `gpu_gl.c` where a backend drops a draw, ignores an upload, copy or mipmap request, falls back from a specialized vertex shader, exact border colors or MetalFX, or fails a pipeline or program (a draw skipped while its pipeline compiles on a worker is reported as expected, apart from one whose pipeline cannot be built); `d3d8_device.c` sends each draw's vertex program, pixel shader key and calling game code ahead of it, and names its own skipped draws, unmapped state values, dropped signed blend bias, ignored render states and the previous back buffer it does not keep
- **render**: a validator at the `gpu.h` boundary for debug builds — `gpu_validate.c` wraps a `struct gpu_backend` and checks every call before the backend sees it: every index against what each vertex stream holds, ranges against their buffers, the mirror's uploaded pages and the frame's streamed ranges, every float of the attributes the vertex shader reads and of the uniforms, the constant registers the shader reads, textures and samplers against what the pixel shader samples, targets, viewport, scissor and state enums; each finding has a named reason, a class (failure, fallback, warning, expected) and the draw's frame, pass and number
- **render**: draw `display.first_person_legs` in a vehicle's first-person seats
- **render**: cast the body's shadow under `display.first_person_legs`
- **render**: hide `display.first_person_legs` while a script animates the unit
- **render**: draw `display.first_person_legs` on flat views only, and `display.first_person_body` in stereo only
- **render**: count render-target lookups and allocations under `debug.gpu_stats` — a `render targets:` line every 60 frames gives `render_target_get`'s lookups a frame and the targets made since the game started (`render_targets_made`), to measure a cache in front of the lookup against
- **render**: switch FXAA on and off while the game runs — hold Back and press R1: `halo_anti_aliasing_toggle` takes FXAA (the separate pass) if it is off and turns it off otherwise, from the next frame, with "FXAA on" or "FXAA off" on screen for two seconds and a log line; not saved, so the next launch takes `display.anti_aliasing` again
- **render**: FXAA and SMAA over the scene before the HUD — upstream's `display.anti_aliasing` (OpenCE `94882796`, as adopted on `metal-render-options` in `6d0f0ed77` and `9f75a5209`), its two passes only: "fxaa" or "smaa", off by default, on both backends
- **display**: draw the HUD and menus at the display's resolution over a smaller scene — `display.native_hud` (default on) gives the back buffer a second target at the drawable's size (`HALO_STEREO_LAYER_NATIVE`) whenever `display.render_height`, the build's render height or `display.render_scale` makes the scene smaller; `render_window` marks each view's scene (`halo_render_scene_begin`, `halo_render_scene_end`, after the weapon's screen effects), the new `gpu_upscale` stretches it into that target with Present's filter, and the HUD, the flash, menus, letterbox bars, loading screens and movies are then drawn there by the game's own blends, with no depth buffer; Present copies the result
- **network**: Apple devices ask the router for a forwarded port with NAT-PMP, then PCP — `posix_upnp_forward_udp` was a stub on Apple devices ("UPnP isn't available on this platform"), since finding a UPnP router takes multicast, which an Apple app may send only with an entitlement Apple grants on request, so two Apple TVs behind strict NATs had no way through; `port/ios/host/host_natpmp.c` now asks the gateway of the default route (from the routing table, through `sysctl`) over unicast UDP on its port 5351 with NAT-PMP (RFC 6886) and, when the router does not answer that or says it speaks only PCP, with PCP's MAP (RFC 6887), each request sent again at 250, 500 and 1000 ms; a mapping is asked for an hour, renewed after half of the lifetime the router gave when that is shorter than `p2p.c`'s half hour (`posix_upnp_renew_seconds`), removed with a lifetime of 0, and the old internal port's removed when the tunnel's socket comes back on another one; `p2p.c` asks again at once after a suspension, removes the forwarding when hosting stops and no join is under way (UPnP's too), and logs how the router was asked (`posix_upnp_method`: "the router forwards ... (NAT-PMP)", "Internet play: PCP: the router refused the mapping (result 2: not authorized)"), still under `network.allow_upnp`; the packets are made and read in `host_natpmp_packets.c`, which `port/ios/tests/natpmp_probe.c`, run by `tools/ios_test.py`, checks byte for byte, with refusals, answers to other requests and result codes; against a stand-in gateway on the loopback both protocols mapped and removed (the lifetime it gave came back), and this network's router refuses port 5351, so a live mapping was not made
- **menus**: a menu's text field brings up the system's keyboard on Apple devices — nothing in the port started system text input, so on a phone, a tablet or an Apple TV the ENTER CODE screen, the password screen and Server Setup's name and password could not be typed into without a hardware keyboard; `platform_text_field` (`xinput_sdl.c`) now tells the host when a field is edited, with its title, text and whether it is a password's (`host_sdl_text_input`, a new import); on a phone, a tablet or a headset the host starts SDL's text input, whose keyboard SDL turns into key presses the field already reads; on an Apple TV, where SDL's hidden text field brings no keyboard up, the host asks with tvOS's own dialog (`host_tv.m`: the field's title, its text, Done and Cancel; choosing the field brings the full-screen keyboard up, and the keyboard's done finishes as the dialog's Done does), whose text the field takes whole (`host_sdl_text_result`, `platform_text_field_result`) before Start is pressed for it once, as Enter does, and Cancel stops the editing; a dialog UIKit cannot present yet (an earlier one still going away) is asked for again on the next poll; a field is let go of as its screen's gone only once other screens' functions have run a while without it, since a long frame such as a load's alone dropped the dialog; `debug.test_text_answer` answers the dialog for the simulator, whose keyboard cannot type into it; the ENTER CODE screen's help says "then JOIN GAME" rather than "press Enter"
- **menus**: a hosted internet game's lobby shows its join code where it said the invite link was copied — an Apple TV has no clipboard to paste a link from, so the lobby's panel now ends "Join code:" and the code on the line after it (after "Join code: " on one line the panel cut the code's last character off, and it has room for two lines below the countdown's); the invite link is still put on the clipboard where there is one, and Server Setup's row still shows the code
- **network**: a join that stops after its host is found says where — a tester joining by code stayed on "Found the game: connecting to its host..." while the host logged it connected, and nothing showed whether the tunnel was up or the game's search went unanswered: Direct Link now says "Reached the host: waiting for its game..." once the tunnel to the code's host is up (`P2P_CODE_REACHED`); each peer's first datagram from the game and first datagram to it are logged with their ports, as is the first one dropped and why (to a port that is not the game's, or with no stand-in for its port); Direct Link logs its games from internet play and the LAN's as they change; and a refused ENTER CODE entry logs its length; none of these lines prints an address
- **network**: join an internet game by an eight-character code — a hosted game's lobby and Server Setup show a join code (`ABCD-EFGH`: `p2p_code.c`, an alphabet without 0, O, 1, I or L, read in either case with spaces and dashes ignored), and Join Game > Direct Link's ENTER CODE screen takes the code or a whole invite link, for a player who cannot paste a link (an Apple TV); the host keeps a record at every signalling broker that retains messages, on a topic made from the code and holding the invite sealed with a key made from the code by Argon2id (`p2p_signal_code_publish`: retained, expiring after two minutes, published again every 30 seconds, cleared when hosting stops); a joiner subscribes to the code's topic, opens the record with the code's key and joins its invite as a pasted link does (`p2p_join_code`, also for a code handed over as a link), and is told after 20 seconds if no game has the code; the code goes with the invite, so going private or a new password gives a new one; `port/ios/tests/join_code_probe.c`, run by `tools/ios_test.py`, checks the code text
- **benchmark**: log when the consent screen asks, so a guided session can tell the tester it is up
- **benchmark**: a `--benchmark-upload` build signs with `aps-environment` — App Store Connect refused a tvOS build with CloudKit and no push environment; the Push Notifications capability is approved by the user for the benchmark's send only, with no push code, server or key
- **profiles**: estimated profiles for the Apple TV 4K (1st and 3rd generation), and a render height for a scene class — `AppleTV6,2` (A10X) plays at 720 with 4x filtering and the game's own model detail, `AppleTV14,1` (A15) at 1080 with 8x filtering, each with its menu at the display's height; every value is marked assumed, with the scaling from the Apple TV HD's and the iPad Pro's measurements it rests on, and both are provisional until a benchmark report replaces them
- **benchmark**: tester builds carry no recorded routes for now — `HALO_BENCHMARK_ROUTES` (`tools/ios_build.py --benchmark-routes`, off with `--tester`) leaves the recordings out of the bundle and the suite with the scenes that play by themselves, until recorded routes are shown to hold on other devices; `--archive-only` stops the App Store Connect packaging before the export that changes the developer account
- **benchmark**: the report names the device profile files in force and flags a drop-in profile — `host_device_profile` hands the resolved profile to `host_benchmark.m`. Not yet built
- **benchmark**: a still screen between runs — it names the next run and how long the suite has been going, Back there stops the suite (its journal keeps what is done), and a warm device's wait before the first run is shown on it. Not yet built
- **benchmark**: `tools/replay_check.py` — records a take with the scripted test input on the Mac runner and replays it through the suite at two frame rates, with and without its `.seeds`, to show whether a recording holds its route; the suite takes drop-in routes from `benchmark/local-routes` in the data folder, and `tools/mac_run.py` collects automatic takes. Not yet run
- **benchmark**: suite replays load a recording's `.seeds`, and the app bundles them
- **recording**: a take records both random seeds at every tick — `NAME.seeds` beside `NAME.input` and `NAME.actions` holds each recorded tick's segment, tick and the game's global and local random seeds as the tick began, and a replay of a recording that has one sets both before each tick's action; the other two files keep their form, so earlier takes read as they did
- **cloud-saves**: key a saved game by the map file's checksum and by `HALO_PORT_SAVED_GAME_VERSION` as well — everything the game requires before loading one, plus a number raised by hand when a change gives the saved contents another meaning without changing a size
- **cloud-saves**: sync saves through the player's private iCloud database, in builds made with `tools/ios_build.py --cloud-saves` — off by default, and without the option the app has no CloudKit code, entitlement or Settings rows
- **cloud-saves**: add the sync rules for cloud saves behind a store interface — `port/runtime/cloud_saves.c` decides per profile and per saved game whether to upload, download or settle a conflict, from the local hash, the cloud's and the base in a state file
- **benchmark**: a gameplay scene's measurements leave out the frames of a cutscene inside it — the window closes while `halo_scene_class` says cutscene, since a cutscene has its own target and a profile may hold it to 30
- **benchmark**: suite replays read a take's `.route` (its seeds and its skipped opening), and `debug.benchmark_suite = "quick:SCENE,SCENE"` runs only the named scenes, for trying a route
- **tvos**: the disc image upload keeps only the maps — once the image's directories have arrived (`xiso_plan`) the Apple TV writes just the parts an import reads and frees what it already stored, and the extraction gives each map's room back as it copies it out (`xiso_extract_maps_consuming`), so an import needs 3 GB free whatever the image's size; a 3.7 GB image was refused for wanting 5.9 GB
- **recording**: a take's `.route` counts its pauses and names its players once the game has made them
- **recording**: automatic takes — `debug.input_record = "auto"` records every level from its load to `recordings/take-<map>-<UTC time>` with a `.route` file naming the map, difficulty, first tick, random seed, players, build and the profile settings in force, and closes the take when another map loads, the level starts over or the menu returns
- **profiles**: `status = "below target"` — a profile derived from a report whose best settings still missed the frame-rate target says so
- **render**: a frame rate for a scene class, and a frame log — a device profile's `[scene.cutscene.display] frame_rate = "30"` holds each frame up for the refreshes that give that rate (`gpu_frame_context`, `pacing_schedule`), and `debug.frame_log` appends frames a second, percentile frame times, late frames, the render size, the scene class, the map, memory and thermal state to `frame-log.txt` every 5 seconds
- **profiles**: the first device profiles, their template and guide, and `tools/profile_check.py` — class profiles for Apple TV, Apple Vision Pro, iPhone and iPad, and Mac; model profiles for the Apple TV HD, the M2 Vision Pro, the iPhone 17 Pro, the 12.9-inch iPad Pro (M1) and M2 Max, M4 and M6 Macs, each value with its basis, reason and source
- **render**: scene classes for the menu and cutscenes — `halo_scene_class` names each frame the main menu, a cutscene or gameplay, and `display.render_scale` takes the device profile's value for that class at the frame's boundary
- **host**: identify the device and resolve its profile — `host_device.m` reads the model identifier, a Mac's chip, the GPU family, memory and display; `host_profile.c` picks the class profile by platform and GPU family and the model profile by identifier or chip from the app's `Resources/profiles`, lays `device-profile.toml` from the data folder over them, and `host_main.m` hands the result to the game
- **settings**: a device value under `config.toml`, and `config.origins` to tell a chosen value from a written default — `port_config.c` reads the device's profile from `HALO_DEVICE_PROFILE_VALUES` as layers between the built-in default and the file, records for each setting whether the file's value is one the game wrote or one somebody chose, and rewrites a written default's line when the device value changes, so a changed default or profile reaches an install that never chose the setting
- **benchmark**: bundle the suite's recordings — `port/benchmark/recordings` holds `a10-bench` and `c40-bench` (controller input and per-tick player actions only, every line checked against the recorder's two formats), the app carries them as resources, and `host_benchmark_start` copies them into the data folder where `benchmark_suite.c` reads them
- **benchmark**: the device benchmark's metrics, plan and journal — `port/runtime/benchmark_metrics.c` turns each frame's interval, CPU and GPU time and once-a-second memory and thermal samples into a run's summary; `benchmark_plan.c` names the next run from the outcomes so far (60 in play, 30 in cutscenes, 120 only measured), so a suite cut off by a crash or a memory kill carries on from `benchmark_journal.c`'s append-only file; `benchmark_probe.c` walks made-up devices and cuts the journal at every byte and after every write
- **tvos**: render at 540 by default on an A8 — an Apple TV HD draws open scenes at 12 to 18 frames a second at 1080
- **tvos**: `--extended-virtual-addressing` for tvOS builds — an Apple TV HD places the 4 GB guest arena only with the entitlement
- **tvos**: default to Metal, log memory, and report the address space when the arena is refused
- **network**: network version 29 — Custom Edition maps keep their own names (OpenCE `3e0ba5511`)
- **stereo**: let each eye count its own visibility tests, behind `display.stereo_flare_counting` — stereo's eyes are two passes that give a light's test the same number, so the second eye's test replaced the first's and both eyes read whichever came last; with the setting `visibility_slot` splits the slots in two halves, one an eye, and each eye's lens flare follows the share of the light that eye sees
- **metal**: count the samples a visibility test passes — the Metal backend asked only whether any sample passed (`MTLVisibilityResultModeBoolean`, `GPU_OCCLUSION_ANY_SAMPLE`), and `D3DDevice_GetVisibilityTestResult` then reported the whole test as visible, so a light mostly hidden behind geometry drew its lens flare at full strength where the Xbox dims it by the share of the light that shows; it now counts (`MTLVisibilityResultModeCounting`, `GPU_OCCLUSION_EXACT`) on every GPU that can, from the A9's family (`MTLGPUFamilyApple3`) and on every Mac, and an A8 keeps the yes-or-no answer
- **pacing**: add a frame pacer apart from the renderer — `host_frame_pacer.h` decides when a frame may start toward the display (`display.frames_in_flight`, 1 to 3 or "system"), how long it must stay up (`display.frame_spacing`, through `presentDrawable:afterMinimumDuration:`, only for frames that wait for the display, so never with `display.frame_rate` "unlimited" or a cap above the display's rate), and when the game's 60 Hz blank falls (`gpu_vertical_blank_wait`, which `vertical_blank_thread` in `d3d8_device.c` now follows instead of a timer of its own that drifts against the refresh); `gpu_metal_present` calls `frame_pacer_frame_begin`, `frame_pacer_present` and `frame_pacer_presented` and holds no policy for them
- **mac**: add `display.frame_rate`, a cap or no cap on the Mac app's frames — the Metal backend always waited for the display's refresh in `nextDrawable`, so the Mac app could not draw faster than the display or be held under it
- **network**: refuse a map download there is no room for — when the host's offer comes, `download_offered` in `map_share.c` asks `GetDiskFreeSpaceExA` for the free space where `custom_maps` is and fails the download, with the megabytes needed and free in the message the main menu shows, unless the offered files' sizes plus 256 MB (`DOWNLOAD_FREE_SPACE_MARGIN`) are free; a part file already there is not counted for, and a file system that does not answer is not held against the download; this port's addition, not upstream's
- **ios**: log the network version at startup — `host_main.m` writes `network version N` (`HALO_PORT_NETWORK_VERSION`) to `ios-runtime.log` before it enters the guest, so a session script can confirm that every device runs the same version before the first game; machines of different versions cannot play together and the menus do not show the number
- **network**: port upstream's network version 26 browser interoperability — import OpenCE `6dc423fdb`'s signaling, ICE-lite, SCTP and DTLS transport, keeping DTLS and Mbed TLS in the Apple host and reaching them from the ILP32 guest through generated POSIX pointer bridges; the Apple renderer and platform layer are unchanged, and the browser app and the Android and Windows build changes are outside this port
- **tools**: add `tools/site_og_image.py`, which draws the site's Open Graph card — the app icon beside the VISR wordmark in the site's font and dark colors, sized from `docs/site/style.css`'s `.brand` rules, so `docs/site/og.png` can be redrawn when the icon or the wordmark changes
- **stereo**: place each HUD piece in front of the nearest thing under it — a compute pass reduces the eyes' depth under each piece's footprint, skipping the weapon and the body by their stencil values, and the piece eases nearer in diopters at the same angular size
- **stereo**: mark the first-person body in the stencil, as the weapon pass marks the weapon
- **stereo**: make the HUD's distance a setting, `display.hud_distance`, every piece keeping its angular size
- **stereo**: log a seat's yaw bounds in the seat log
- **stereo**: keep foveated head-tracked stereo at render quality 0.6, the highest that holds 90 Hz in the headset sweep
- **stereo**: spike an immersive cutscene with the frame's outside blurred, behind a debug setting
