- **shader-lists**: the menu's, a10's, b30's, c40's, d40's and bloodgulch's lists — recorded on the native Mac runner from runs of the menu, a10 (the opening and the `a10-bench` route), b30, c40, d40 and bloodgulch (slayer) with `--record-shader-lists`, merged with `tools/shader_lists.py`
- **d3d8**: compile each map's shader list at load and record what it missed — `halo_shader_list_warm`, called from `main_new_map`, reads the map's list from the app (`port/shader-lists/MAP.txt`, bundled as `shader-lists/` by CMake) and compiles its vertex shaders, pixel shaders and pipelines before the first frame; a `vs` line warms every program object whose own declaration mask (0 for immediate mode) is the line's and a `pipeline` line every object compiled with its mask, so no shader is ever translated for another declaration, and damaged hex skips the line; a shader compiled at load dumps under `debug.gpu_dump_shaders` at its first draw, so dumps match a run without lists; anything still made while drawing is logged and, under MSL, appended once to `shader-lists-missed/MAP.txt` in the data folder (`debug.shader_list_record`, `""` for none; `mac_run.py run --record-shader-lists`), which `tools/shader_lists.py merge` folds into `port/shader-lists` (`tools/test_shader_lists.py`); settings `debug.shader_list_warm` (on) and `debug.metal_pipeline_archive` (off)
- **metal**: compile listed shaders and pipelines in parallel while a map loads — new `gpu.h` operations (`warm_list_read`, `warm_begin`, `pipeline_warm`, `warm_end`, `pipeline_built_take`; GL stubs, guest and host plumbing): between `warm_begin` and `warm_end` the Metal backend compiles each shader library synchronously on a worker thread (not in Metal's completion handler, whose queue `newFunctionWithName:constantValues:` would wait on), and `warm_end` makes the listed pipelines' vertex specializations, exact-border pixel variants and pipelines in parallel, keeping the dictionaries on the game thread; pipelines built by draws outside warming are queued for the front end to record; an optional `MTLBinaryArchive` in the app's caches (opened after the device, reads under a shared `pthread_rwlock`, adds and writes under the exclusive lock) stays off by default (`GPU_INITIALIZE_NO_PIPELINE_ARCHIVE`) because `serializeToURL:` fails in the Mac app with "cannot create temporary file", and a failed write logs the temporary folders, `HOME`, the working folder, the folder's writability and the open files
- **metal**: `debug.metal_specialize` turns vertex specialization off — `vertex_function` then returns each vertex shader's unspecialized function, the fallback for a shader that fails to specialize, which until now had compiled but never drawn (round 2 review, I2)
- **metal**: time shader work done while drawing under `debug.gl_debug` — each frame that compiled a shader library, specialized a vertex function or made a pipeline logs how many and their total and longest time, and every 600 frames the running totals; the game thread waits for all three, so these are the candidates for hitches
- **metal**: count pipeline changes under `debug.gl_debug` — every 600 frames the log gives the pipeline changes a frame and how many of them kept the same vertex and pixel shaders, to see whether merging pipelines could save any
- **ios**: label the import button "Choose XISO" instead of "Choose Halo XISO" — the product-facing button no longer carries the mark, the text above it still says which game's disc to pick, and `README.md`, `port/ios/README.md` and `docs/site/index.html` follow; the `chooseXISO` accessibility identifier is unchanged
- **icon**: make `visor-helmet-gold` the app icon — `DEFAULT_CONCEPT` in `tools/visr_icon.py` moves from `reticle` to `visor-helmet-gold`, and `tools/visr_icon.py --catalogs` re-renders `Assets.xcassets`, `Assets-tvOS.xcassets` and `Assets-visionOS.xcassets` from it (byte-identical to the `icon-gold-preview` renders)
- **ios**: open `halo://join` links in the app and join them through internet play
- **ios**: name the Mac Catalyst app VISR — `CFBundleDisplayName` and `CFBundleName` in `Info-macOS.plist`, which `tools/ios_build.py --mac` builds from and which arrived after the rename
- **render**: add `halo_render_before_hud`, a hook between a view's world and its HUD — `render_window` calls it after the fog and lens flares and before `interface_draw_screen`, with the view's player, rasterizer target and viewport bounds, where pfista/halo-og runs its FXAA; it does nothing yet
- **metal**: sample the game's exact border colors — a stage whose BORDER addressing has a color Metal's samplers lack (the motion sensor's sweep `0x46000000`, the loading bar `0x05050505`) samples a second time with an opaque white border, and the pixel shader rebuilds the color as `black + color * (white - black)`, after pfista/halo-og's native Metal renderer
- **mac_run**: make the native Mac runner the default
- **mac_run**: build and run the Catalyst app under `HALO_MAC_BUILD`
- **mac_run**: add `--fresh-config` so the iPad runner's `config.toml` can start from the defaults
- **mac_run**: `compare-inputs` checks that two runs were given the same guest, display, GL, audio device and settings
- **ios**: hold off App Nap for a native Mac runner's run, and skip the landscape request under Catalyst
- **mac_run**: `run --runner native` runs the Mac Catalyst app with `open`, without Xcode
- **ios**: log the guest's SHA-256, its display and the audio device, and refuse a malformed display pin
- **ios**: Catalyst data folder, background hold and a pinned display for the runner
- **metal**: list one frame's passes and blits under `debug.gl_debug` — every 600 frames `gpu_metal_present` logs each render pass's targets, sizes, load and store actions and draw count, each blit, and the present pass, in the order the GPU runs them, and the blit and present encoders get labels, so a GPU trace's encoders can be matched with what they do
- **tools**: add icon concepts that look out through the visor from inside the helmet — `inside_projection` puts the traced Halo: CE visor outline on a glass that wraps around the eye and projects it from inside, `APERTURE_HALF` simplifies that into chamfered runs with a brow notch and a chin piece, and `tools/visr_icon.py` draws five concepts on it: `inside-frame`, `inside-hud`, `inside-ladders`, `inside-contact` and `inside-dusk`
- **tools**: add icon concepts traced from the Master Chief's visor in the game — `tools/visr_trace.py` segments the visor, the helmet and the brow pads' lit rims in the `c40-02820` cutscene frame, levels and mirrors them, and fits paths, and `tools/visr_icon.py` embeds those paths and draws four concepts from them: `visor-traced`, `visor-helmet`, `visor-helmet-gold` and `visor-closeup`
- **ios**: replace the Master Chief icon with an original VISR reticle — `tools/visr_icon.py` draws the icon as SVG and renders every size into `Assets.xcassets`, `Assets-tvOS.xcassets` and `Assets-visionOS.xcassets`, and the Master Chief source art `port/ios/Icon-Artwork.png` is deleted
- **ios**: rename the app to VISR — `CFBundleDisplayName` and `CFBundleName` in the iOS, visionOS and tvOS `Info.plist` files, the import screens' titles in `host_import.m` and `host_tv_import.m`, the Apple TV upload page, the fatal-error dialog in `host_main.m`, and the app name in `README.md` and `port/ios/README.md`
- **display**: mirror reflections at full resolution as an option
- **visionos**: size the theater screen by angle, and a dark environment
- **metal**: draw reversed-Z
- **input**: a round stick dead zone of any size
- **visionos**: show the game on a screen in the room (theater mode)
- **replay**: end recordings with Back and Start, and skip paused input
- **ios**: let ProMotion iPhones run at 120 Hz
- **replay**: record and replay each tick's player action
- **debug**: change the render scale while the game runs — with `debug.render_scale_dpad`, holding Back and pressing the D-pad's left or right steps `display.render_scale` down or up by 0.05 (0.25 to 1.0), which the next frame draws at, as a window resize does; the new scale shows on screen for two seconds through the frame's debug pass, and the game doesn't see the D-pad meanwhile
- **metal**: log render passes and their memory traffic — every 600 frames, how many render passes a frame has and how many megabytes their attachments load and store, which on a tile-based GPU is most of a pass's cost beyond its draws; `a10` at 2732x2048 has about 3 passes a frame, loading about 40 MB and storing about 90 MB
- **metal**: render below the display's size and scale up with MetalFX — `display.render_scale` (0.25 to 1.0, default 1.0) renders that fraction of the display's pixels each way, and `display.upscaler = "metalfx"` scales the picture back up with MetalFX's spatial scaler (edge-aware, with sharpening) into a texture the present pass draws 1:1, instead of the present quad's bilinear stretch (still the default); the scaler is made again when either size changes; MetalFX is weak-linked and left out of simulator builds, which have none; the HUD and menus are upscaled with the scene
- **debug**: record and replay controller 1, and benchmark a replay — `debug.input_record` writes the controller's state at each change, stamped with the game time, to `<name>.input` in the data folder; `debug.input_replay` plays one back in place of the controller at the same moments of the game, so a replay at 45 frames a second and one at 90 press the same buttons; with `debug.benchmark` the replay's frames are timed, a report (frame-time percentiles, frames over each refresh budget, every frame's time) goes to `benchmark-<name>-<time>.txt`, and the game quits when the replay ends; `tools/mac_run.py` resets all three between runs
- **render**: `display.direct_camera` on iOS and visionOS too — upstream made it desktop-only, but the controller turns the player's facing every frame there as well, so on foot the view follows the stick the frame it moves instead of blending between snapshots a tick behind
- **metal**: pace frames to whole display refreshes, and blend each frame for when it shows — `gpu_present` now returns how long until the next frame is due; the Metal backend presents each drawable to stay up a whole number of refreshes (`presentDrawable:afterMinimumDuration:`), raising the number when frames stay up longer than asked and lowering it when frames fit in fewer, and `render_interpolation.c` advances the blend by how far each frame's lead time is from its average, so the world moves the same amount between refreshes; `display.frame_pacing` picks `"tick"` (rates that are multiples of 30 frames a second, the default), `"refresh"` (any whole number of refreshes) or `"off"`; the log says every 600 frames how many refreshes frames actually stayed on screen; GL and the fixed timestep are unchanged
- **renderer**: `debug.frame_trace` logs each frame's real time, the blend between ticks and the camera's yaw against the two ticks it blends, to see how smoothly the view turns
- **visionos**: render at the window's native resolution — the visionOS build's default render height is 0 (native), 2556x1440 at the window's default size instead of 1080 lines, and follows the window when it's resized
- **visionos**: a visionOS target — `ios_build.py --visionos` builds the app for Apple Vision Pro in a window, with Metal as its only renderer, `host_tv.m`'s controller input, the Files importer, a layered icon and entitlements for memory; tvOS's asset preparation moves into its importer
- **render**: draw the shadow maps and the camouflage source larger — `display.shadow_map_size` (128 as on the Xbox to 2048, default 512) sizes the 128x128 R5G6B5 targets characters' and vehicles' shadows are drawn into, and `display.effect_resolution` (default on) draws the 320x240 active-camouflage source at the screen's scale; their users address them in normalized or logical coordinates, so only the pixel count changes; `tools/mac_run.py` pins the Xbox values
- **render**: `display.anisotropic_filtering` filters the game's textures anisotropically, 1 (as on the Xbox) to 16 (the default) — every linear, mipmapped stage gets it, since the game never asks for it; point-sampled and unmipmapped stages (the HUD, text, render targets) stay as they are; `tools/mac_run.py` pins 1 so GL and Metal runs still compare against the Xbox-faithful records
- **ios**: the frame counter names the renderer — `Metal` or `GL` before the frame number, so a phone screenshot or a glance says which backend drew it
- **ios**: `debug.fixed_timestep_paced` holds each fixed-timestep frame for at least 1/30 s, so the game plays at its own speed while frame N stays the same moment in every run; the frame counter's log line carries the real time since frame 0
- **ios**: `debug.frame_counter` shows the frame number and game time in a corner of the screen — a UIKit label the host updates from `gpu_present` under either renderer, so a moment can be named and found again (with `debug.fixed_timestep` the same frame is the same moment in every run); screenshots and `gpu_stats` are unchanged
- **ios**: Metal visibility tests — tests count in Metal's boolean mode, one result entry per render pass a test reaches, resolved into each slot's answer when the command buffer completes; an answer still in the open command buffer is committed and waited for, as GL's run gets it
- **ios**: Metal draws — MSL shaders compile at runtime with safe math and invariance, pipelines, depth-stencil states and samplers are cached, the vertex shader pulls its attributes, constants are bound whole per draw, and fans and line loops become index lists
- **ios**: the Metal backend's resources and frames — textures, buffers and streams keep GL's write order by renaming what the GPU still reads, three frames are in flight, clears become load actions or quads, and readback and Present work
- **renderer**: the shader translators emit MSL — `nv2a_msl.c` frames the unchanged GLSL bodies with a prelude, Metal signatures, vertex pulling and an output struct; the replay and the dumps speak MSL, and `tools/shader_fixture_keys.py` adds 49 keys for the paths no recorded scene reaches; GLSL output unchanged
- **ios**: `display.renderer` selects GL or Metal at launch — with `"metal"` the window is a Metal window with no GL context, SDL's Metal view goes in before the touch overlay, and the host dispatches to `gpu_backend_metal`, for now a stub that presents a fixed color
- **ios**: run the GPU backend in the host — the guest calls 20 `host_gpu_*` imports in place of ~99 GL imports, `gpu_gl.c` compiles into the iOS and tvOS host with `GPU_GL_HOST`, and the `host_gl_*` services become its own uncounted GL calls; output and GL calls unchanged
- **ios**: the bridge generator maps `host_gpu_x` imports to the host's `gpu_x` and rejects host imports with more than eight integer or pointer arguments
- **mac-runner**: `compare --ignore-gl-calls` — compares `gpu_stats` lines without their GL call totals, for changes that move GL calls but not what is drawn
- **port**: with `debug.fixed_timestep`, end each frame only once the game's worker threads are idle — loads (the UI map and `a10` decompress on every launch) now take the same number of frames in every run, so screenshots repeat; off by default
- **tools**: turn `debug.fixed_timestep` off in `mac_run.py` runs that don't ask for it, like the other debug settings
- **port**: add `debug.fixed_timestep`, a virtual game clock — `GetTickCount`, `QueryPerformanceCounter` and game code's `time()` advance 1/30 s per presented frame, and `debug.exit_after` counts frames, so two runs show the same game time on the same frame; off by default, `source/` untouched
- **port**: add the arithmetic of a virtual game clock — `halo_virtual_clock.h` turns a presented-frame count into a performance counter, milliseconds and Unix time at 30 frames per second, for `debug.fixed_timestep`; a native probe in `ios_test.py` checks the rate, remainders and 32-bit range
- **renderer**: record shader inputs and replay them — `debug.gpu_dump_shaders` also writes each vertex program (`.vsh`) and pixel shader key (`.key`), and `debug.gpu_shader_replay` translates a folder of them at startup, so translator changes can be compared byte for byte
- **renderer**: count GL calls per frame on the `debug.gpu_stats` line — every `gl*` alias in `gl.h` increments `halo_gl_call_count`, so refactors that add redundant GL calls without changing pixels show up
- **ios**: keep stderr in `Documents/stderr.log` when that file exists at launch — lets tools read `platform_log` and `debug.gpu_stats`, which otherwise reach only a debugger's console; installs without the file behave as before
- **tools**: run the iPad build on a Mac with `tools/mac_run.py` — wraps the CMake-built `HaloCE` in an `xcodegen` project that Xcode installs and launches without a debugger, writes the run's `config.toml` and `init.txt` into the app's container, waits for `debug.exit_after`, and copies the logs, screenshots and shader files out
- **tools**: add the Mac runner's config, container and comparison helpers — `merge_config` and `reset_settings` write a run's `config.toml`, `find_container` locates the app's container, and `compare` diffs two runs' shaders, screenshots and `gpu_stats` lines
- **tvos**: add the app icon and top shelf images
- **tvos**: import the player's XISO from a browser on the local network
- **tvos**: package Apple TV IPAs with the user's own maps
- **tvos**: drive player one with the Siri Remote
- **tvos**: build the app for Apple TV with bundled maps

### fix

- **ios**: declare iPad orientations required for distribution
- **metal**: warm multiplayer maps at load — join loads now select the map shader list, including captured `chillout` shaders
- **replay**: make simulated mouths benchmark-only — `debug.benchmark_lip_sync` preserves original gameplay lip sync and scopes replay opt-in
- **data-packs**: retain snapshots after direct level launches — `main_loop` signals completed scene rendering before presentation
- **external**: build with SDKs before iOS 27 — `UISceneAccessory` and `UISceneAccessoryRegistration` exist only in the iOS 27 SDK, so the CI's iOS 26 SDK did not compile `host_external.m`; the accessory registration and its availability check are now compiled only when `__IPHONE_OS_VERSION_MAX_ALLOWED` is 270000 or more, and an app built with an earlier SDK leaves iOS 27 mirroring
- **ios**: build SDL without its camera subsystem — App Store Connect rejected the iOS build for a missing `NSCameraUsageDescription` (ITMS-90683) because SDL's CoreMedia camera driver references `AVCaptureDevice`, and VISR never opens a camera. Set `SDL_CAMERA` off in `port/ios/CMakeLists.txt`
- **external**: keep the chosen output mode when the display changes its mode on its own at connect — on a 4K display behind an EDID spoofer the system put the screen back in its preferred 3840x2160 just after the scene connected: a forced 1280x720 was logged as refused although the display then ran at 1280x720, and a run that found the screen already at 1920x1080 accepted it and was left drawing 1920x1080 on a 3840x2160 screen. The negotiation now waits the whole second before judging a request and asks once more if the screen went to another mode meanwhile, `host_external_poll` chooses again (at most twice per connection) when the screen leaves the accepted mode, and a refused forced mode falls back to the profile's choice instead of leaving the display black
- **external**: replace SDL's scene configuration method only once per implementation — if one of `SDLUIKitSceneDelegate` and `SDLUIKitDelegate` ever inherits `application:configurationForConnectingSceneSession:options:` from the other, `install_configuration` would have saved its own replacement as SDL's original and recursed on every non-external role
- **external**: a reconnect, an early disconnect and the Mac window no longer switch outputs wrongly — a new connection that starts negotiating clears a pending switch (`begin_modes`), so the game no longer moves to the new display before its mode is chosen and then again after it; a display that goes away before the game moved to it leaves the renderer alone and logs that instead of restarting pacing on the device and logging a return; a switch the renderer refuses is logged once (`output_switch`) rather than retried silently; and the Mac app's window on a second monitor is not counted as an external output (`screen_is_external`), so it takes no external profile values and reports `output.kind` as built-in
- **loading-screen**: the tail waits for six ticks, not three — after three, b30's first game frame showed the cutscene's first shot fully lit for one frame before its fade from black took hold; with six no frame between the loading screen's end and the fade is lit (Mac runner, every other frame)
- **loading-screen**: the progress's time share runs from the loaded level, not from the screen's start — a level that took a second to read spent the whole minimum while the picture stood still, so the band jumped from a tenth to four fifths in the first frames after the load; `halo_loading_screen_progress` now spreads the minimum's remainder after the load (at least `RUN_MILLISECONDS`) over the compiles' range
- **render**: hold a flat screen black at a campaign level's start until its opening script has had its ticks — after the loading screen's fade the level's first frames showed the player's start (a10's cryo room, a30's beach) for about three frames before the cutscene took the view. This takes `278cb0193`'s `halo_stereo_level_start_hold` and `halo_level_first_tick_pending` (on the headset branches) and runs the same hold when `display.stereo` is off (`halo_level_start_hold_flat`, a campaign level only, three ticks, at most 300 frames, the stereo path unchanged). `D3DDevice_Present` clears the picture to black for those frames instead of drawing a flash over it, because the flash's pipeline isn't compiled yet in a level's first frames and an async-shader draw whose pipeline isn't ready is skipped. Mac runner frames at the start of a10 and a30 (`tools/mac_run.py`, every frame): black from the loading screen's fade to the cutscene's own first shot, no player-start frame. `tools/ios_test.py` and the tvOS, iOS and visionOS builds pass
- **replay**: a unit's mouth moves with its speech once a tick, not with the mixer, while a take is recorded or replayed — `game_sound_set_mouth_aperture` set `unit.mouth_aperture` from the playing channel's mouth data at the device's pace, and a replay plays no sound, so it stayed shut there; the unit's mouth function moves its jaw in its node matrices, which the game reads markers from, so a take recorded with sound and its replay computed different node matrices from tick 893 of a b30 run (seen by hashing every object's nodes each tick) and left the take's path at the first fight. While `input_replay_sim_isolated`, the mixer leaves the mouths alone and `unit_dialogue_update` moves each one toward the mouth data of its speech's first permutation at the ticks since the speech began, by at most 0.3 a tick as the mixer did, closing when the speech ends (`unit_mouth_aperture_from_speech`). Takes of b30 on legendary recorded on the Mac, live in real time and at a fixed timestep, each driven by an Apple TV take's controller states, replay with no seed miss; without the change every such take left its path within 1,000 to 1,700 ticks
- **replay**: the scripts' action tests take what a tick's action carries, not what the frames' input set, while a take is recorded or replayed — `player_control_action_test_check_reset_input_blob` set `action_flags` (look up, look down, move, jump, accept and the rest, which a10's tutorial waits on) from each frame's input, and the frames come as the display and the device's load have them, so live play and a replay's controller set them at different ticks, and the replayed tick actions never set them at all. A frame's action tests now go to `player_control_frame_action_flags`; `update_server_next_update` puts them in the next tick's action (its unused `pad`, which `update_client_dequeue` now copies), a take records them with the action and a replay hands them back, and `update_client_dequeue` notes them where the action is taken (`player_control_action_test_note_tick`). A take recorded on the Mac in real time, its controller driven by the a10 take's input, replays at a fixed timestep with no seed miss in all its 36,011 ticks
- **replay**: a tick that follows no tick (a level's first, the first after a revert) starts the local random sequence from the global one too — `input_replay_tick_begin` did so only for the tick after the last one, so the first tick after a checkpoint revert began from the local seed the frames had left while the dead player's timer ran, as many numbers as frames came (a replay of the 2026-10-15 a10 take at two frames a tick began the tick after its revert from local seed `8c9bbb54`, one at a frame a tick from `0bff6851`, with the global seed `b70f1620` in both); the frames never draw from the global sequence, so it stays as the last tick, the level or the revert left it
- **replay**: while a take is recorded or replayed, a checkpoint, a BSP switch, a skipped cinematic, the revert after a death and the next map come between the same two ticks whatever the frame rate — the main loop did them between frames, and `main_save_map_private` counted a checkpoint's waits in frames: one frame a tick on the Xbox, but on the native ports a frame runs no tick, one or several (`game_time_update`), so at 60 Hz the waits ran out twice as fast and what a tick asked for waited for the frame's later ticks. Replays of the 2026-10-15 a10 take at a frame a tick and at two frames a tick saved the checkpoint before the player's death at ticks 14480 and 14430, and went on from different checkpoints. `main_tick_end`, after each tick in `game_time_update` while `input_replay_sim_isolated` (single player only), takes the checkpoint's next step and says whether the main loop has work before the next tick; `game_time_update` then runs no more ticks in that frame (the ones held back are dropped from the clock, a tick or two at a checkpoint), and the main loop no longer steps the checkpoint itself. Every replay of the take, at a frame a tick, at two, and at uneven frames of 1/120 to 3/40 s, now saves at 14480, and those with uneven frames have the same objects as one at a frame a tick at all 35,400 ticks
- **replay**: scripts read the scripted camera's transition time (`camera_time`) in ticks, not in the frames the director drew — `scripted_camera_update` counts `camera_script_globals.timer` down by each frame's seconds, so a cutscene script that waits on `camera_time` went on at a tick that followed the frame rate: one frame a tick on the Xbox, two at 60 Hz, a late frame's longer step in live play. Replays of the 2026-10-15 a10 take with a fixed timestep of one frame a tick and of two parted at tick 2476, where the captain began his next animation a tick apart, and every run with a frame pattern other than one frame a tick parted there, as a real-time replay did. `scripted_camera_time` now returns `camera_script_ticks`, set with the timer and counted down once per tick by `scripted_camera_tick` at the end of `game_tick` while the camera is the script's; the camera's own motion still follows the frames. With it, a replay whose frames come at an uneven 30 a second (steps of 1/30, 1/40, 1/24, 1/60 and 1/20 s) has the same objects at every one of the take's 35,000 ticks as one with a frame per tick
- **replay**: a replay plays no sound and a unit speaks a scripted sound whether or not the mixer started it, while a take is recorded or replayed — two runs of the b30 take parted when a sound the cache had not yet loaded was not started, because `scripted_sound_new` told a unit to speak only when `sound_new_impulse` returned a sound, so the unit's speech and the AI's followed the cache's timing (`input_replay_sim_isolated`); a replay's `sound_is_active` is false (`input_replay_sound_silent`), which also drops the stepped mixer of the last commit (`dsound_sdl.c` is as it was), and the sounds draw from `sound_random_seed`, their own sequence, not the local one the simulation draws from; the b30 take replays the same route and fight in 5 of 5 full-length runs, 5 of 5 through the suite and 8 of 8 on a busy machine
- **replay**: a replay of a take is the same every time — the random seeds an enemy chooses from came from the frames drawn since the last tick and the recording's seeds were set only after `ai_update` had chosen, so `game_tick` now notes both seeds when a tick ends and puts them back when the next begins (`input_replay_tick_end`, `input_replay_tick_begin`; the local sequence starts again from the global one), and a replay's mixer is stepped one tick of frames at the end of each tick by `dsound_replay_step` and mixes nothing for the device or the silent clock, because the sounds a mixer has finished at the real rate free channels and start the units' scripted speech, so two replays heard different sounds finish at different ticks; the route log also gives the player's body and shield, the AI's actors and the dead objects at each tick, and counts the ticks whose global seed was not the take's, apart from the local one
- **render**: pass `frame_start` through the render validator and the render capture — the late start added `frame_start` to `gpu.h`'s `GPU_OPERATIONS`, which `gpu_validate.c`, `gpu_capture.c` and the two probes' mock backends build their tables from, so a `HALO_RENDER_VALIDATION` build and `tools/ios_test.py` did not compile; `validate_frame_start` and `capture_frame_start` hand it to the backend (it touches no GPU state, so nothing is checked or captured) and the mocks do nothing
- **pacing**: time the late look by the port's frame clock — `SDL_GetTicksNS` is not among the guest's SDL imports, so the previous commit did not link on any target; `look_read` in `xinput_sdl.c` now uses `halo_frame_trace_milliseconds`, and `halo_look_read_age` gives the age of the game's read whether or not there is a controller, so the log's "late look" line reports it on every run
- **p2p**: watch the invite's host's slot while `joins[0]` is active — `main`'s map sharing replaced `signalling.joining` with the `joins` table, so the Direct Link join stages' `_topic_join_slot` subscription now tests the invite's own join, and the per-join fields the port carried over into `signalling` are dropped (each `struct join` has its own)
- **ios**: the game's window is key again when theater mode closes — `window_hidden(NO)` makes SDL's window the key window unless an alert or share sheet is still up, since one put away while the game's window was hidden could not give the key window back and left none
- **ios**: an invite's jump from Join Game's Server Browser stops its browsing — `invite_jump` calls `lobby_browser_end` before switching the mode to Direct Link, which reuses the screen, so `lobby_browser` no longer keeps running behind Direct Link's list
- **ios**: a link opened while internet play is on but could not start is taken and explained instead of silently ignored — `p2p_initialize` marks `start_failed` when its socket or thread cannot start (`p2p_start_failed`), and `platform_link_consent` then takes `join_link.txt` and prints a `console_notice` ("Internet play could not start, so VISR cannot join the invite"), as it now also does when a consent Join's start fails; that failure path clears `join_requested`, so a later start does not run a join the player was told was dropped
- **ios**: tell the player when an invite's host never answers — where `update_joining` gives up after `JOIN_TIMEOUT` it sets `join_failed`, which the main thread takes like `invite_taken` (`p2p_take_join_failed`, under `p2p_lock`); `menu_join_failed` clears `invite_pending`, so Direct Link's empty list stops saying "Connecting to the host..." and shows its PASTE LINK line again, and prints a `console_notice` ("Could not reach the host"); a new join clears an earlier failure; only Apple builds take the mark
- **ios**: a link opened in a network game waits for the game to end instead of asking at once — `platform_link_consent` leaves `join_link.txt` where it is while `menu_in_network_game` (a server, or a client past searching, as `invite_waits` tests) says a game or its lobby is up, and prints one `console_notice` per waiting link ("Invite received: VISR will ask about it after this game"); the host keeps writing newer links over the file, so the newest is the one asked about once the game is over, and a LAN host returning through a tapped link no longer freezes its game for everyone behind a modal question
- **ios**: alerts and share sheets take the key window, keep the game's landscape lock and survive theater mode opening — `host_present_window` makes its window key and `host_dismiss_window` (now used by `host_alert` too) gives the key window back, so tvOS's focus reaches OK and Cancel and an iPad's Return reaches `preferredAction`; the window's root controller, the alert (`HostAlert`) and the sheet (`HostSheet`) allow only landscape and keep the scene's orientation lock, which a plain `UIViewController` unlocked while one was up; `host_theater.m`'s `window_hidden` leaves windows of `host_present_window`'s alone (`host_is_present_window`), so the immersive space opening during a cold-launch alert no longer hides it and closing it never shows a put-away one
- **ios**: keep the player's input from the game while the share sheet is up, and fit the lobby's invite lines — `host_share.m` keeps the sheet's window as the one "a sheet is up" state (`host_window_up`): `posix_share_link` offers no second sheet while it is set, and `host_sdl.c` holds back key, pointer, touch and gamepad presses and movements (the releases still pass) and reads every gamepad as at rest; `host_dismiss_window` clears it when the sheet is chosen or canceled, when its scene goes to the background (the sheet put away first), or when UIKit never presented it
- **ios**: show message boxes in theater mode — `host_alert` presents each from a window of its own at the alert level on the game's scene, which theater mode does not hide, instead of from the game's hidden window where it would wait unseen; `host_sdl_show_simple_message_box` and `host_sdl_show_message_box` go through it and fall back on SDL's box only with no window scene or off the main thread, and the first button stays the preferred one as in SDL's box
- **ios**: draw the console's notices for the player on Apple builds — `interface_draw_fullscreen_overlays` skips `terminal_draw` under `HALO_IOS` to keep developer output and counters off the picture, which also hid the in-game invite notice and the host's away, back, kicked and banned notices; the new `console_notice` prints those as `console_warning` does and marks their lines, and Apple builds now call `terminal_port_draw_notices`, which draws only the marked lines (no prompt, no other console output, no framerate counter), so Linux, Windows and Android draw exactly as before
- **ios**: a link answered Join never replaces a newer one the host wrote during the question — `platform_link_consent` now asks for the join (`p2p_join_invite_starting`, which skips `p2p_join_invite`'s "internet play is off" line) before `xnet_start_internet_play` starts p2p, so `poll_invite_file`'s first pass always comes after it and the newest link wins; the `fopen` check that raced that pass is gone, and the menus jump only once internet play runs; `xnet_start_internet_play` returns at once when p2p already runs (INTERNET PLAY turned on twice in one run); the consent check tests its one-second wait before the settings lookup; the README says Apple builds host internet games PRIVATE by default
- **ios**: the local network usage string names the app VISR — `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` in `Info.plist`, `Info-visionOS.plist` and `Info-macOS.plist` began with "Halo", while the alert, Settings > Local Network and the LAN list's text all call the app VISR
- **ios**: Bonjour starts again after a DNS-SD error and never lists this machine under a new name — an error other than `kDNSServiceErr_PolicyDenied` in `browse_reply` or `register_reply` (`ServiceNotRunning` or `DefunctConnection` after mDNSResponder restarts) only logged, leaving dead references while `running` stayed 1, so a host's discovery stayed down for the listening socket's life; it now stops and starts again in 2 seconds without counting a denial
- **net**: `lan_listeners` is touched only under `lan_listeners_lock` — the game's connect thread closes a failed connection's socket while the main thread listens and broadcasts, so `halo_ws_closesocket`'s remove-and-count, `halo_ws_listen`'s add and `halo_ws_sendto`'s hosting read now hold the lock, with `posix_lan_discovery` called outside it
- **ios**: the native Mac runner sends real broadcasts again — `broadcast_allowed` in `host_bonjour.m` took only `isiOSAppOnMac` for a Mac, which is false under Mac Catalyst, so once `xnet.c` used `posix_lan_status` the runner dropped 255.255.255.255 and, with no `network.broadcast` list, sent nothing on the LAN; it now accepts `isMacCatalystApp` too, as `host_lifecycle.m` does
- **tools**: reset the network addresses and internet play between Mac runner runs — two-instance runs would leave `network.address`, `network.broadcast` and `network.online` in `config.toml`; a test keeps `DEFAULTS` to known settings and the cherry-picked phase3-stereo keys
- **bink**: draw a movie's panel at the display's rate in a head-tracked view — `bink_playback_update__internal` blocked in `BinkWait` until the movie's next frame was due, so the game presented 30 times a second during a movie, and the panel, which is placed against the head with each presented frame, followed the head in steps. In HEAD mode and on the theater's screen (`halo_stereo_head_tracked_view`) it now waits once, and a frame that isn't due draws the last decoded one again; the movie's end waits for its last frame as before. A flat display plays as the original does. The log gains the frames the game drew during a movie and how long it ran
- **build**: remove a stale FFmpeg license from the app bundle when building with `--bink off`
- **render**: keep a seated rider's shifted knees out of the dash — at 0.2 world units the Warthog passenger's raised right knee stood in the dash's side panel in the Mac runner's captures; `FIRST_PERSON_LEGS_SEAT_SHIFT_MAXIMUM` is now 0.12 (0.37 m), where the thighs come into the bottom of the view and nothing meets the dash
- **tools**: give `DEFAULTS` in `tools/mac_run.py` a `display.anti_aliasing` of `off` — `tools/ios_test.py` requires the runner to set every setting a device profile may set, and the new key was missing
- **benchmark**: count frames under OpenGL ES — iPhones and iPads draw with GL unless a profile asks for Metal, and only `gpu_metal.m` reported frames, so every run on a phone recorded "0 frames"
- **benchmark**: support touch on mobile report screens
- **rasterizer**: split screen goes without the cinematic blur instead of halting — `rasterizer_screen_effect_get_cinematic_parameters` ended the game with a `FATAL_ERROR` when a convolution effect was on while `main_get_window_count` was above 1, which a co-op campaign start on `a10` reached right after its cutscene was skipped
- **tvos**: Menu or B over the text dialog is taken by the dialog, so it can never reach the system — with `controllerUserInteractionEnabled` on, a controller's B arrives at UIKit as a Menu press, and a Menu press nothing handles goes to the system, which leaves the app for the Home Screen; the dialog now has a Menu press recognizer that puts the keyboard away if one is up and otherwise cancels as Cancel does (`HaloTextPromptMenu` in `host_tv.m`); an SDL event watch also logs any quit, terminate, close or background event that arrives while the dialog is up, since SDL's UIKit code sends none of its own and a quit can only come from a `SIGTERM` or `SIGINT` or the window closing
- **build**: a feed URL may not hold `;` or `,` — `;` is CMake's list separator, so such a URL reached `Info.plist` mangled
- **benchmark**: a stray file in `local-routes` no longer stops the benchmark from starting — `host_benchmark_snapshot_create` refused a source folder holding anything but route files, so a `.DS_Store`, a README or an empty `.checks` made `host_benchmark_start` fail; source folders now skip links, folders, empty files and names that are not route files, while the staged snapshot itself still holds route files only
- **packs**: bound what an unsigned feed can make the app hold, and recover the store instead of disabling it — the envelope is parsed before its signature is checked, so a feed host without the key could make every client hold several copies of a 128 MiB download; recording snapshots are now at most 4 MiB a file and 12 MiB in all (`HOST_RECORDING_PACK_MAX_FILE_BYTES`, `HOST_RECORDING_PACK_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES`, matched in `data_pack.py`), the download at most 24 MiB, and `data_pack.py` refuses to write an envelope the app would refuse; a missing or unusable `state.json` (Caches can be purged file by file) removes every cached generation and starts over as a fresh install instead of turning updates off for good; a failed state write no longer marks the state invalid for the rest of the launch, which made the next launch quarantine a good trial pack; and a broken later publish uses up only its own sequence, keeping a valid pack already staged
- **render**: the menus and HUD draw again when the scene is smaller than the display — `render_target_get`'s recent-targets cache (`0c47de307`) answered every back-buffer lookup with the scene's target, so with `display.native_hud` active the draws outside a view's scene (menus, HUD, letterbox) went into the scene's target instead of the display-sized one and the stretch overwrote them; the cache key now includes whether the back buffer's color is the display-sized target (`native`), and a miss looks up that layer. `main` has the same bug wherever the scene renders below the display (an Apple TV HD, a render scale below 1.0), reproduced in the tvOS simulator at `display.render_height` 720: no menu before, menu after
- **tvos**: a controller works tvOS's text dialog, and the game no longer reads it meanwhile — SDL's tvOS view controller is a `GCEventViewController` whose `controllerUserInteractionEnabled` stays off unless `SDL_HINT_APPLE_TV_CONTROLLER_UI_EVENTS` is "1", so a controller's input never reached UIKit's focus engine and the ENTER CODE dialog could not be reached, while the game went on reading the same controller and moved the menu behind it; `host_tv_text_prompt` now sets the hint to "1" while the dialog is up (SDL's hint callback applies it at once) and puts the old value back when the dialog is gone, however it went (checked each poll by `host_tv_text_prompt_tick`); `host_ios_gamepad_axis`, `host_ios_gamepad_button` and the remote's key filter report nothing meanwhile and until the hardware controller's buttons are let go, so the press that closes the dialog does not reach the menu; the text field becomes first responder once presented, so the keyboard comes up and a Bluetooth keyboard types into it; Menu or B cancels through the dialog's Cancel action as UIKit does
- **recording**: the Windows build names an automatic take with `gmtime_s` — `take_open` in `input_replay.c` called `gmtime_r`, which the Windows C library does not have, so `ninja windows` stopped at an undeclared function
- **benchmark**: the suite loads each scene at the scene's own difficulty — `benchmark_scenario_load` set it with `game_difficulty_level_set`, which changes only the game in progress, and the map load then took `global_difficulty_level` (normal unless the menu changed it), so every recorded route replayed on normal: on a10 that runs the look tutorial the heroic and legendary takes never saw, and the player stood in the cryo tube for the whole replay; it now calls `main_set_difficulty`, as the console's `game_difficulty_set` does, and `a10-crew` leaves the tube and follows `a10-bench` to the bridge in two runs out of two on the native Mac runner
- **network**: a hosted game no longer ends when its Apple device's app comes back from the background — the game is held at its next frame while the app is in the background and the system then suspends it, but its clock went on, so on the return every timer saw the whole absence at once: a hosted game's own client timed out its connection to the server it had not heard from for the minute and a half the app was away ("timeout in network_connection_idle") and the game ended; the game's clock (`GetTickCount` and `QueryPerformanceCounter`, `xbox_kernel.c`) now stands still while the app is in the background, by the time the host counts there (`posix_background_milliseconds`, `host_lifecycle.m`), and internet play keeps the real clock (`platform_real_milliseconds`, `p2p_now`), by which it sees the stop; `posix_app_in_background` says whether the app is there now (0 on the desktop and Windows); the same trip (hosting an internet game, Settings brought to the front for 90 seconds) now goes on with the game after the return; the system took back none of the game's sockets in either trip
- **ios**: the game's sockets are made again when the app comes back from the background — iOS and tvOS take back a suspended app's sockets, which then fail every send and receive, and nothing but internet play's own tunnel socket was opened again, so the game's sockets and internet play's stand-ins stayed dead after a trip to the home screen; the Apple host now keeps how each socket the guest makes was made (`host_socket_revival.c`: its kind, the address it is bound to or that a first send gave it, whether it listens or is connected, the options set on it) and, on entering the foreground, puts a new socket made the same way in place of each one taken back, under the same descriptor (`dup2`, so the descriptor is never free in between), bound to the same port where that is free, listening ones with them; a connected TCP socket's connection is gone and is left for its owner to see fail; each is logged, as is a foreground where none was taken back; `debug.test_reclaim_sockets` does it to every socket and breaks every TCP connection, for the simulator, whose apps keep their sockets; `port/ios/tests/socket_revival_probe.c`, run by `tools/ios_test.py`, checks on a Mac with force that a UDP socket keeps its descriptor, port, options and non-blocking mode and takes datagrams again, that a listening socket keeps its port and takes connections, and that a connection is left alone; not yet seen on a device
- **render**: the validator and the capture wrap the `gpu.h` operations added beside them — `native-hud` added `upscale`, `anti_alias_prepare` and `anti_alias`, the device profiles `frame_context` and `loading-screen` `compiles_outstanding`, and both wrappers install a function for every operation in `GPU_FUNCTIONS`, so a `--render-validation on` build no longer compiled once the branches met; the validator checks that the stretch's and the anti-aliasing pass's pictures are live color targets and passes the rest straight through, and the capture passes all five through, logging once that a window holding a stretch or an anti-aliasing pass replays without it
- **loading**: end the loading screen when its load is done — the screen's hold waited for the drawn progress to reach its end, but the wobble in `progress_bar_render` kept a finished load's progress as much as 0.05 short of it for seconds at a time, so an 8 s minimum stayed up 11.5 s with the picture dimly lit. A progress of 1, which the Xbox's own loop never drew, now settles without the wobble, and `halo_loading_screen_finish` gives the end at most half a second after the load is done
- **loading**: start the loading screen from the last frame under `display.async_shaders` — a pipeline's first draw is skipped while it compiles, and the screen's own three pipelines were first drawn in its first frames, so the copy of the last frame and the blur of it were the draws skipped and the screen started from black. `progress_bar_start_frame` now makes the screen's draws once into a target nobody sees, waits for the compiles they start, and then copies the last frame; `progress_bar_keep_frame` no longer clears a newly made kept frame in place of the copy, and `progress_bar_render` keeps the wobbled progress at 0 or above, where the color's `pow` of a negative sine was not a number
- **render**: a capture regenerates mipmaps that were generated before its window — the high-res HUD and text upload one level and call `gpu_texture_generate_mipmaps` for the rest, so a replay of the menu sampled textures with one level of five (the validator's `texture-incomplete-mips`, and black text under OpenGL ES); the capture layer now remembers the level a texture's mipmaps came from and writes the call after the texture's uploads; `render_replay.py info --records` lists a file's records by kind
- **render**: validation no longer reports Metal's border colors as missing — the Metal backend reports no `border_clamp` so that its capabilities line reads as OpenGL ES's, and samples borders where the GPU has them, so the validator's check now applies to OpenGL ES alone and `gpu_metal.m` reports the fallback itself on a GPU without border colors; a draw that tests depth in a color target smaller than its depth target is now named as such (the test reads the depth target's top left corner)
- **render**: the validator passes `gpu_vertical_blank_wait` straight through — main's new call comes from the front end's vertical blank thread, so the wrapper touches none of the validator's state
- **stereo**: read the first-person body's dead test from `damage_flags`
- **cloud-saves**: add the iCloud keys to the entitlements the target already signs with, not only to `HALO_ENTITLEMENTS` — the block read the base keys from `HALO_ENTITLEMENTS`, which only visionOS sets, so a tvOS build with `--extended-virtual-addressing` (from `tvos-first-run`, which sets `XCODE_ATTRIBUTE_CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS` directly) and `--cloud-saves` would sign without the extended virtual addressing entitlement, and an Apple TV HD cannot place the guest arena without it; the block now reads the target's own `XCODE_ATTRIBUTE_CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS`, so any platform's entitlements set before it are carried over whichever branch reaches `main` first
- **network**: internet play works again after an Apple device's app comes back from the background — iOS and tvOS take back a suspended app's sockets, and `network_resumed` asked the STUN servers again through the same dead tunnel socket, so nothing went out: a tester's Apple TV, back after 50 seconds at the home screen, logged no public address, offered none, and never reached the host it joined by code ("could not connect"); it now opens the tunnel's socket again on the same port where that is free (`tunnel_open`, also used by `p2p_initialize`), and the first failed send in a row is logged with its error (`tunnel_sendto`, for every send of the tunnel's socket); checked with a joiner in an iPhone simulator stopped for 50 seconds before it was given a join code: the stop was seen, STUN answered and the pair played 203 ticks with both players
- **ios**: a host no longer takes an internet play joiner's datagrams for its own machine's — `posix_socket_recvfrom` reads with `recvmsg` (since upstream's "Harden and fix the netcode"), which the Apple host's adapter in `port/ios/host/posix_net.c` did not wrap as it wraps `recvfrom`, `accept4`, `getsockname` and `getpeername`, so a datagram's source address came back in Darwin's layout (a length byte, then the family) and its family read 0x0210; `peer_incoming_address` in `xnet.c` then took it for no IPv4 address and rewrote nothing, a datagram from a peer's stand-in on the loopback reached the game as 127.0.0.1's, `network_game_server_get_client_machine_at_address` credited it to the host's own machine, the joiner was never heard in a game (`client machine 1 timed out` after `NETWORK_GAME_SERVER_CLIENT_TIMEOUT`, 15 seconds) and its player never moved on the host; system link was unaffected because a LAN datagram's address needs no rewriting, and `network.address`'s rewrite of incoming datagrams was skipped the same way; `ios_recvmsg` converts the address, and the new `port/ios/tests/posix_net_probe.c`, run by `tools/ios_test.py`, checks the family and port of every address the adapter returns
- **shaders**: a run that dumps its shaders does not warm the lists — check-metal compares the shaders its Metal and GL runs dumped, and the a10 and ui lists now carry shaders recorded in played Apple TV sessions, which the check's scenes never draw, so the Metal dump held about 80 shaders the GL run had no reason to make
- **benchmark**: the report reads the screen through helpers that build on visionOS — `UIScreen` does not exist there, so the visionOS builds failed; a visionOS report gives 90 Hz and no screen size
- **pacing**: the frame pacer spaces and judges frames by a scene class's rate — under a cutscene's 30 it counted every frame late (599 of 600 at two refreshes each, on an Apple TV HD) and spaced frames for 60; `host_frame_rate_target` gives it the scene class's rate when one is in force, else the Mac app's cap
- **build**: a device build after an App Store archive removes the link the archive left in place of the app — the next build failed with "unable to create directory" for `VISR.app`
- **render**: `halo_scene_class` leaves the game time alone while a level is being replaced — `game_precache_new_map` presents a frame after the old game's time is disposed of, `game_time_get` asserted there, and the game ended at every level change; the class stays what it was until the next level's time runs
- **replay**: a replay's own cinematic skip begins the next segment even when the game time does not step back — a take recorded after its level's opening was skipped never started on the Mac runner: the skip came on the level's first tick, the revert landed on that same tick, and the player stood in the cryo tube for the whole replay; also room for a scene list in the suite's launch variable, which was cut at 47 characters
- **tvos**: the App Store icon is found — its entry in `AppIcon.brandassets` had the idiom `tv-marketing` where the asset catalog expects `tv`, so App Store Connect reported the icon missing; the layers also gain 2x images scaled from the 1x ones
- **build**: a build without `--benchmark-upload` turns the option off — `cloud_definitions` passed nothing for `HALO_BENCHMARK_UPLOAD`, so CMake's cache kept an earlier ON and the app stayed signed with the iCloud entitlement, which App Store Connect rejects on tvOS without `aps-environment`
- **build**: an App Store archive holds the app — CMake's targets skip the install step, so `xcodebuild archive` made a generic archive that `-exportArchive` refused ("expected one {} but found app-store-connect"); the `VISR` target alone now sets `SKIP_INSTALL=NO` and an install path
- **benchmark**: a run is judged by what the display got and the CPU's margin, not by the GPU time — on an Apple TV HD the GPU figure read 29.5 ms at the 95th percentile for a menu whose every frame was shown for one refresh, so it spans more than a frame's own work; it stays in the report
- **benchmark**: the result summary says what a run's ending means and counts a cutscene's frames against 30 — the first report screen on an Apple TV read "b30-beach: killed" and "0% of frames on time" for a cutscene at 29 frames a second
- **benchmark**: baseline runs leave the render size to the device's profile — the suite took the profile's size from its first frame, which is the menu's, and forced gameplay and cutscenes to it (1080 on an Apple TV HD whose profile plays at 540); a baseline run now sets no size, the sweep starts from the profile's gameplay value, and a run records the size it drew at. Not yet built
- **render**: `halo_scene_class` leaves the game time alone while a level is being replaced — a frame presented from `game_precache_new_map` asked `game_time_get` with the old game's time disposed of, the assertion at `game_time.c` line 463 halted, and the game ended at a level change (seen on an Apple TV HD going from a10 to b30)
- **benchmark**: the consent screen names both IDs iCloud records with a report — a read-back of a sent record showed a device ID in its metadata beside the creator's user ID
- **replay**: a replay's own cinematic skip begins the next segment even when the game time does not step back — a take recorded after its level's opening was skipped never started on the Mac runner: the skip came on the level's first tick, the revert landed on that same tick, and the player stood in the cryo tube for the whole replay; also room for a scene list in the suite's launch variable, which was cut at 47 characters
- **runner**: a native run past its time limit is killed when it ignores TERM — `end_native` in `tools/mac_run.py` waits 15 s after TERM and then sends KILL, so a game spinning in its own code cannot hold a host's line; also `--app-env` for the app's own environment, and `HALO_BENCHMARK_SEND=send` for a developer's unattended test of the report upload (ignored by tester builds)
- **benchmark**: a run's refresh rate falls back to the screen's own where the presenter reports no refresh period — the Mac runner's runs said 0.0 Hz, so nothing could hold a target
- **benchmark**: the pause between runs sleeps where the thread's run loop has nothing to wait on, in place of spinning
- **recording**: a replay takes the recording's seed at the tick it was noted — a take that began at tick 1 was replayed from a revert that landed on tick 0, and the seed set there was a tick's random numbers away from the recording's by tick 1, so a Legendary take parted from its route within a minute
- **shaders**: warm a device's own record only under Metal, and only a record of a sane size — under GL a record left in the data folder by a Metal run made `halo_shader_list_warm` compile shaders at load that GL compiles as it draws, which a GL run compared with its records must not do
- **render**: a scene class's frame rate waits for the display's refreshes — a wait timed from the last Present gave 35 frames a second for 30 on an Apple TV HD, so the Present now waits until the display link has called for a frame's refreshes since the one before
- **render**: a scene class's frame rate holds, and a cutscene needs the script's camera — on an Apple TV HD `presentDrawable:afterMinimumDuration:` alone let a cutscene run at 60 while asked for 30, so a Present under a rate now also waits until the frame before it is that many refreshes old; and a cinematic in which the player looks around (a10's cryo pod) is gameplay, not a cutscene
- **metal**: leave the presented-time handler out of simulator builds — the tvOS simulator's drawables have no `addPresentedHandler:` or `presentedTime`, so the tvOS simulator app has not compiled since the frame pacer was merged
- **metal**: take the texture size limit, relaxed math and the pipeline archive from the GPU family — an A8 (family Apple2) has an 8192 limit, no relaxed math and no binary archives
- **tvos**: declare the Bonjour service and stamp a build number in `Info-tvOS.plist` — an Apple TV could not browse `_halo-ce._udp`, and a constant `CFBundleVersion` made an install over the app keep the old binary
- **ios**: give every Apple app a Bluetooth purpose string — SDL's controller support links `CoreBluetooth` (`CBCentralManager`), so App Store Connect rejected the tvOS TestFlight upload's binary checks with ITMS-90683 for a missing `NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription`, which Apple requires whenever the code references the API, used or not; the string is added to `Info.plist`, `Info-tvOS.plist`, `Info-visionOS.plist` and `Info-macOS.plist`
- **pacing**: build for the iOS and tvOS simulators again — `gpu_metal_present` added a presented handler to the frame's drawable for the frame pacer, but those simulators' `MTLDrawable` declares neither `addPresentedHandler:` nor `presentedTime`, so both simulator builds stopped at `gpu_metal.m`; under `TARGET_OS_SIMULATOR` the pacer is now told the frame was shown as it is presented, with no time, which is what it already allows for (it holds no frames in flight in a simulator and takes the time of the call)
- **pacing**: time a shown frame by its notice when the system gives no time — on a virtual display a drawable's `presentedTime` is 0 for every frame, so `debug.present_times` logged them all as not shown and `frame_pacer_presented` counted no intervals; the time the presented handler ran now stands in, marked "noticed" in the log
- **network**: helping send maps is off by default on Apple builds — `network.map_seed` defaults to false under `HALO_IOS` (iOS, visionOS, tvOS and the Mac app; upstream's platforms keep true), so a player's device in another's game on a Custom Edition map opens no listening port (TCP 5153, which takes connections from any address) and uploads nothing unless the player turns HELP SEND MAPS on in Settings > Network Setup; a host's `network.map_share` is unchanged; `port/ios/tests/config_probe.c`, built a third time by `tools/ios_test.py` as an Apple build, checks that the file a fresh install writes has `map_download = false` and `map_seed = false`; the settings table, `NETCODE.md` and `ROADMAP.md` say so
- **tvos**: include `halo_port_limits.h` on tvOS too — `host_main.m` logs `HALO_PORT_NETWORK_VERSION` on every platform, but the include sat inside the `#if !TARGET_OS_TV` block with `host_texture_settings.h`, so the tvOS device and simulator builds stopped at an undeclared identifier
- **metal**: report `base_vertex` only on a GPU that has base vertex drawing — the capability was on for every device, but `drawIndexedPrimitives:...baseVertex:baseInstance:` needs GPU family Apple3 (an A9) or a Mac, and the iOS and tvOS deployment targets admit an A8 (the Apple TV HD, family Apple2)
- **mac**: draw unsynchronized frames without waiting for a drawable — with `display.frame_rate = "unlimited"` the layer's `displaySyncEnabled` was off, but `gpu_metal_present` still blocked in `nextDrawable`, and the window server hands drawables out at its own pace: behind a locked screen the game stayed at 120 frames a second
- **network**: map downloads are off by default on Apple builds — `network.map_download` defaults to false under `HALO_IOS` (iOS, visionOS, tvOS and the Mac app; upstream's platforms keep true), because a download of up to about 2.3 GB starts without a question; joining a game on a Custom Edition map the device lacks then leaves the game as before, and `cache_files_map_present`'s message at the main menu now adds that downloads are off and that DOWNLOAD MAPS in Settings > Network Setup turns them on; the setting governs receiving only, and its description, `port/linux/README.md`, `NETCODE.md` and `ROADMAP.md` say that sending stays with `network.map_share` and `network.map_seed`
- **ios**: the route probe's own address reaches `connect` as Darwin's — `sockaddr_inward` in `port/ios/host/posix_net.c` read every address as the game's layout (a 16-bit family first), but `posix_local_ipv4_address` builds a native `sockaddr_in` (a length byte, then the family) and passes it through the same `connect` wrapper, so its family came out 0, the probe connected to nothing, and the machine's LAN address was always the first interface `getifaddrs` lists that is up, not the one the default route leaves from; an address whose second byte is set is now taken as Darwin's already, which no family the game uses can be mistaken for
- **metal**: pace a held window frame by a refresh, and log while draws keep skipping
- **metal**: compile shaders and pipelines off the game thread, skipping a draw until its pipeline is ready
- **ios**: name the Apple platforms' guest VISR on the halt screen and in the version line — the guest builds with __linux__, so upstream's native build label called it "OpenCE Linux | local build (local)"; it says "VISR | network version 23" instead
- **windows**: send no crash reports and never ask to — `crash_reports_enabled` is off, so a crash writes no minidump and starts no reporter or uploader, and a hand-run `--crash-report` or `--crash-upload` does nothing; the `crash_reports.upload` setting is gone, and `port/windows/README.md` and `port/linux/README.md` say so. A crash still writes its backtrace to debug.txt and the log, which `tools/symbolize_crash.py` reads

- **metal**: record and warm the HUD layer's separate alpha blend in shader lists

- **stereo**: release the cutscene's film when the camera becomes the player's own first-person camera with the look enabled — b30's Pelican bench is head-tracked gameplay after the Pelicans' film, through the film's settle and expansion

- **stereo**: keep a seat's held-back yaw through a second turn in the frame and while the director holds the facing
