- **textures**: upsample 1-texel axes in full and fail cleanly when a GPU buffer cannot be allocated
- **ios**: link `host_config.c` into the iPhone and iPad app — `host_texture_settings.m` now calls `host_config_write_boolean`, which only the visionOS app linked
- **ios**: refresh the upscaled textures' size in Settings when the app enters the background
- **textures**: tell an unreadable cache manifest from a missing one
- **textures**: keep the tag list on a failed `strdup` and report every map-reading failure
- **metal**: keep the pipeline-change counter's bookkeeping under `debug.gl_debug` — it stored the last draw's shaders on every draw, the only part of the counter that ran without the setting (round 2 review, M4)
- **metal**: say why a vertex function could not be made, for each shader — the unspecialized function was made with `error:nil`, so a failure logged "no entry point" whatever the cause, and a failed specialization was logged once for the whole run, so a second shader failing was silent (round 2 review, M1 and M5)
- **ios**: end a `config.toml` value read by the host at its comment — `host_config.c` kept a trailing `# ...`, so a hand-edited `online = true # on` read as off for join links while the game took it as on
- **mac**: open `halo://join` links that arrive while the Mac app runs — Mac Catalyst delivers them as GetURL Apple Events, which AppKit dispatches only when `NSApplication` looks at its event queue, and the game, SDL and the import screen pump the run loop with `CFRunLoopRunInMode` instead, so a link opened while the app ran was never opened; a run-loop timer in `host_main.m` now looks every 0.25 s with an empty event mask, which dispatches the waiting Apple Events and takes nothing else off the queue
- **tools**: have `mac_run.py`'s iPad runner copy the app's own executable — `app_executable` reads `CFBundleExecutable` from the app's `Info.plist`, so a `--app` built before the VISR rename (`HaloCE.app`) still runs
- **mac_run**: run `xcodegen` only when the runner project's inputs changed — it rewrote the open `HaloRunner.xcodeproj` before every run, which is what drew Xcode's "changed on disk" alert
- **mac_run**: tell apart why an `osascript` call to Xcode failed — every failure while closing the runner projects read "a dialog is probably open", including a denied Automation permission
- **mac_run**: check that the runner projects really closed before `xcodegen` rewrites one — `CLOSE_RUNNER_PROJECTS` closes each inside a `try`, so a close that failed went unnoticed and left the project open under the rewrite
- **mac_run**: detect an Xcode held by a modal alert from its main thread instead of failing a run still "not yet started" after `LAUNCH`'s two-minute poll — that check came late (up to three polls) and could fail a cold Xcode still building or indexing, and ask again over a pending run
- **mac_run**: close the runner projects in Xcode before `xcodegen` rewrites them — Xcode answered a rewrite of the open `HaloRunner.xcodeproj` with a modal "changed on disk" alert no one could click, so every later iPad-runner run failed with "Build operations are disabled: 'project.xcworkspace' has changed and is reloading" or sat at "not yet started" until the 300 s timeout (an M4 build host since 2026-10-05 16:46; an M6 build host on 2026-10-06, where Xcode then aborted in the same handler)
- **ios**: ship the Expat, Monocypher and zlib licenses in the app's `Licenses` folder — `tools/ios_build.py` copies `port/third_party/expat/COPYING`, `port/third_party/monocypher/LICENCE.md` and `port/third_party/zlib/LICENSE` beside the other notices, and `THIRD_PARTY.md` names them with stb_truetype and the fonts
- **ios**: ship internet play's `brokers.txt` — since OpenCE's `88a7c07e` the MQTT brokers come from `brokers.txt` beside `config.toml` (`network.brokers_file`), not from a setting, so the iOS and visionOS apps had no brokers, and so no internet play or server browser
- **nv2a**: read BUMPENVMAP_LUMINANCE's luminance from red, not blue — our loader puts L in red for both luminance formats, as xemu's `psh.c` reads it; blue held U
- **metal**: bind the exact border colors at fragment buffer 3, not 1 — `phase3-stereo` uses fragment buffers 1 and 2 for the rate map and the foveation sizes, so a foveated shader with exact borders would have declared buffer 1 twice
- **nv2a**: clamp PASSTHRU to [0, 1] again — the hardware clamps it (NV_texture_shader, nxdk `Pixel_shader/PassthruClamping`, xemu PR #3085), and the unclamped `t = xT` that `cc0a6faa` took from xemu's `psh.c` is a known xemu bug
- **nv2a**: take pfista/halo-og's combiner corrections that xemu's `psh.c` confirms — the LSB mux truncates `r0.a`'s byte, PASSTHRU is unclamped, a NONE stage reads as (0, 0, 0, 1), BUMPENVMAP reads the stage `PSINPUTTEXTURE` names and scales every channel by the unclamped luminance, the final combiner's unsigned identity is `max(x, 0.0)` with an unclamped V1R0 sum, MINUS1_TO_1_GL maps two's complement bytes, and alpha kill and color sign act only on stages that sample
- **mac_run**: send `open`'s `--stdout` and `--stderr` to the native data folder and copy them into `--out` afterward — `--out` sits on a removable volume and macOS's TCC denies launchd's helper access to it, so `open` failed with -10810 before the app started
- **mac_run**: seed a native data folder until its `seeded` marker exists, whether or not it has `maps/` — `run_native` only seeded a folder with no `maps/`, so a folder whose `maps/` came from an earlier Catalyst spike never got the throwaway a10 and menu runs and its first real a10 run precached a10
- **mac_run**: turn Metal validation warnings off in the native runner so `stderr.log` holds only errors
- **mac_run**: play a throwaway menu run after the a10 seed run in `seed_native_data`
- **mac_run**: unseed the data folder when `open` fails during the throwaway a10 run
- **gl**: take GL entry points from `OpenGLES.framework` under Catalyst
- **mac_run**: ask again when Xcode cancels a run — a project reload after `xcodegen` rewrote `HaloRunner.xcodeproj` cancelled the run before it launched, and `LAUNCH` counted `cancelled` as started, so the runner waited 300 s and failed
- **mac_run**: close other runner projects by path — `CLOSE_OTHERS` matched by name and missed one Xcode listed as `project.xcworkspace`
- **mac_run**: turn off Xcode's Metal API validation in the runner scheme
- **hs**: refuse a console tag name the scenario doesn't reference — `hs_parse_tag_reference` left the tag index at -1 and running the line halted the game in `cache_files.c`
- **ios**: ship the stb_truetype and font licenses in the app's `Licenses` folder — `tools/ios_build.py` now copies `port/third_party/stb/LICENSE`, `Overpass-OFL.txt`, `OpenCE-OFL.txt` and `Newtown-LICENSE.txt` beside the other notices
- **replay**: let go of the replay's buttons when the game time goes back
- **replay**: end on stopped game time only near the recording's last tick
- **tools**: reset the theater settings between runs
- **replay**: count the game time's jumps frame by frame to end a replay
- **tools**: reset the high-res HUD and text, theater and dead-zone settings between runs
- **replay**: end the last segment after its recorded length at most
- **metal**: sample BORDER addressing with border colors
- **metal**: measure GPU busy time and report medians
- **replay**: end at the last action, or when the game time stops for good
- **replay**: skip a cinematic once it can be, past the recording's skip
- **config**: keep the debug settings in one section
- **ios**: ask the display link for 120 Hz
- **replay**: end with the tick actions and keep presses between frames
- **metal**: report CPU and GPU frame times as averages
- **ios**: stamp a new build number on every iPhone build
- **replay**: carry the recorded time across checkpoint reverts
- **replay**: skip the main menu's map when recording and replaying
- **hs**: read an object name's index from the low half when making an object list — taken from halo-ce-universal's `709bfc3a`: `hs_object_name_to_object_list` took a `short` but is called through `hs_typecasting_procedure`, which passes a `long`, and Apple's arm64 ABI leaves narrow arguments for the caller to extend, so the callee saw a compiled constant's whole value (`0xFFFF006F`), found no object and returned an empty list; every script function given an object name got none, which stopped Two Betrayals' opening cutscene forever and left Pillar of Autumn's bridge crew standing instead of seated
- **metal**: frame pacing measures frames by the time between Presents — a drawable's presented handler never reports a time in a visionOS window or the simulators, so the pacer couldn't see a frame stay up too long; Present waits in `nextDrawable` for a drawable the display has let go of, so with frames held for whole refreshes, Presents come that many refreshes apart and a later one is a miss; the log's histogram of refreshes per frame now works everywhere, and the blend's lead is reckoned from the schedule; still off by default until it's tried on a Vision Pro
- **tools**: `mac_run.py` resets `display.render_scale` and `display.upscaler` between runs — a run that set them left the next runs rendering at that scale, since the runner's `config.toml` keeps what isn't reset
- **port**: `_snprintf` and `_vsnprintf` return -1 when the output doesn't fit, as MSVC's do — they were C's `snprintf` and `vsnprintf`, which return the length needed, so `terminal_printf`'s check that a line fit (`terminal.c`, line 413) halted the game on any message over 254 characters, where the Xbox truncated it; two minutes into Two Betrayals' opening cutscene one did; the output is still terminated
- **metal**: build for the simulators again — their drawables have no `addPresentedHandler:`, `presentedTime` or `presentDrawable:afterMinimumDuration:`, which frame pacing called, so simulator builds stopped compiling; there pacing now presents each frame plainly
- **metal**: frame pacing is off by default — in a visionOS window the drawables' presented handlers never report a time, so the pacer can't see a frame stay up too long: at best it sat at one refresh, the same as no pacing, and earlier it locked the game to 30 or 22 frames a second; `"tick"` and `"refresh"` stay as experiments, and `tools/mac_run.py` pins `"off"` so a runner's leftover setting can't pace a run
- **visionos**: the controller note fits a window narrower than itself — 520 points, or the window's width less a margin
- **tools**: `mac_run.py run --simulator` stops with an error when the game never starts, instead of reporting an empty result; tests pin the simulator process pattern and the missing-app check
- **visionos**: quit on window close from the start — the scene-disconnect observer was registered after the import setup screen, whose wait loop is when a player is most likely to close the window, and a scene reconnecting then would have re-entered `main`
- **ios**: pause the game in the background — while the scene is in the background the game's audio pauses and the game holds at its next Present, pumping the run loop until the scene returns, so iOS doesn't end the app for GPU work in the background and a closed visionOS window doesn't keep playing; an iPad app on a Mac keeps running
- **ios**: a Metal visibility answer waits for its own resolution — `gpu_metal_visibility_result` waited on `completed_serial`, which a later command buffer's completion handler can raise before the test's own handler has written the answer; it now waits until the slot's answer carries the test's serial
- **ios**: a missing OpenGL ES 3.0 entry point stops the host with its name — `gl_functions_load` leaves only the ES 3.2 `glCopyImageSubData` and `glDrawElementsBaseVertex` NULL for the capability probe and calls `host_fatal` for any other, as the GL bridge did before the backend moved into the host
- **renderer**: make unit 0 active before desktop `gpu_texture_read` reads an upload — `state_texture` returns early when the cache already has the texture on unit 0, leaving another unit active, and `glGetTexImage` reads the active unit's binding
- **renderer**: count GL calls through a function, `halo_gl_count_call` — with `++` inside `HALO_GL_COUNTED`, a counted GL call nested in another's arguments made two unsequenced increments of `halo_gl_call_count` (undefined behavior; clang's `-Wunsequenced` flagged one site); counts unchanged
- **tools**: fail the Mac runner's launch at once, with Xcode's reason, when all three run attempts fail — the script used to finish quietly and the runner then waited 300 s with the cause lost
- **tools**: retry when Xcode answers the Mac runner's run with "Cannot run" or is still loading the regenerated project — the first run after switching worktrees failed that way and then waited 300 s for a game that never started
- **tools**: drive this checkout's runner project, not another worktree's — Xcode keeps one project named `HaloRunner` open, so a second worktree's `open -a` did nothing and the launch script ran the first worktree's build; the runner now closes other runner projects and matches its own by full path
- **tools**: see a run that starts and quits between the Mac runner's polls — with `debug.fixed_timestep` a short run takes seconds, so the runner waited 300 s for a launch that had already finished; `prepare` now removes `ios-runtime.log` and a fresh one counts as started
- **tools**: fail the Mac runner's launch when the Designed-for-iPad destination never appears — the retry loop swallowed the error and `run doc` then ran on whatever destination Xcode had active; one last unguarded attempt now raises
- **tools**: wait for Xcode to open the runner project before driving it — when Xcode wasn't already showing the project, `open -a` returned first and the launch script failed with `Invalid index`; it now also retries until the Designed-for-iPad run destination is listed
- **tools**: reset rendering and debug settings to their defaults on every `mac_run.py run` — `--set` writes into the container's `config.toml`, so a value one run set (`display.interpolation`, `debug.gpu_debug_flat`, …) used to carry into every later run
- **tools**: report a truncated or empty screenshot in `mac_run.py compare` instead of crashing — a run killed at the timeout can leave a partial BMP, and `read_bmp` now rejects it so `compare` lists it as unreadable
- **tvos**: make the Siri Remote's Back button B, not Start
- **tvos**: read maps from the bundle instead of symlinking them
- **tvos**: enable SDL's OpenGL ES backend on tvOS

### perf

- **metal**: warm captured campaign shaders — expand the map lists with ordinary-play misses and their dependencies
- **packs**: check the data-pack feeds on every launch — the once-a-day limit on `checkForUpdateAtURL:` is gone, each launch starts one background check with a 10 second request timeout and no retry, a request carries the `ETag` saved with the last accepted envelope in `feed-validator.txt` as `If-None-Match` so an unchanged feed answers 304 without a download, and a network, HTTP or staging failure logs one line and leaves the current snapshot in use. `data_pack_probe` and `docs/data-packs.md` follow
- **render**: draw the decals that share a texture in one draw — every decal was its own quad-list draw after setting its color as a constant vertex attribute, 1,064 draws a frame after a10's mess hall fight in stereo, about 1.1 ms of the game thread on an M4; `_rasterizer_decals_draw` now gathers each run of decals with the same blend function and bitmap and `halo_draw_quad_ranges` (`d3d8_device.c`) draws the run as one indexed draw from the same vertex stream, with each decal's color given to its own vertices at the value the constant would have had
- **render**: draw a view's object shadows before applying any, and share them between the eyes — each shadow had the one shadow target and its blur, so the picture's render pass was ended and begun again around every shadow (91 passes a frame in a10's mess hall in stereo, 13 without shadows); now `render_object_shadows` generates each into a target pair of its own (`rasterizer_shadow_slot_select`, made as first needed and kept) and then applies all of them in one stretch, and a shadow the frame already generated for the same object and matrix (the other eye's, a mirror's) is applied from its targets rather than drawn again
- **gl**: move the framebuffer `framebuffer_get` finds to the front of its list, after upstream OpenCE's `197c1994` — each draw asks again for the framebuffer the draw before it had, which now sits first instead of wherever it was made
- **render**: answer `render_target_get` from the four targets it found last, after upstream OpenCE's `197c1994` — every draw and clear looks up its color and depth targets again (`draw_targets`), almost always the two the draw before it had; in a frame without stereo's eyes a surface's target follows from the surface's address, format and size, the screen's width and scale and the frame's eye count, so a hit on all of them returns the entry without `surface_dimensions`, the scale rules or the bucket walk. Stereo frames, whose layers and foveated sizes change between draws, take the full lookup as before, and `stereo_targets_release` forgets the recent targets before it frees any. `debug.gpu_stats`'s `render targets:` line counts the hits.
- **render**: FXAA as a pass over the scene again by default — inside the stretch it runs at every display pixel, four times the scene's at half scale, and on the Apple TV HD's b30 it cost 15 ms of GPU a frame (24 to 39 ms, 42 to 26 frames a second) against the separate pass's 6 ms (to 30 ms, 32 frames a second); the folded shader stays behind `debug.anti_aliasing_folded` (in place of `debug.anti_aliasing_separate`), off by default
- **render**: FXAA inside the stretch to the display-sized back buffer — with `display.native_hud` on, `gpu_upscale` takes an `anti_alias` argument and Metal draws its quad with `upscale_fxaa_fragment`, FXAA evaluated at each display pixel's place in the scene and the plain bilinear sample where there is no edge, so FXAA no longer costs a copy of the scene and a pass of its own
- **metal**: keep the HUD's pass in one piece after MetalFX too — when `display.upscaler = "metalfx"` scales the scene straight into the display-sized back buffer, `gpu_metal_upscale` now commits and opens the HUD's pass as it does after its own quad, so the motion tracker's pass runs ahead of it (`native_pass`) and the back buffer is loaded once a frame and stored once (37 MB and 38 MB a frame on b30 at 2732x2048, from 59 and 61)
- **shaders**: add a `b40` list from a hand-played run — recorded on an Apple TV HD on 2026-10-13 (b40 on Heroic from the level's start, 11 minutes), 2,287 lines, written by the session script with `tools/shader_lists.py`
- **shaders**: list what a hand-played b30 run and the menu still made while drawing — recorded on an Apple TV HD on 2026-10-13 (b30 on Heroic from the landing, a10 and a30 from earlier takes), merged with `tools/shader_lists.py` by the session script
- **shaders**: list the shaders an Apple TV HD session still made while drawing — 145 for `a10`, 32 for `b30` and 2 for `ui`, recorded during the hand-played session of 2026-10-13 (21:39) with build `20261013.205745` and merged by the session script
- **cloud-saves**: let the host skip a pass when no save changed — `cloud_local_signature` hashes the slots' file names, sizes and times, and the deletion journal
- **shaders**: list 365 more `a10` shaders from two further hand-played runs — recorded on an Apple TV HD while playing deeper into the level on Legendary, merged with `tools/shader_lists.py` (1,288 lines in all)
- **shaders**: warm what this device recorded as missing along with a map's list — `halo_shader_list_warm` appends `debug.shader_list_record`'s file for the map to the bundled list, so a shader made while drawing in one session is compiled at the level's load in the next without a new build, and a map that has no bundled list still gets one from play
- **shaders**: list the shaders a played a10 fight and the menu still made while drawing — 337 lines for `a10` and 11 for `ui`, recorded on an Apple TV HD during a hand-played Legendary run of the level's first fights, merged with `tools/shader_lists.py`; each had cost a hitch of about 100 ms the first time it was drawn
- **metal**: fast pixel math on an A8, and `HALO_METAL_PIXEL_MATH` names the mode for one run on any GPU
- **build**: add the host's profile, `pgo/visr_host.profdata` — recorded with `tools/host_pgo_train.py` on an Apple Silicon Mac from `main` at `4a6465f7d` (Xcode 27.2, Apple clang 21): the menu, a10 along a recorded route, b30, c40 along a recorded route, a10 in side-by-side stereo, and a10 on OpenGL ES. 182 KB; 1,219 functions ran and are in it, 8,341 did not and are left out. `port/ios/README.md` says what it is and when to record it again.
- **build**: profile-guided optimization for the host — the part Xcode builds (the app, `gpu_metal.m`, the OpenGL backend, the stereo code, SDL) was compiled without knowing which paths are hot. `tools/ios_build.py` takes `--host-pgo use|off|generate` (default `use`) and passes `HALO_HOST_PGO` and `HALO_HOST_PROFILE` to `port/ios/CMakeLists.txt`, which adds `-fprofile-instr-use` or `-fprofile-instr-generate` to the `VISR` and `SDL3-static` targets. `use` reads `pgo/visr_host.profdata` and builds without it, saying so, when the file is missing or this Xcode's `llvm-profdata` cannot read it.
- **build**: link-time optimize the Apple guest — the game code was compiled one file at a time, so a call into another file was never inlined; `tag_block_get_element_with_size` alone, a bounds-checked accessor called from everywhere, was 11.8% of the main thread's own time in a b30 profile. `tools/ios_build.py` now takes `--lto full|off` (default `full`) and passes it to `configure.py`.
- **build**: build the Apple guest with profile-guided optimization — `tools/ios_build.py` passed `--pgo=off` to `configure.py`, so the game code in every Apple app (Mac, iOS, visionOS, tvOS and the simulators, which share one guest image) was compiled without the profile in `pgo/halo_linux.profdata` that the Linux and Windows builds already use. It now takes `--pgo use|off`, default `use`.
- **metal**: draw indexed primitives with a base vertex — the Metal backend reported `base_vertex = 0` to match Apple's OpenGL ES and ignored `gpu_draw.base_vertex`, so `D3DDevice_DrawIndexedVertices` copied and rebased every draw's indices on the CPU
- **metal**: read vertex colors as BGRA — the Metal backend reported `vertex_bgra = 0` to match Apple's OpenGL ES, so the front end copied every colored vertex stream and swapped its D3DCOLOR bytes on the CPU each time it was drawn
- **stereo**: share each HUD piece's depth rectangle among 32 threadgroups
- **stereo**: keep the foveation resolve's output textures instead of making one per call
- **stereo**: shade only the zoom inset's central square and the blur's margin
- **stereo**: let the Compositor pace stereo frames instead of the Xbox's 60 Hz queue
- **d3d8**: remember the last four render targets `render_target_get` found — each draw asks for its color and depth targets again, and finding one cost a texture description (`surface_dimensions`) and a scale decision before the bucket walk; a hit now compares the surface's `Data`, `Format` and `Size` and the screen's width and scale, everything the entry depends on but the settings `offscreen_target_scale` reads once (port of upstream `197c1994`'s renderer cache; entries are never freed, so a remembered one stays valid)
- **metal**: hash front-cache keys a word at a time — `front_cache_index` ran FNV-1a a byte at a time over each draw's pipeline, depth-stencil and sampler keys, and the pipeline key is now 40 bytes
- **metal**: specialize each pipeline's vertex function for its attribute formats — `fetch_attribute` switched on each attribute's format, read from the attribute table, for every vertex
- **metal**: keep the draw path's object locals unretained — `gpu_metal_draw` and its `bind_*` helpers retained and released each record, texture, sampler, pipeline and depth-stencil state they looked up, about 3 percent of the game thread in a10 once the lookups were inlined
- **sdl**: poll events at most once every 2 ms — a frame polled at its Present and again when the next frame read the controller (`XInputGetState`), well under a millisecond later; on Apple platforms each poll runs the main run loop once, and on the Mac runner the two polls were 9 percent of the game thread in a10
- **msl**: read vertex attributes a word at a time where they are aligned — `fetch_attribute` assembled every float and short from single-byte loads (12 loads for a position), since the front end's offsets need not be aligned
- **metal**: ask for encoder execution status only under `debug.gl_debug` — every frame's command buffer asked for `MTLCommandBufferErrorOptionEncoderExecutionStatus`, a debugging aid for which the driver tracks every encoder; a failed command buffer is still logged with its error either way
- **metal**: write converted fans and loops straight into stream memory — `convert_primitive` built each list in an `NSMutableData` and then copied it into the frame's stream chunk
- **metal**: remember each stage's last sampler — `sampler_state` hashed its 32-byte key for each of a draw's 4 stages, 2 percent of the game thread in a10, while consecutive draws mostly sample the same way
- **metal**: look up handles with an inlined array read — `[MetalTable get:]` was a message and an autoreleased return for each of the 20 or more handles a draw looks up; with `objc_msgSend` and the retain and autorelease calls around it, that was 4 percent of the game thread in a10
- **d3d8**: reuse two scratch buffers for the index lists draws make — `quad_indices` and the rebased copy in `D3DDevice_DrawIndexedVertices` were a `malloc` and `free` for nearly every indexed draw under Metal and GL (neither has `base_vertex`), 2 percent of the game thread in a10
- **guest**: compare memory 8 bytes at a time — musl's `memcmp` compares a byte at a time, and the front end's per-draw state compares (the pixel shader key in `fragment_shader_get`, the uniform inputs in `prepare_draw`, about 600 bytes a draw) made it 6 percent of the game thread in a call-stack sample of a10
- **metal**: compile pixel shaders with relaxed math — the scene's pixel shaders are ALU-bound (Instruments' ALU limiter at 85% in the main pass), and under `MTLMathModeSafe` they took about twice ANGLE's GPU time for the same draws; `MTLMathModeRelaxed` allows reassociation, fused multiply-adds and reciprocal division but keeps infinities and NaNs
- **metal**: skip encoder calls that set what the encoder already holds
- **metal**: don't store the screen's depth and stencil at the end of a frame — passes now decide their depth-stencil store when they end, and the one Present ends discards it, since nothing reads the screen's depth after the frame: a10's stores fall from about 89 to 61 MB a frame at 2732x2048 (about 65 MB a frame less at the largest Vision Pro window) with the Mac check's frames unchanged
- **metal**: look up pipelines, depth states, samplers and handles without Objective-C on the common path — on Apple Vision Pro about a quarter of the main thread's CPU went to building an `NSData` key and hashing it into a dictionary for every draw's pipeline, depth state and samplers, and to `MetalTable get:`'s messages; a 256-entry direct-mapped cache of key bytes now sits in front of each dictionary (which keep every object for good, so the cache doesn't retain), and the handle table mirrors its records in a plain array; the Mac check's frames and `gpu_stats` are unchanged
- **metal**: keep the game's DXT textures compressed on the GPU — where the GPU has BC formats (`supportsBCTextureCompression`) the backend now reports `s3tc`, so the front end uploads DXT1, DXT3 and DXT5 blocks as BC1, BC2 and BC3 instead of decoding them to BGRA8: a quarter to an eighth of the texture memory (`a10` decodes to about 212 MB today) and less work at load; `display.compressed_textures` (default on) turns it off, and `tools/mac_run.py` pins it off so runs still compare with GL's decoded textures; with it on, `a10`'s frames match the decoded ones to within 3 levels (the GPU's DXT decoder rounds differently) and `gpu_stats` is unchanged
- **metal**: answer visibility tests without waiting for the GPU in real time — reading a lens flare's answer committed the frame so far and waited for the GPU to finish it, so every frame the CPU and the GPU took turns (on Apple Vision Pro the CPU's 14-15 ms a frame was mostly that wait; now 3-6 ms); the answer is now the latest the GPU has written for the slot, a frame late while the GPU is behind, as desktop GL's persistent results do; the fixed timestep still waits, so runs stay repeatable
- **tvos**: render at 1080p by default

### refactor

- **ios**: name the app VISR in the internet play texts and keep the noted socket table to Apple builds — "VISR will turn on Internet Play" in `platform_link_consent`'s question and "the next time VISR starts" in `setting_changed_save`'s message, as the display name and the usage string do; `noted_sockets`, its lock and the remember and forget calls in `note_socket_port` and `halo_ws_closesocket` are under `HALO_IOS`, so Linux, Windows and Android take no extra lock per bind or close, and `xnet_start_internet_play` does nothing there
- **profiles**: no reserved `[quality]` section — profiles stay invisible to players, so a profile has one set of values and its scene classes; the Apple TV HD's cutscene entry records the decision to hold 30 at 720 once a frame-rate target exists, and `profile_probe.c` checks its measured values
- **models**: `halo_model_lod_set` replaces `display.model_lod`'s cached value while the game runs — the port's own cache in `source/models/models.c` was read once a launch, so the device benchmark could not try the other value between runs; not yet built or checked against check-metal
- **pacing**: leave the present call's pacer block out of a build without `HALO_FRAME_PACING` — the empty inlines already made it do nothing, but the compiler kept a zero for `frame_pacer_present`'s answer and assigned registers differently, so a Vision Pro build's `gpu_metal_present` differed from `main`'s by 62 lines of disassembly with the same effect
- **stereo**: keep a draw's own scissor when its viewport misses the inset's columns
- **theater**: draw a picture per view, with an optional HUD, and expose the screen's pose
- **metal**: decide a draw's attributes once for the pipeline and the bindings — `attribute_kinds` and `bind_attributes` each decided on their own whether an attribute is a constant, so a change to one could leave the specialized vertex function reading a stream as a constant, or the reverse, with no error (round 2 review, I1)
- **ios**: remove a half-written `join_link.new` at start, and have the bridging header include `host_join_link.h` instead of declaring `host_join_link_open` a second time
- **tools**: remove the icon concepts traced from the game's Master Chief art — delete `tools/visr_trace.py` and the `visor-traced`, `visor-helmet`, `visor-helmet-gold`, `visor-closeup` and `inside-*` concepts from `tools/visr_icon.py`, so the public repository carries only original icon art
- **renderer**: the GL backend is a table — `gpu.h`'s `GPU_FUNCTIONS` lists the 20 functions once, `gpu_gl.c` exports them as `gpu_backend_gl`, and the iOS host's `host_gpu_dispatch.c` calls through the table `gpu_initialize` selects; GL logs its capabilities; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: key the GL backend's ES paths on `GPU_GL_ES` — defined for the iOS guest today and for the host from step 4; nothing compiled changes
- **renderer**: the GL backend needs no XDK or guest header — `gpu.h` gains `GPU_STAGE_COUNT` and `GPU_ATTRIBUTE_COUNT`, `debug.gl_debug` reaches `gpu_initialize` as a flag, and `gpu_gl.c`/`gl_functions.c` declare the three platform functions they call; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: rename `d3d8_gl.c` to `d3d8_device.c` — the Direct3D device no longer touches GL; references follow, and the headers of `d3d8_device.c`, `xgpu.h` and `gpu_gl.c` say what each file is now
- **renderer**: no GL in the front end — the attribute seeding, the closing cache reset and the `debug.gl_debug` error checks move into `gpu_gl.c`, `debug.gpu_stats` reads the call count through `gpu_call_count_take`, and `xgpu.h` stops including `gl.h`; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: visibility tests and flush through `gpu.h` — the queries, the desktop result buffer and the ES counter ring move into `gpu_gl.c` behind `gpu_visibility_begin`/`end`/`result`, and the front end keeps slots, areas and the D3D result conversions; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: present and read back through `gpu_present` and `gpu_texture_read` — the letterbox blit, swap and stream-ring advance move into `gpu_gl.c`, screenshots and `texture_dump` read BGRA rows through the backend (so `texture_dump` works on ES for uncompressed textures), and the ES R/B swap and `GL_BGRA` aliases leave the front end; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: clear through `gpu_clear` — the front end turns a D3D clear into `gpu.h`'s `struct gpu_clear` and target-pixel rectangles, and `gpu_gl.c` issues the clear; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: draw through `gpu_draw` — the front end builds one `struct gpu_draw` per draw and the GL backend applies the whole packet; shaders, screenshots and every `gpu_stats` count but the GL call total are unchanged, and the total's per-frame average moves by −1 on the menu (frame 60) and by −2 and −1 on `a10` (frames 60 and 120)
- **renderer**: describe vertex input with `gpu.h`'s `struct gpu_vertex_stream` and `struct gpu_vertex_attribute` — the front end translates D3D vertex types into neutral formats and missing attributes into `GPU_STREAM_CONSTANT` ones, and `gpu_gl.c` applies each attribute where the front end did; the `gpu_stream` kinds become `GPU_STREAM_KIND_*`; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: describe texture stages with `gpu.h`'s `struct gpu_stage` — the front end fills texture, type and sampler state from D3D texture-stage state, and `gpu_gl.c` binds and configures through its own sampler cache; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: describe raster state with `gpu.h`'s neutral packet structs — the front end translates D3D render states into viewport, scissor, depth-stencil, blend and raster structs, and `gpu_gl.c` applies them through its cache; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: move the GL state cache into `gpu_gl.c` — the front end binds through temporary `gpu_gl_state_*` calls until the draw packet, and the backend's buffer and program code uses the cache directly; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: link programs and upload constants and uniforms in `gpu_gl.c` — locations and uploads come from `gpu_uniforms.h`; the front end drives a program through temporary `gpu_gl_program_*` calls until the draw packet; output and GL call counts unchanged
- **renderer**: generate `struct gpu_uniforms` from `gpu_uniforms.h` and convert the draw's uniforms into it — every field a vec4 array, scalars in x; `c` gets its own stage, since its values live in the constant store; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: keep the vertex constants in a fixed-width `struct gpu_constant_store` (`gpu.h`); output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: compile shaders through `gpu_shader_create`; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: stream vertices and indices through `gpu_stream_reserve` and `gpu_stream` — the stream and index buffers, the ES ring, the vertex array and their creation move into `gpu_gl.c`; Present advances them through `gpu_gl_stream_frame`; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: write the vertex mirror's segments through `gpu_buffer_create` and `gpu_buffer_write` — a `gpu_buffer` is the GL name; the ES unsynchronized write for first uploads moves into `gpu_gl.c` as `GPU_WRITE_UNUSED`; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: delete idle cached textures through `gpu_texture_destroy`; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: upload cached textures through `gpu_texture_create` and `gpu_texture_upload` — `xgpu_texture_get` returns a `gpu_texture` and a `GPU_TEXTURE_*` type; the ES swizzle and the compressed formats move into `gpu_gl.c`; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: build mip composites through `gpu_texture_create`, `gpu_texture_copy_level` and `gpu_texture_generate_mipmaps`; the framebuffer cache and the ES blit copy move into `gpu_gl.c`; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: add `gpu.h`'s texture functions and create render targets through `gpu_texture_create` — in the GL backend a `gpu_texture` is the GL name, with a record of its description; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: decode DXT textures when `gpu_capabilities.s3tc` is 0 instead of under `#ifdef HALO_ILP32` — the decoder now also compiles on desktop; output unchanged
- **renderer**: swizzle D3DCOLOR vertex streams when `gpu_capabilities.vertex_bgra` is 0 instead of under `#ifdef HALO_ILP32` — `stream_upload_swizzled` and `stream_has_colors` now also compile on desktop; output unchanged
- **renderer**: read `border_clamp`, `base_vertex` and `occlusion_mode` from `struct gpu_capabilities` instead of `#ifdef HALO_ILP32` — the index rebasing path now also compiles on desktop; output unchanged
- **renderer**: build the shader dialect from `struct gpu_capabilities` instead of `#ifdef HALO_ILP32` and `xgpu_capabilities`; output unchanged
- **renderer**: start the GPU backend with `gpu.h` and `gpu_initialize` — the context probe and setup move from `gl_initialize` into `gpu_gl.c`, which reports a fixed-width `struct gpu_capabilities`; output and GL calls unchanged
- **renderer**: flip the front face from the shader dialect's `clip_y_flip` instead of `#ifdef HALO_ILP32` — the winding change belongs with the vertex shader's y flip; output unchanged
- **renderer**: declare shader uniforms from one table, `gpu_uniforms.h` — `nv2a_uniform_declarations` emits each stage's declarations in their existing order, replacing `XGPU_PIXEL_UNIFORMS` and the vertex prologue's hand-written lines; ES 3.0 output byte-identical
- **renderer**: emit the pixel shader's dialect differences from `struct nv2a_dialect` — the version and precision lines, the sampler LOD bias, occlusion counting and the debug overrides no longer depend on `#ifdef HALO_ILP32` or read `port_config`; ES 3.0 output byte-identical
- **renderer**: emit the vertex shader's dialect differences from `struct nv2a_dialect` — the version line, precision, the clip-space capture and the y flip and z remap become run-time choices instead of `#ifdef HALO_ILP32`; ES 3.0 output byte-identical
- **renderer**: pass a shader dialect to the NV2A translators — `struct nv2a_dialect` describes the GLSL to emit, `gl_initialize` fills it from the context, and `nv2a_vertex_shader_translate`/`nv2a_pixel_shader_translate` take it; `debug.gpu_shader_replay_dialect` replays through desktop GLSL; output unchanged
- **tvos**: drop bundling the player's maps into the IPA

### build

- **third_party**: vendor FFmpeg n9.0.2's Bink demuxer and decoders under `port/third_party/ffmpeg-bink`
- **testflight**: add `--internal-only` distribution
- default iOS, tvOS and visionOS builds to `org.steverice.visr` — one App Store Connect record (universal purchase) needs the same bundle ID on every platform, so `tools/ios_build.py` no longer appends `.tvos` or `.visionos`; the Mac Catalyst build keeps `org.steverice.visr.mac`
- **render**: `tools/ios_build.py --render-validation on` builds the validator in — CMake's `HALO_RENDER_VALIDATION` (off by default) adds `gpu_validate.c` and wraps the chosen backend in `host_gpu_dispatch.c`; `configure.py --render-validation` defines it for the guest and adds the two imports the front end reports through; `debug.render_validation` is `off`, `log` (the default in such a build) or `strict`, and `debug.render_validation_allow` names reasons a strict run goes on past
- **ios**: the App Store archive carries the app's dSYM — the archive build writes it beside the product in the build folder, where Xcode's archive does not look, so it is copied into the archive's dSYMs folder before the export (same build, same UUID)
- **ios**: Release builds generate debug symbols, so the dSYM exists — CMake's Release flags have no `-g`, its Xcode project set `GCC_GENERATE_DEBUGGING_SYMBOLS = NO`, and the archive's dSYMs folder stayed empty (the second TestFlight upload warned again)
- **ios**: Release builds write a dSYM — the first TestFlight upload warned that the archive held no debug symbols, so App Store Connect could not symbolicate a tester's crash
- **tvos**: stamp tvOS builds, add `--register-device`, and run tvOS simulator apps from `mac_run.py`
- default the bundle IDs to `org.steverice.visr` — the iPhone and iPad app (`port/ios/CMakeLists.txt`, `tools/ios_build.py`), `org.steverice.visr.tvos`, `.visionos` and `.mac` for `--tvos`, `--visionos` and `--mac`, and `org.steverice.visr.macrunner` for the runner in `tools/mac_run.py`, whose xcodegen `bundleIdPrefix` follows; `port/ios/README.md` names the new default. The `org.haloce.*` defaults carried the mark under a domain we don't own. The maintainer's own builds already pass these IDs with `--bundle-id`, and containers are still found by the ID each app declares
- **ios**: name the app target, product and executable `VISR` — the CMake project and target, `CFBundleExecutable` in every `Info*.plist`, the visionOS entitlements files, and the tools that find the built app (`ios_build.py`, `ios_package.py`, `mac_run.py` and its tests) now use `VISR.app` and `VISR` instead of `HaloCE.app` and `HaloCE`
- **ios**: give the Catalyst build its own `Info-macOS.plist` and test the `OpenGLES.tbd` rewrite
- **ios**: build the iOS host for Mac Catalyst with `ios_build.py --mac`
- **visionos**: stamp a new `CFBundleVersion` on each visionOS build, so installing over the app replaces it and keeps its Documents (the imported game) instead of silently keeping the old binary

### revert

- **metal**: drop this branch's simulator build fix — `main` now carries the pacer agent's fix for the same `addPresentedHandler:` error, which the next commit merges
- **d3d8**: take the render-target cache back out of `render-perf` until it can be keyed for the stereo branches — `3193194d` is correct on this branch, but the branches this one merges into (`phase3-stereo`, `headset-terminal`, `metal-cpu-stereo`, `visor-interior`, `controller-aim`) free eye and HUD targets in `stereo_targets_release` (dangling remembered pointers) and choose a target by stereo layer, which the key lacks, so one eye's lookup would return another's; and `metal-render-options` changes the shadow-map scale at runtime with the same surfaces, which the cache would keep returning at the old scale; port it on the stereo line instead, keyed on the layer and the shadow scale and cleared on release (Fable review)
- perf(msl): read vertex attributes a word at a time where they are aligned — it made the GPU slower, not faster
- **ai**: hold back the exact `actor_move_test_avoidance_vector` from Jonas's fork

### test

- **benchmark**: `b30-beach`'s fight checks are those of a replay whose units' mouths follow their speech — the take was recorded while the Apple TV's mixer moved the mouths, so its replay leaves the take's seeds at tick 1500 now rather than 1471 and kills one more enemy by tick 1800; the actors and the dead at ticks 1800 to 2400 are the new replay's, the same in three runs, and the positions are unchanged
- **benchmark**: `b30-beach`'s points and fight are those of a replay with no sound — the expected positions at ticks 2100 and 2400 and the actors and the dead at ticks 1800 to 2400, from the replay that is the same in every run
- **replay**: `tools/route_check.py` checks the fight as well as the route — a `state SEGMENT TICK alive=1 actors=N dead=N` line in a `.checks` file compares player 1's body, the AI's actors and the dead objects at a tick with the replay's route log, and `tools/test_route_check.py` covers a dead player and a different count of the dead
- **ios**: count a join code's lookup among the places internet play may begin signalling — `look_up_code` in `p2p.c` (the join code typed under Direct Link, from `main`) begins signalling and STUN as a join does, so `tools/ios_test.py`'s scan now expects six `p2p_signal_start` calls and three STUN starts, with `look_up_code` checked beside `update_joining` and `update_hosting`
- **ios**: map downloads stay off by default on Apple builds — `tools/ios_test.py`'s scan of `port_config.c` fails if `DEFAULT_MAP_DOWNLOAD` is not false under `HALO_IOS` or `network.map_download` stops using it
- **ios**: map sharing may begin signalling inside a joined game — network version 27 has a machine in an internet game ask that game's other players for a map and answer them, so `p2p.c` calls `p2p_signal_start` in `update_answering` and `update_reaching` as well; `tools/ios_test.py`'s scan, which holds that nothing reaches a server before the player hosts, joins or browses, now allows those two and checks that each is behind `joined_host`, so only after a join the player agreed to
- **build**: match the explicit `HALO_BENCHMARK_UPLOAD` default
- **network**: the scripted player of `debug.network_test_players` takes its controller ahead of a connected gamepad — the Mac runner's touch controls were `gamepad 2` and kept the second player standing still
- **network**: `debug.network_test_players` gives an automated test host more local players for split screen — `network_test.c` adds the players of controllers 2 to 4 a second apart, and `xinput_sdl.c` plays those controllers with the `debug.test_input` script when no gamepad is connected to them
- **build**: match the explicit `HALO_BENCHMARK_UPLOAD` default
- **replay**: `tools/route_check.py` replays a recorded route and checks where the player stands — `tools/route_checks/a10-bench.checks` names `a10-bench`'s positions (in the cryo tube at tick 180, out of the bay at 1080, at the bridge at 2880), and the tool runs the replay on the native Mac runner either with `debug.input_replay` or, with `--suite`, through the benchmark suite the Apple TV and the simulators use, which is the path that left the player in the tube; `tools/test_route_check.py`, run by `tools/ios_test.py`, checks the verdicts (a player stuck in the tube fails) and that `benchmark_scenario.c` sets a scene's difficulty with `main_set_difficulty` and not `game_difficulty_level_set`, which the map load overwrites
- **network**: `tools/ios_test.py` checks the away notice — `port/ios/tests/away_probe.c` checks the packet (60 seconds, more than 255, the clamp to 1 and 65535, "back", a packet too short) and the wait across the clock's wrap (`p2p_away.h`), and the script checks the call sites the probe cannot see: the tunnel, the game's server and connections wait for an away peer, the app asks for background time, and the game's loop shows the notices; the seven builds and `ios_test.py` pass
- **render**: the validation and replay probes' mock backends have the five new `gpu.h` operations — `gpu_validate_probe.c` and `gpu_replay_probe.c` fill a `struct gpu_backend` from `GPU_FUNCTIONS`, so `tools/ios_test.py` stopped compiling them once `upscale`, `anti_alias_prepare`, `anti_alias`, `frame_context` and `compiles_outstanding` joined it; both probes pass again
- **render**: `tools/test_render_replay.py` runs as a script from `tools/ios_test.py` — the job hosts' Python has no pytest
- **network**: a join code typed any way finds its host's record, checked through the function the ENTER CODE screen uses — the text a player types becomes a code's topic and key in one place, `p2p_code_lookup` in `p2p_code.c`, which `p2p_join_code` (ENTER CODE) and `p2p_invite_received` (a code handed over as a link) both call, and the record is made and opened there too (`p2p_code_record_make`, `p2p_code_record_open`); `port/ios/tests/join_code_probe.c` now builds with `p2p_crypto.c` and Monocypher, makes a host's record from a code and finds and opens it from seven typings of that code (with and without its dash, either case, spaces, a trailing newline), and checks that another code's key and an altered record open nothing; a code typed without its dash was reported as refused by a tester, which this does not reproduce: the refusals in that Apple TV's log came before it had any keyboard, so they were empty entries
- **network**: the join bot survives the main menu starting again — `network_test.c`'s `_network_test_join` marked its player as joined to multiplayer once, before its first search; when `main_menu_initialize` ran again a few seconds later it disposed that client and cleared the joined players, the invite's screens made a new client, and the bot joined the game with no player to add, so the host dropped the machine 15 seconds later (`client machine 1 timed out`) and a pair test failed by a race at startup; the bot now starts its search over when its client is gone and marks its player again as it joins, as the menus' own joins do (`multiplayer_player` before every `ui_widget_port_join`)
- **mac_run**: let a GL run and a Metal run differ in the vertex BGRA and base vertex capabilities — `compare --across-backends` required identical `GPU capabilities` lines and identical `debug.gpu_stats` upload totals, which holds only while the Metal backend copies what Apple's OpenGL ES reports
- **network**: record the wire format at network version 26 — the imported `port/linux/network_format.txt` was recorded upstream at version 27, with the map sharing, voice chat and vote messages this port has not imported
- **network**: use a portable loopback address in the bot message probe
- **network**: validate native WebRTC against Chromium — import OpenCE's network version 26 browser round-trip and packet-loss harness, using `arc4random_buf` on Apple hosts and keeping `getrandom` on Linux; Chromium returns all 320 messages without loss and keeps the channel open with ten percent UDP loss
- **input**: let `debug.test_input`'s `zoom:` pattern click out of the zoom after the flashlight
- **render**: `ios_test.py` checks that the in-game, pregame and stereo frames call the render's random seed functions
- **input**: let `debug.test_input`'s `zoom:` pattern press the flashlight after zooming
- **input**: add a `zoom:` scripted pattern to `debug.test_input` that swaps weapons and zooms on cue
- **linux**: skip the XDK header compile and the weak reference link check where they cannot run — the header compile needs a 32-bit Linux C library's headers, which macOS hosts lack ('time.h' file not found), and the link check reads ELF objects, which clang only makes on Linux; both failed on every Mac, at main too
- **textures**: turn the damage tests off in `texture_policy_probe`'s hand-built table
- **mac_run**: drive the Xcode `osascript` failure paths end to end — the tests so far checked only the scripts' text and order
- **shaders**: add fixture keys for the LSB mux, the GL dot mapping, a bump stage's named input, the final combiner's V1R0 sum, and alpha kill and color sign on a PASSTHRU stage — the paths the xemu-confirmed combiner fixes changed, which no recorded scene reaches
- **tools**: use the VISR runner's bundle ID in the `find_container` tests — `org.steverice.visr.macrunner` in place of `org.steverice.haloce.macrunner`
- **tools**: `mac_run.py run --simulator UDID` runs a simulator build with the runner's settings and result folders, so the visionOS build's runs compare with the Mac's; `--maps` seeds a new container with imported maps
- **tools**: compare GL against Metal — `mac_run.py compare --across-backends` matches shader names across `.glsl`/`.metal`, shader inputs byte for byte and the capability lines, ignoring GL calls; `--channel-tolerance` and `--fraction` set a pixel threshold; `run --metal-validation`/`--metal-shader-validation` write Metal's validation into the runner's scheme, and `--time-limit` lets a validated run finish
- **ios**: pin `gpu.h`'s struct layouts across the 64-bit host and the `arm64_32` guest — a host program prints `_Static_assert`s that the guest compiler must accept

### ci

- **ios**: run the iOS workflow on pushes and pull requests to `main` instead of `ios-port` — `main` is the public repository's default branch
- **ios**: name the build artifacts `VISR` — the IPAs and simulator archives are `VISR-*` and the uploaded artifacts `visr-*`, from the renamed `VISR.app`

### chore

- **ios**: limit `UIRequiresFullScreen` to the iPad (`UIRequiresFullScreen~ipad`), as in every run that got an external display scene — the iOS 27 probe connected its scene only after registering the scene accessory and making this change together, and the iOS 26 run with the SDL method replacement used the same key; the key concerns iPad multitasking, so the iPad keeps it unchanged and an iPhone loses nothing. The external display's scene configuration is indented with tabs like the rest of `Info.plist`
- **profiles**: turn FXAA on in `profiles/iphone-17-pro.toml` — the iPhone 17 Pro held 120 fps in every quick-suite scene with `display.anti_aliasing = "fxaa"` (reports `47PS5LC2`, against `NMYSEQ6R` with it off), GPU mean 5.37 ms against 5.28 on the b30 beach and 5.45 against 5.24 on the c40 bridge, and 5.36 against 2.62 in the menu; the class profile and other devices are unchanged, and `profile_probe.c` expects the new value
- **profiles**: record the iPhone 17 Pro evidence for the Metal default in `profiles/iphone-ipad.toml` — Metal ran the quick suite at 119.9/119.9/120.0 fps (reports `C23KM6WA`) against 60.0/60.0/59.9 fps on OpenGL ES (`LBAE78A5`), the GL path's 60 Hz cap
- **profiles**: make Metal the renderer on iPhone and iPad, and state it for the Vision Pro — `profiles/iphone-ipad.toml` set no `display.renderer`, so iPhone and iPad ran the OpenGL ES default and none of the Metal backend's performance work (shadow-map and decal batching, pipeline caching, frame pacing)
- **audio**: log once when a looping sound buffer first plays — the loading screen's tone can't be seen in a runner's screenshots, so its log line shows the buffers decoded and joined the mix
- **profiles**: menu and cutscenes at the display's own height on the Apple TV 4K (3rd generation) — measured by a tester's Full report (`NHC5Z2D5`): menu GPU 9.3 ms at 2160, a10's opening 59.8 uncapped at 2160; cutscenes capped at 30; play stays at 1080 since 2160 throttled the device
- **profiles**: the Apple TV 4K (3rd generation) plays at 1080 with 16x filtering, measured — a tester's benchmark report (H7C78EJ8) held 59.9 frames a second on b30's beach with every frame on time (GPU 95th percentile 15.0 ms), 59.4 in the menu and 57.4 in a10's opening; three easy scenes, so the rest stays assumed
- **test**: `debug.test_win_level` wins the level after a set time — a run nobody plays can go through the end of a level and the load of the next, the path that ended the game before `f513a24b7`
- **profiles**: the Apple TV HD's menu at 720 and its cutscenes at 1152x648 — decided after the first played sessions: the 1080 menu ran 53.5 frames a second while being moved through, and 720 cutscenes missed 30 by one to three frames in their heaviest windows; the cutscene height is assumed until measured, and the high-res HUD stays on by decision
- **profiles**: the Apple TV 4K (3rd generation) estimate keeps its menu at the play height — a 2160 menu was the one value in the two estimated profiles that a first run could not fall back from, and nothing is known yet about that model's memory in the menus
- **metal**: `HALO_METAL_PIXEL_MATH=fast` on GPUs without relaxed math — a launch switch to measure what safe pixel math costs an A8
- **build**: give each host-profile scene a time limit that follows its length — a scene that hangs stopped the recording for half an hour
- **stereo**: drop the HUD messages' corner log and wrap a long comment
- **config**: register `debug.test_extra_scene`
- **docs**: remove the game screenshots `docs/ios/menu.png` and `docs/ios/import.jpg` — they show Halo's menu art, came from `NicholasDominici/halo-ce-ios`, and nothing in the tree links them anymore

### merge

- `external-display-implementation` into `main` — the external display code builds with SDKs before iOS 27, as the CI's iOS 26 SDK did not compile `host_external.m`; the iOS, Mac and simulator builds pass with the iOS 27 SDK. No conflicts
- `external-display-implementation` into `main` — an iPhone or iPad cabled to a display plays on it: the game moves to the display at the mode the device profile picks (`display.output_height`, 1080 lines for the iPhone and iPad class), and the device shows a companion of the HUD, menus and full-screen effects behind the touch controls. iOS 27 registers an external scene accessory, iOS 16 to 26 answers SDL's scene configuration method for the external role, OpenGL ES and AirPlay leave the system mirroring, unplugging during play returns to the device and pauses, `debug.external_mode` forces a mode or a list cycled with Back and the D-pad, profiles may hold `[external]` sections (pack compatibility `profiles-2`, published for testers as `docs/site/packs/tester/profiles-2.json`), and benchmark reports gain an `output` block. Codex's implementation (`83f397dab`) with review fixes for reconnects, early disconnects, refused switches and the Mac window on a second monitor, a guard on the SDL method replacement, `UIRequiresFullScreen` limited to the iPad, and a mode negotiation that holds the chosen mode when the display changes its own mode at connect. On an iPhone 17 Pro with a 3840x2160 display through an HDMI EDID spoofer, the menu, a10 and b30 ran on the display at 1920x1080 and 60 Hz with the HUD, reticle and motion tracker on the device's companion, and a forced 1280x720 took; unplugging, controllers, AirPlay and iPad were not tried. All seven builds, the tester builds, `tools/ios_test.py` and `check-metal` (the same result as `main` on the same host, with the companion captures matching) pass. No conflicts
- `main` into `external-display-implementation` — brings in SDL built without its camera subsystem. No conflicts
- `sdl-no-camera` into `main` — SDL built without its camera subsystem, fixing ITMS-90683. No conflicts
- `main` into `external-display-implementation` — brings in tester profile packs 2 and 3 (`docs/site/packs/tester/profiles.json`). No conflicts

- `packs-check-every-launch` into `main` — the data-pack updater checks its profile and recording feeds on every launch instead of at most once a day: one background request with a 10 second timeout and no retry, `If-None-Match` from the saved `ETag` so an unchanged feed answers 304, and any failure logs one line and keeps the current snapshot. No conflicts
- `main` into `external-display-implementation` — brings in the late frame start and late look, the Bink movies, the network extras, the replay fixes and the level-start loading screen. Five conflicts, all from both sides adding beside each other: the backend table in `gpu.h`, `guest_gpu.c`, `guest_host.h` and `host_imports.list` keep the companion entries (`companion_size`, `companion_set`) and `frame_start`, and `D3DDevice_Present` clears a flat level start to black before re-issuing the companion's draws
- `docs-ffmpeg-notice` into `main` — `NOTICE.md` and the bundled `port/ios/THIRD_PARTY.md` say where the complete public source of the app, FFmpeg code included, is and give the command that rebuilds the app with a modified FFmpeg, as the LGPL asks of a statically linked build. No conflicts
- `level-start-hold` into `main` — the loading screen stays up over a level's first ticks, through its first six ticks (`halo_loading_screen_tail`), which start an opening cutscene and put its fade from black in force, so no frame shows the player's start first, and its progress eases toward its target and never moves back (`halo_loading_screen_progress`), with the time share counted from the loaded level; a flat screen presents black for a campaign level's frames before its first ticks (`halo_level_start_hold_flat`), and the stereo eyes are held black the same way (`halo_stereo_level_start_hold`). Two conflicts, both from `halo_stereo_head_tracked_view` landing in `main` beside the branch's new functions in `stereo.c` and their declarations in `halo_stereo.h`; both sides are kept
- `fix-take-drift` into `main` — a replay of a take is the same every time and follows the take as played: each tick takes the take's random seeds before it runs, and a tick that follows no tick starts the local sequence from the global one; a replay plays no sound and a unit speaks a scripted sound whether or not the mixer started it; scripts read `camera_time` in ticks rather than in the frames the director drew; a checkpoint, a BSP switch, a skipped cinematic, the revert after a death and the next map come between the same two ticks whatever the frame rate; the scripts' action tests read the tick's action; and while a take records or replays, a unit's mouth moves once a tick from its speech (`unit_mouth_aperture_from_speech`) rather than from the mixer. The per-tick `camera_time` and the checkpoint, BSP and revert waits apply to all play, not only to takes, and a co-op game over the network still plays through them. It also bundles `b30-beach`, a hand-played heroic beach fight from an Apple TV, with its `.checks`, and `tools/route_check.py` checks the fight (`state` lines: alive, actors, dead) as well as the route. No conflicts
- `port-late-frame` into `main` — `display.late_start` starts a frame's work as late as it can and still make the next refresh, so the controller is read later (on in the Mac app, off on iPhone, iPad and Apple TV, never in a Vision Pro build, whose frames the Compositor paces; only while a frame's share is one refresh; F8 in the Mac app), and `display.late_look` re-reads the look stick just before the first-person camera is built and turns the picture, not the aim, by what the stick did since (off by default everywhere; F9). No image is warped. Ported from `frame-presentation` without its headset flare-counting changes; the render validator, the render capture and their probes pass the new `frame_start` through. One conflict, in `host_sdl.c`'s event poll, kept the share sheet's held-back events and added the F8 toggle after them. On the branch all seven builds, the tester build, a render-validation Mac build, `tools/ios_test.py` and `check-metal` pass, and the frame pacer logs its late starts on a10 on the native Mac runner (up to 112 of 600 frames started late, none shown late)
- `port-net-extras` into `main` — Apple devices find system link games with Bonjour (`host_bonjour.m`, `host_lan.c`, `posix_lan_*`, the Wi-Fi-first interface choice), Apple builds ask before turning internet play on, with the approved disclosure that the matchmaking servers are other people's and see the player's IP address (`HALO_INTERNET_DISCLOSURE`; `network.online_ask` defaults to false), the host's invite goes out through the share sheet, a taken invite opens Direct Link, a failed join says which step failed, an empty server browser says when no matchmaking server answers or the games are on other versions (`p2p_servers_status`), notices for the player are drawn on Apple builds, alerts and sheets keep the key window through theater mode, a link opened in a game waits for it to end, and `tools/p2p_broker_probe.py` checks the brokers from a computer. Ported from `multiplayer-session-3`; its host grace period is left out for `main`'s `network.background_grace`, and with it the Vision Pro window close that ended a hosted game. Map downloads stay off by default on Apple builds. One conflict, the CMake source list, kept both `host_bink.c` and the Bonjour and share sources. On the branch all seven builds, the tester build, `tools/ios_test.py`, the network tests in `tools/` and `check-metal` pass
- `port-bink` into `main` — the intro and credits movies play in the Apple apps through the vendored FFmpeg Bink decoders (`port/third_party/ffmpeg-bink`, LGPL 2.1 or later, its license bundled as `Licenses/FFmpeg-LGPL-2.1.txt` and described in `NOTICE.md`), behind the `HALO_BINK` build switch (on by default, `tools/ios_build.py --bink off` builds an app with no FFmpeg code); the disc import copies `bink/intro*.bik` and `bink/credits*.bik` beside `maps`, on the Apple TV's upload too, where `xiso_plan` now keeps the movies' bytes; in a head-tracked view a movie's panel is drawn at the display's rate. The branch's commits are ported from `bink-videos` and `aim-round-5`'s `d03b22888`; the headset's room-fixed movie panel (`1e66e6f97`) stays on the headset line. All seven builds, the tester build, three `--bink off` builds, `tools/ios_test.py` and `check-metal` pass, and the intro decodes and plays, 481 frames, on the native Mac runner under GL and Metal

- bring `main` into `texture-upscale` — the VISR rename, the OpenCE sync, join links, Mac Catalyst and the settings menu
- `render-perf` into `main` — its features came in with `render-perf-warm-wip`; what is left is the render-target cache `3193194d` and its revert `f5787aea`, so this merge changes no files and closes the branch
- `render-perf-warm-wip` into `main` — Metal vertex functions specialized for each pipeline's attribute formats, word-at-a-time front-cache hashing, shader timing and pipeline-change counts under `debug.gl_debug`, and each map's shader list compiled in parallel while it loads (`halo_shader_list_warm`, `port/shader-lists/`), with misses recorded for `tools/shader_lists.py`
- `main` into `render-perf` — `main` is the renamed `upstream-merge`, now with the in-code VISR rename (`VISR.app`, the `VISR` target and executable); the settings table keeps both `debug.metal_specialize` and `main`'s `debug.menu_open`
- `main` into `publish-prep` — brings join links and `9d4405e1`, which makes `visor-helmet-gold` the app icon; `tools/visr_icon.py` gets back that concept and what it draws from (`VISOR_PATH`, `HELMET_PATH`, `BROW_RIMS`, the gold glass, the helmet shell and the brow rims), while `tools/visr_trace.py` and the `visor-traced`, `visor-helmet`, `visor-closeup` and `inside-*` concepts stay removed as `d9d4eed7` left them
- `join-links` into `main` — the app opens `halo://join` invite links on iPhone, iPad, Apple Vision Pro, Apple TV and the Mac, and joins them through internet play when `network.online` is on; the Mac app now dispatches the Apple Events that carry a link opened while it runs
- `main` into `join-links` — `main` is the renamed `upstream-merge`, now with the in-code VISR rename (`VISR.app`, the `VISR` target and executable)
- `main` into `publish-prep` — `main` is the renamed `upstream-merge`, now with the in-code VISR rename (`VISR.app`, the `VISR` target and executable)
- `visr-rebrand` into `upstream-merge` — the app is named VISR with the original reticle icon on iOS, visionOS, tvOS and Mac Catalyst
- `upstream-merge` at `935e1d1c` into `visr-rebrand` — the native Mac runner, the halo-og Metal work, the CPU draw-path performance work and the 151-commit upstream sync, so the VISR rename merges into `upstream-merge` cleanly
- `upstream-merge` at `e2fc1a7f` into `upstream-sync-2026-10-06` — the CPU draw-path performance work (`2390f213` to `e2fc1a7f`), so the sync branch merges into `upstream-merge` cleanly
- halo-ce-universal (OpenCE) main at `b115a412` — online co-op and split screen, network version 18, the audio fixes, the vertex and texture fixes, the performance work with many enemies, and the map and network hardening
- `multiplayer`'s merge of halo-ce-universal at `193cbf59` (`984da56a`) — the PC menus, the server browser, split-screen co-op and the quit-slot fixes, with that merge's conflict resolutions
- halo-ce-universal main at 193cbf59 — network version 11, the PC menus, the server browser, split-screen co-op and the quit-slot fixes
- `upstream-merge` into `metal-gpu-cost` — `gpu_metal_shader_create` keeps `upstream-merge`'s saved pixel source with this branch's per-stage options, and `pixel_function`'s `EXACT_BORDERS` recompile now uses `pixel_compile_options`, so exact-border variants compile relaxed like the plain ones (review I1)
- `metal-cpu` — the runner scheme no longer turns on Metal API validation, and the Metal backend skips redundant encoder calls
- `console-tag-fix` — a console line naming a tag the scenario doesn't reference no longer halts the game
- `decomp-sync` — six reconciled matches from Jonas's fork, with `actor_move_test_avoidance_vector` held back
- halo-ce-universal's main (111 commits) into the iOS and visionOS port — the high-res HUD, text and titles, upstream's game and network fixes, and the removal of the matching-era tooling and research

### style

- **render**: the FXAA chord's label on the HUD's message line in a game — `hud_print_message`, where "Checkpoint... done" shows, rather than the debug text, which sat over the ammo counter at the top left; the menus keep the debug text
